§ 10.01 NAME.
   The name of this code is the “Lawrence County Code,” “this code,” or “this
county code.”
(Prior Code, § 1-1-1)
§ 10.02 RULES OF INTERPRETATION, CONSTRUCTION.
   (A)   The construction of ordinances contained in this county code shall be
by the following rules, unless such a construction would be obviously against
the intent of the Board of Commissioners or against the context of the same
ordinance.
   (B)   Generally, words and phrases shall be understood in their plain,
ordinary, and usual sense. But technical words and phrases having a peculiar
and appropriate meaning in law shall be understood according to their technical
import.
   (C)   Words importing a joint authority to three or more persons shall be
construed as authority to a majority of such persons, unless otherwise declared
in the ordinance giving such authority.
   (D)   Words in the singular shall include the plural, and likewise words in
the plural shall include the singular.
   (E)   Each section, chapter, subchapter, and division of this code is
declared to be separable. Further, should any section be declared to be invalid
by any court of law, this shall nevertheless not be construed by any such court
to adversely affect the remaining portion of this code in any manner.
   (F)   The male gender shall include the female gender and the neuter; the
female shall include the male gender and the neuter; the neuter gender shall
include both the male and female genders.
(Prior Code, § 1-3-1)
§ 10.03 DEFINITIONS.
   (A)   (1)   The masculine shall include both the feminine and neuter.
      (2)   Words in the singular shall include the plural, and words in the
plural shall include words in the singular.
      (3)   When a general term follows a specific enumeration of terms, the
general term is not limited to the class so specifically enumerated, unless
such intention is clear.
      (4)   Tensed words used in the past and present tenses include the future
tense by implication, unless otherwise stated.
      (5)   The term ELECTED COUNTY OFFICIAL shall include the County
Commissioners, County Council, County Auditor, Recorder, Treasurer, Assessor,
Surveyor, and Clerk. Should their titles be later changed, this code shall
continue to apply to them unless this code is amended. This code does not apply
to judicial officers, the Coroner, nor the Sheriff, except as stated otherwise.
(Prior Code, § 1-3-2)
   (B)   For the purpose of this code, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      AUDITOR. The Auditor of the county.
      COUNTY. Lawrence County, Indiana.
      COUNTY EXECUTIVE. The Board of Commissioners of the county, as defined by
I.C. 36-2-2-2.
      COUNTY FISCAL BODY. The seven-member County Council, as defined by I.C.
36-2-3-2. It shall act in the name of the County Council.
      COUNTY LEGISLATIVE BODY. The Board of Commissioners of the county, as
defined by I.C. 36-1-2-9.
      I.C. The Burns Indiana Statues Annotated, Code Edition.
      MAY. Permissive and no obligation is created thereby.
      MONTH. A calendar month.
(I.C. 1-1-4-1, I.C. 1-1-4-5)
      PERSON. A natural person, partnership, a corporate body, or any other
persons, whether or not they are incorporated. Whenever used in any clause
which prescribes a penalty, the word PERSON, as applied to any unincorporated
entity, shall mean the partners or members thereof, and as applied to
corporations, shall mean the officers thereof.
      PERSONAL PROPERTY. Every kind and type of property except real property.
      PROPERTY. Kinds and types of property, including both real and personal
property.
      REAL PROPERTY. Land, tenements, and hereditaments, and all chattels real.
      SHALL. Mandatory.
      STATE. The State of Indiana.
      STREETS, ROADS, OR HIGHWAYS. Except as provided in the traffic code, this
term means the entire width between property lines of every way or place when
any part thereof is open to the use of the public.
      YEAR. A calendar year, unless otherwise stated.
(I.C. 1-1-4-1, I.C. 1-1-4-5)
(Prior Code, § 1-6-1)
§ 10.04 DISCOVERY OF ERRORS, OMISSIONS.
   If a manifest error is discovered, consisting of the misspelling of words,
the omission of any word or words necessary to express the intention of
provisions affected, the use of a word or words to which no meaning can be
attached, or the use of a word or words when another word was clearly intended
to express intention, the spelling shall be corrected and the word supplied,
omitted, or substituted the will conform with the manifest intention, and the
provisions shall have the same effect as those as originally published. No
alteration shall be made or permitted if any question exists regarding the
nature or extent of the error.
(Prior Code, § 1-3-2)
§ 10.05 REPEAL OF ORDINANCES.
   Whenever an ordinance is repealed which repealed a former ordinance, the
original ordinance shall not be revived unless specifically stated otherwise.
(Prior Code, § 1-3-2)
§ 10.06 CONFLICTING PROVISIONS.
   (A)   If there are different provisions of this code which conflict with
each other, then the provision with the latest passage date is shall prevail.
   (B)   However, if there is no latest passage date, then the conflict shall
be construed so as to be consistent with the meaning or legal effect of the
subject matter taken as a whole.
(Prior Code, § 1-3-2)
§ 10.07 INVALIDITY.
   If any provision of this code, or the application to any person or
circumstance is held invalid, such invalidity shall not affect other provisions
or applications of this act, which can be given effect without the invalid
provision or application, and to this end the provisions of this code are
declared to be severable.
(Prior Code, § 1-3-2)
§ 10.08 PROVISIONS ON A COUNTY-WIDE BASIS.
   (A)   This code contains all of the provisions on a county-wide basis which
are of a general nature pertaining to the subjects herein enumerated and
embraced.
   (B)   All prior county ordinances which pertain to these subjects treated
hereunder are hereby deemed repealed from and after the effective date of this
code.
(Prior Code, § 1-3-2)
§ 10.09 TEMPORARY, SPECIAL NATURE.
   All ordinances of a temporary or special nature and all other ordinances
pertaining to subjects not embraced in this code shall remain in full force and
effect unless herein expressly repealed or repealed by implication.
(Prior Code, § 1-3-2)
§ 10.10 NUMBERING OF CHAPTERS, SUBCHAPTERS, SECTIONS, DIVISIONS.
   Any and all chapters, subchapters, sections, and divisions which are
contained in or added to this code by ordinances passed subsequent to its
initial publication may be numbered in accordance with the numbering system of
this code and printed for inclusion herein. Any chapter, subchapter, section,
or division which might be repealed by subsequent ordinances may be excluded
from this code by omission from reprinted pages. All subsequent ordinances as
so printed or omitted shall be prima facie evidence of the subsequent
ordinances until the County Commissioners should adopt a new county code of
ordinances, recodifying those then in existence.
(Prior Code, § 1-3-2)
§ 10.11 AVAILABILITY OF CODE TO PUBLIC.
   Two copies of the county code are on file in the office of the County
Auditor (who is the Clerk for the Board of Commissioners of the county), Main
Floor, Courthouse, Public Square, Bedford, Indiana, for public inspection as
required by I.C. 36-1-5-4. These copies of the code may be inspected during the
normal business hours which are maintained by the Auditor’s office.
(Prior Code, § 1-5-1)
§ 10.99 GENERAL PENALTY.
   Any person who violates any provision of this code for which another penalty
is not specifically provided, shall not be fined more than $2,500.
(I.C. 36-1-3-8(10)) (Prior Code, § 1-4-1)
CHAPTER 11: COUNTY STANDARDS
Section
   
11.01   Home rule
   
11.02   Boundaries
§ 11.01 HOME RULE.
   (A)   I.C. 36-1-3-1 et seq. recognizes the validity of “Home Rule” and
enacts it throughout the state. The county code is based upon the powers
extended by such statues which grant counties and other units of government
“all the powers that they need for the effective operation of government as to
local affairs” (I.C. 36-1-3-2). There is a presumption in favor of the
existence of these powers as recognized by statute and “any doubt as to the
existence of a power of a unit shall be resolved in favor of its existence.”
(I.C. 36-1-3-3) (Prior Code, § 1-7-1)
   (B)   The County Commissioners have:
      (1)   All powers granted to it by statute; and
      (2)   All other powers necessary or desirable in the conduct of its
affairs, even though not granted by statute.
(I.C. 36-1-3-4) (Prior Code, § 1-7-2)
   (C)   A unit may exercise any power it has to the extent of that power:
      (1)   It is not expressly denied by the State Constitution or by statute;
and
      (2)   It is not expressly granted to another entity.
(I.C. 36-1-3-5) (Prior Code, § 1-7-3)
§ 11.02 BOUNDARIES.
   (A)   The boundaries of the county are those which existed in August 31,
1981, and shall remain unchanged until changed in the manner provided for in
I.C. 36-2-1-2.
(I.C. 36-2-1-1)
   (B)   For the map of the county, see the official map kept in the Auditor’s
office showing the boundaries of the county.
(Prior Code, § 1-9-1)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 30.01 COUNTY GOVERNMENT, GENERALLY.
   (A)   The county’s government shall consist of four branches:
      (1)   The county executive;
      (2)   The county legislative;
      (3)   The county fiscal body; and
      (4)   Statutory boards and commissions.
   (B)   The County Council is, by statute, both the county legislative body
(see I.C. 36-1-5-2) and the County Executive Body (see I.C. 36-2-2-2).
   (C)   The County Council is the county fiscal body (see I.C. 36-2-3-2).
There might be some confusion as to which of these two bodies is the county
legislative body. On one hand, I.C. 36-1-2-9 states that the Commissioners
shall be the legislative body, while I.C. 36-2-3.5-2 states that the County
Council shall be the legislative body.
   (D)   (1)   The statute I.C. 36-2-3.5-2 states that the County Council can
be the county legislative body only if both the county executive and the county
fiscal bodies adopt identical ordinances providing for the county to be
governed by that chapter (see I.C. 36-2-3.5-1 et seq.) does not apply. Because
the Commissioners and Council have never adopted such identical ordinances (as
of the date of this passage), then that statute (I.C. 36-2-3.5-1 et seq.) does
not apply.
      (2)   Thus EXECUTIVE BRANCH means the Board of Commissioners.
(Prior Code, § 2-1-1)
§ 30.02 VOTING PRECINCTS.
   (A)   Board of Commissioners.
      (1)   The Board of Commissioners hereby establishes the following
districts:
         (a)   County Commissioner District 1 shall be comprised of: Indian
Creek North, Indian Creek South, Perry, Marshall 1, 2, and 3, and Shawswick 1A,
5B, 7A, 7B, 10, 12, 13, 15A, and 16;
         (b)   County Commissioner District 2 shall be comprised of: Bono,
Marion 1, 2, 3, 4, 5A, 5B, 5C, and 6, and Spice Valley North and Spice Valley
South; and
         (c)   County Commissioner District 3 shall be comprised of: Pleasant
Run, Guthrie, Shawswick 1B, 2, 3, 4, 5A, 6, 8, 9, 11, 14A, 14B, and 15B.
      (2)   This division (A) shall not be construed as an attempt to vacate
any duly elected or appointed office holder or precinct officer of his or her
office during his or her present term thereof.
   (B)   County Council.
      (1)   The County Council hereby establishes the following districts:
         (a)   County Council District 1 shall be comprised of: Indian Creek
North, Indian Creek South, Spice Valley North, Spice Valley South, and Marion
1, 2, 3, 4, 5A, and 5B;
         (b)   County Council District 2 shall be comprised of: Marion 5C and
6, Bono, Guthrie, Pleasant Run, and Shawswick 3, 11, 14B, 15A, and 15B;
         (c)   County Council District 3 shall be comprised of: Marshall 1, 2,
3, Perry, Shawswick 2, 12, 13, and 14A; and
         (d)   County Council District 4 shall be comprised of: Shawswick 1A,
1B, 4, 5A, 5B, 6, 7A, 7B, 8, 9, 10, and 16.
      (2)   This division (B) shall not be construed as an attempt to vacate
any duly elected or appointed office holder or precinct officer of his or her
office during his or her present term thereof.
(Ord. 2021-13, passed 12-7-2021; Ord. 2021-14, passed 12-7-2021)
§ 30.03 ABSENTEE VOTING.
   (A)   The County Election Board hereby authorizes the Circuit Court Clerk to
use an electronic voting system for voting by absentee ballot in the office of
the Circuit Court Clerk pursuant to I.C. 3-11-10-26.2.
   (B)   The County Election Board adopts the procedures attached to the
resolution codified within this section marked as Exhibit A and incorporated
herein by reference, which are substantially similar to the procedures for
casting a ballot in the office of the Clerk under I.C. 3-11-10-26.2, and that
specifically provide procedures:
      (1)   Secure absentee votes cast via an electronic voting system in the
office of the Circuit Court Clerk in a manner that provides protection
comparable to the protection provided to absentee votes cast by paper ballot;
      (2)   Require the comparison of the signature on the absentee ballot
application with the applicant’s signature on the applicant’s voter
registration application;
      (3)   Ensure that an invalid ballot (as determined under I.C. 3-11.5) is
not counted; and
      (4)   The other procedures that Board considers otherwise useful in
implementing I.C. 3-11-10.26.2.
   (C)   In accordance with I.C. 3-11-10-26(f), an absentee ballot voted on an
electronic voting system pursuant to this section is not required to bear the
seal, signature, and initials of the clerk prescribed by I.C. 3-11-10-27.
(Res. 2007-01, passed 2-27-2007)
**** BOARD OF COMMISSIONERS ****
§ 30.15 COUNTY EXECUTIVE.
   The three-member Board of Commissioners of the county is the county
executive. It shall transact all business of the county, and do so in the name
of “The Board of Commissioners of the County of Lawrence” (see I.C. 36-2-2-2).
It shall constitute a corporate entity. (Dice v. County Board of Finance, 99
Ind. 405, 192 N.E. 770 (1934).)
(Prior Code, § 2-2-1)
§ 30.16 TERM OF OFFICE; MANNER OF ELECTION.
   (A)   The term of office for a member of the County Commissioners is four
years, beginning January 1 immediately after his or her election, and
continuing until his or her successor is elected and qualified.
(I.C. 36-2-2-3)
   (B)   County Commissioners shall be elected at general elections by the
county’s voters, the number of which to be elected to the executive alternating
between one and two at succeeding general elections.
(Prior Code, § 2-2-2)
§ 30.17 QUALIFICATIONS; FORFEITURES; DECLARATION OF VACANCY.
   (A)   To be eligible for election to the County Commissioners, a person must
have been a registered voter of the district from which he or she have run for
at least six months before his or her election, and must remain a resident of
that district after taking office.
   (B)   If any elected Commissioner fails to remain a resident of the district
from which he or she was elected, then he or she forfeits that office.
   (C)   In such a case, the County Council (“county fiscal body”) shall
declare the office vacant whenever a County Commissioner forfeits his or her
office under division (A) above.
   (D)   Should a member of the County Commissioners (“county executive”) want
to resign, he or she must send written notice of such fact to the President of
the County Council (“county fiscal body”) and the County Council shall then
declare his or her office to be vacant.
(Prior Code, § 2-2-3)
§ 30.18 COUNTY SHERIFF, POLICE OFFICER ATTENDANCE.
   The County Sheriff, or a Deputy Sheriff, shall attend the meetings of the
County Commissioners, if requested by the County Commissioners, and shall
execute the orders of the Board of Commissioners. (I.C. 36-2-2-15) (Prior Code,
§ 2-2-4)
§ 30.19 CHIEF ADMINISTRATIVE OFFICERS.
   As the county executive, the Board of Commissioners is the chief
administrative body of the county and shall control the day-to-day operations
of the following departments which are hereby established:
   (A)   County Courthouse custodian(s);
   (B)   County Attorney;
   (C)   County Veterans Officer;
   (D)   County Sanitarian; and
   (E)   County Engineer (if such Commissioners should determine to create such
a position).
(Prior Code, § 2-2-5)
§ 30.20 MEETINGS.
   (A)   (1)   The Board of Commissioners shall meet one time every two weeks.
Said meeting is to coincide with the pay-day schedule of county employees, in
order that the Commissioners may sign claims and attend all other business.
      (2)   The current state statute requires at least one meeting each month,
with the dates of regular meetings to be established by resolution at the first
meeting in January of each year. (See I.C. 36-2-2-6.)
(Prior Code, § 2-3-1)
   (B)   (1)   (a)   Special meetings of the County Commissioners may be called
by a member of the County Commissioners, or by the County Auditor, provided at
least six days’ notice is given of such special meeting (unless the meeting is
called to deal with an emergency under I.C. 5-14-1.5-5).
         (b)   If the office of the Auditor is vacant, a special meeting may be
called by the County Clerk. If both the offices of County Auditor and County
Clerk are vacant, a special meeting may be called by the County Recorder.
      (2)   The notice of the special meeting must include a specific statement
of the purposes of the meeting, and the County Commissioners may not conduct
any unrelated business at that special meeting. (I.C. 36-2-2-8) (Prior Code, §
2-3-2)
   (C)   (1)   The County Commissioners shall meet in the County Commissioners’
court room adjacent to the Auditor’s office in the Courthouse, Public Square,
Bedford, Indiana.
      (2)   However, the County Commissioners may select a new location other
than the County Courthouse for its meetings only if the courthouse is not
suitable, not convenient, or has been replaced or supplemented by other
buildings to house county government offices (see I.C. 36-2-2-9). If any such
alternate place is chosen as a place for meeting, notice shall be posted on a
wall in the Auditor’s office.
(Prior Code, § 2-3-3)
   (D)   The County Commissioners shall keep their office in the Commissioners’
court room open each day and at least one of its members shall be available
during normal business hours for county offices. (I.C. 36-2-2-10) (Prior Code,
§ 2-3-4)
   (E)   (1) The County Auditor shall be in attendance at all meetings of the
Board of Commissioners and shall record in writing the official proceedings of
said Board.
      (2)   If a copy of the Commissioners’ minutes has been signed and sealed
by the Auditor and introduced into evidence in any court of law, then that copy
shall be presumed to be an accurate record of the executive’s proceedings.
(I.C. 36-2-2-11) (Prior Code, § 2-3-5)
   (F)   (1)   In the Commissioners’ court room no person shall use foul,
abusive, or contemptuous language or behave in a disorderly manner. Nor shall
they refuse to obey the orders of any of the Commissioners or any Sheriff or
County Deputy Sheriff who might give any such order while the Commissioners are
in session. (Power of contempt provided by I.C. 36-2-2-15 and I.C. 36-1-4-13.)
      (2)   The Board of Commissioners may, in order to enforce and execute its
orders, and to preserve peace in the Commissioners’ court room, do the
following:
         (a)   Have the right to formally cite a person of, hear evidence, and
issue a finding as to whether or not a person is in contempt of said Board of
Commissioners. And if so, at the same time determine what punishment should be
meted out as a result of such finding, within the limits as hereinafter stated
in division (F)(2)(b) below.
         (b)   The procedure for citing any person for contempt and for the
hearing of any such person so charged is as follows.
            1.   The Board of Commissioners shall file with the County Auditor
a combined petition for citation in writing, which shall consist of a written
statement which shall name the person so cited for such contemptuous behavior.
The citation shall further contain the allegations of contempt and a statement
of the facts upon which such allegations are based. The same petition for
citation shall be verified by such Commissioners. As a further part of such
statement, there shall be a written notice of the date, hour, and place of the
hearing on such citation for contempt.
            2.   Thereafter the Board shall issue the original and one copy of
such petition for citation and notice of hearing to the County Sheriff or any
of their duly appointed deputies, of this county or any other county in which
said person so accused may be found, which shall be served upon the person so
alleged to be in contempt by such county police officer.
            3.   a.   Such Sheriff or Deputy Sheriff shall serve a copy of such
petition for citation and notice of hearing upon such person so alleged to be
in contempt. Upon serving such petition and notice, said county police officer
shall read the entire contents of such document to such person so alleged to be
in contempt and shall leave a copy of the document with such person so served.
               b.   Moreover, the county police officer shall then make due
return in writing upon the original document back to said County Commissioners
by filing the same in the office of the County Auditor.
               c.   Such return shall state the date, hour, and place that such
document was served, that the contents of such notice and citation were read to
such person alleged to be in such contempt, and be signed by the person so
making such notice.
            4.   Such petition for citation for contempt and notice of hearing
shall be sufficient if the same is in the words and figures following, to-wit:
STATE OF INDIANA
COUNTY OF LAWRENCE, SS:
IN THE COMMISSIONERS’ COURT OF
LAWRENCE COUNTY, INDIANA
NO.: _______________
To be Served Upon:
______________________________
Address:
______________________________
CITATION FOR CONTEMPT OF COMMISSIONERS’ COURT
AND
NOTICE OF HEARING:
   To the person named above: You are hereby notified that the Board of
Commissioners of the County of Lawrence, State of Indiana, hereinafter
referred to as “Commissioners,” are of the preliminary opinion that you are
in contempt of the Commissioners for the following reason or reasons:
   And that you are ordered to appear before the said Commissioners on
_____________, 20__, at the hour of _____ (A.M./P.M.), at the following
location:
City:_______________ State:_______________ ZIP:________________
   And to then and there answer to such allegation of contempt, and, in the
event that you should be found to be in contempt by said Commissioners,
then to show cause, if any there be, why you should not be punished for
such contempt.
   Your failure to appear on that date without the prior written approval
of the Board of Commissioners shall result in a writ of attachment or order
of attachment being issued for your arrest.
   Further, you have the right in these proceedings before the
Commissioners to be represented by an attorney, to hear the evidence
against you, and present evidence by the testimony of witnesses or the
introduction of evidence. Should you not attend the hearing and your
absence is not excused in writing, as above provided for, then the
Commissioners shall have the right to proceed to hear and make final
determination of the matter in your absence.
Dated:           Board of Commissioners, County of Lawrence
________________
By: ___________________.

            5.   Any person so charged must be given at least five days minimum
notice between the date of service of notice of such contempt citation,
including Saturdays, Sundays, and holidays, said time to begin to run on the
first day after such notice has been served upon such person. (Such hearing
shall not be on a Saturday, Sunday, or holiday which is recognized by the
county by being on the official county holiday recognition list on file with
the County Auditor.)
            6.   a.   The hearing shall be conducted by the County
Commissioners sitting together as a judicial panel to be known as the
Commissioners’ Court, hereinafter referred to as “Court,” and with the County
Auditor administering all oaths. All testimony shall be under oath as in civil
suits. In the event all three Commissioners cannot for any reason sit as such
panel, at least two of such Commissioners may do so, and the same shall
constitute a valid judicial panel. However, if only two Commissioners so serve,
then their decision must be unanimous.
               b.   If all three Commissioners so serve, then their decision
must be by majority. The hearing may be continued for the convenience of the
parties as the Commissioners should determine, but not otherwise. The Auditor
shall record the hearing by means of tape recorder or another available device.
Nothing herein shall prevent the Commissioners from using any court room in the
county and the recording devices already available there, provided it is
available and would not cause an inconvenience to such Court.
            7.   The decision shall be by majority vote of those County
Commissioners so sitting in judgment, and shall be in writing, with the
original filed with the Auditor, a copy served upon the person so charged, and
a copy to the Sheriff’s Department if further action by the Sheriff’s
Department is necessary. Provided, however, that the Commission shall have the
right to take such a matter under advisement for not more than 48 hours.
            8.   All the hearings shall be conducted informally in order to
determine the substantial rights of the parties. The parties may present such
evidence as the Court deems necessary in order to determine the ultimate
question of whether a person did or did not act in contempt of such
Commissioners. Any person so charged shall have the right to examine his or her
own witnesses, present evidence, and cross-examine witnesses of the opposing
party. The Court may examine any witness and may require any person so before
the Court to produce any evidence which it may deem necessary for the proper
determination of the case. In general, the State Rules of Civil Procedure shall
apply in any such hearing, but not to such an extent as to prevent as a full
presentation of fact or to jeopardize the right of the other party. No improper
conduct shall be permitted, but the Court shall consider all hearsay evidence
of any nature permitted under the State Rules of Evidence as exceptions to the
hearsay rule, and the Board of Commissioners may, in its discretion, consider
any hearsay evidence of any nature to which timely objection is not made by an
opposing party.
               a.   This does not mean, however, that the Board of
Commissioners must hear all hearsay evidence merely because no timely objection
is made thereto.
               b.   The Board shall have the right to make a determination to
not hear certain evidence it considers unnecessary. This shall be left up to
the judgment of the hearing members.
(Prior Code, § 2-3-6)
      (3)   (a)   The County Commissioners enforces its orders by attachment or
other compulsory process. (I.C. 36-2-2-15)
         (b)   Fines assessed by the County Commissioners shall be executed,
collected, and paid over in the same manner as other fines.
(I.C. 36-2-2-15)
         (c)   The power of the Board of Commissioners of the county to enforce
its orders by attachment, contempt, or other compulsory process should not be
confused with the limitation of I.C. 36-1-3-8(8), which prohibits both the
power to prescribe a penalty for conduct constituting a crime of infraction
under state statute, and I.C. 36-1-3-8(9) which prohibits the power to
prescribe a penalty of imprisonment for an ordinance violation.
         (d)   The County Sheriff or one of his or her deputies shall execute
the orders of the Board of Commissioners in these respects.
(I.C. 36-2-2-15) (Prior Code, § 2-3-7)
      (4)   (a)   The Commissioners shall have the right to require any person
to attend one of its meetings or a meeting of one of the county departments, by
serving upon that person a subpoena to so attend. Such subpoena shall be
essentially in the form provided by the State Rules of Procedure, but shall be
signed by at least two of the Commissioners, be stamped with their seal, and be
attested to by the County Auditor. Such subpoena shall be in duplicate with any
police officer or Sheriff being directed to serve same without charge, read it
to the person so named therein, leave a copy of same with the person so served,
and make due return of the date, time, person served, and manner of service in
writing on the back of the subpoena. Failure to do so authorizes the County
Commissioners to order such person to be taken into custody and brought before
such Board.
(I.C. 36-1-4-12, I.C. 36-2-2-15)
         (b)   The Board of Commissioners may also order the production of
documents and things which the Board considers to be evidence, without any
necessity for the filing of any petition for production of documents, or
things. It shall be sufficient if the order to produce is in a form
substantially like that prescribed by the State Rules of Procedure. Such form
shall be in duplicate, shall be issued to any Sheriff or Deputy Sheriff in the
state, and shall order the person so served to produce, at a date, place, and
hour, any evidence which the Board of Commissioners considers relevant to any
matter being taken under its consideration. Such order shall describe such item
or items to be produced with sufficient clarity so that the average person
would understand what is sought. Such Sheriff or Deputy Sheriff shall make due
return upon the original in the same manner as they make returns in civil
cases, showing the specific information set for in division (F)(4)(a) above.
Any person receiving such an order to produce shall have the right to produce
xerox copies, or other copies made by wet or dry photo-copying process, and
retain the originals, so long as the copies so produced are clear, legible
copies and can be read by the Commissioners; provided, however, that the Board
of Commissioners shall have the right to specify on the order that no copies
will be accepted. In that case, the originals must be produced.
         (c)   When the matter has been concluded, the County Commissioners
shall order the items so produced to be released, and the person so order to
produce the items may receive such items back, provided they are the originals
and not copies, upon such person signing a receipt prepared by the Auditor to
acknowledge such return. No copies need be returned by the Commissioners unless
they should determine voluntarily to do so.
         (d)   Because the option of furnishing copies is for the convenience
of the person so served, and not for the convenience or benefit of the County
Commissioners, the cost of copying shall be paid for solely by the person so
ordered to produce such evidence.
(Prior Code, § 2-3-8)
      (5)   The County Sheriff or any of their duly appointed deputies of any
county in the state shall execute the orders of the Board of Commissioners in
these respects.
(I.C. 36-2-2-15) (Prior Code, § 2-3-9)
   (G)   (1)   The President of the Board of Commissioners shall preside over
all Commissioners’ meetings. In the absence of the President, either one of the
other two member may preside over the meeting with the consent of the remaining
member announced to the Auditor.
      (2)   (a)   The Commissioners’ meetings shall have the following order of
business:
            1.   Call to order by Auditor and roll call;
            2.   Reading of the minutes of the previous meeting and approval by
Commissioners. At such time any corrections to those minutes must be brought up
for consideration;
            3.   Old business;
            4.   New business; and
            5.   Adjournment.
         (b)   Due to the fact that bids are required to be opened at certain
hours, and out-of-town persons may be in attendance, it may be necessary to
occasionally set aside one certain subject temporarily, to attend to such
items, and then return to the regular agenda.
      (3)   The following rules shall apply to all Commissioners’ meetings:
         (a)   After discussion of any subject or matter, any Commissioner may
“call for the question,” which is the same as asking if there is a motion (or a
second for a motion already made). If there is no motion and second, which
would qualify the issue to be voted upon, made within five minutes after said
question is called for, then the subject is automatically continued to the next
Commissioners’ meeting and the Chairperson will proceed without delay to the
next matter before the Board; provided, however, that this rule may be set
aside upon a majority vote of the Commissioners at any time.
         (b)   An ordinance, order, or resolution is considered adopted when it
is passed by a majority vote and signed by the presiding officer. If required,
an order, ordinance, or resolution shall be published (promulgated) as required
by statute before it takes effect.
         (c)   An ordinance prescribing a penalty for or forfeiture for a
violation must, before it takes effect, be published once each week for two
successive weeks.
(I.C. 5-3-1-1 et seq., I.C. 36-2-4-8)
         (d)   All appointments by Commissioners shall be in writing and
certified to by the County Auditor (I.C. 36-2-2-12), and shall bear the seal of
the Board of Commissioners, and either a photocopy or xerox copy of such
appointment bearing such seal and signature of the Auditor shall be conclusive
proof that said person whose name appears thereon has been appointed to the
position stated therein.
          (e)   Unanimous consent of the members present is required to pass an
ordinance on the same day or at the same meeting at which it is introduced.
(I.C. 36-2-4-7)
         (f)   A majority vote of the Commissioners is a two-thirds vote of all
members.
(Prior Code, § 2-3-10)
   (H)   For all matters that are not controlled by this section, Robert’s
Rules of Order shall apply. The Board of Commissioners shall conduct its
meetings in accordance with the terms thereof.
(Prior Code, § 2-3-11)
   (I)   (1)   It is the duty of each member of the County Board of
Commissioners to attend and be present at any course conducted for local
officials which the Purdue Road School may cause to occur.
      (2)   The County Council shall appropriate sufficient monies to pay each
Commissioner a per diem for expenses each day together with mileage for each
mile necessarily traveled to such Road School at the rate permitted to be paid
to state officers and employees which is prevailing at the time the trip is
made.
(I.C. 8-17-7-7) (Prior Code, § 2-3-12)
§ 30.21 OPERATION OF COUNTY OFFICES.
   (A)   The County Board of Commissioners has been given the responsibility
and authority of determining the times and manner in which the Courthouse and/
or the county offices are to be open. No other office holder, deputy, or
employee has the authority to close the Courthouse and/or the county offices
other than the Board of Commissioners.
   (B)   The County Board of Commissioners acknowledges that there may be times
where the weather or other intervening factors may result in the need to close
the Courthouse unexpectedly or without prior notice or scheduling. All office
holders, deputies, and employees are hereby notified that the Board of
Commissioners, and only the Board of Commissioners, has authority to make the
decision as to whether or not to close the Courthouse and/or the county
offices. If and when the Board of Commissioners decides that circumstances
warrant the closing of the Courthouse or county offices, one of the
Commissioners or a designee of the Commissioners (an employee from the
Auditor’s office or the Commissioners’ Administrative Assistant) will notify
all of the office holders as soon as possible. The Commissioners hope that
notice of such closing can be issued in writing and delivered or faxed to all
offices. However, under some circumstances it may not be possible to deliver
written notice and the Commissioners reserve the right to issue notice verbally
when necessary.
   (C)   The Board of Commissioners understands that concerns regarding safety
can be involved in addressing matters related to the closing of the Courthouse
and County offices. Notwithstanding the assessment and judgment of others, the
Board of County Commissioners is the sole and final authority in making the
decision regarding closing of the county facilities.
(Res. 99-01, passed 1-19-1999)
**** COUNTY COUNCIL ****
§ 30.35 COUNTY FISCAL BODY.
   The seven-member County Council elected under I.C. 36-2-3-2 is the county
fiscal body. It shall be known as the “Lawrence County Council” or the “County
Council.”
(I.C. 36-2-3-2) (Prior Code, § 2-4-1)
§ 30.36 TERMS OF OFFICE.
   The fiscal body shall be elected at general elections for four-year terms on
a staggered basis, pursuant to the state election laws as they now exist or may
hereafter be changed.
(I.C. 36-2-3-3) (Prior Code, § 2-4-2)
§ 30.37 COUNTY SHERIFF, POLICE OFFICER ATTENDANCE.
   The County Sheriff or a Deputy Sheriff shall attend the meetings of the
fiscal body, if requested by the fiscal body, and shall execute its orders.
(Prior Code, § 2-4-3)
§ 30.38 COUNTY AUDITOR AS CLERK.
   The County Auditor is the Clerk for the fiscal body and shall preserve the
fiscal body’s records in the office of the Auditor; keep an accurate record of
the fiscal body’s proceedings; record the “ayes” and “nays” on each vote
appropriating money or fixing the rate of a tax levy; and record the “ayes” and
“nays” on other votes when requested to do so by two or more members.
(I.C. 36-2-3-6) (Prior Code, § 2-4-4)
§ 30.39 PRESIDENT.
   The fiscal body must elect a President. Any and all persons serving as
President, President Pro Tem, or otherwise Chief Presiding Officer, must be
elected from the membership of the fiscal body.
(I.C. 36-2-3-6) (Prior Code, § 2-4-5)
§ 30.40 RIGHT TO HIRE ATTORNEY, OTHER PERSONNEL.
   The fiscal body shall have the right to employ legal and administrative
personnel necessary to assist and advise it in the performance of its function
and duties.
(I.C. 36-2-3-6) (Prior Code, § 2-4-6)
§ 30.41 SOLE POWER TO FIX COMPENSATION.
   The county fiscal body alone may fix the compensation of officers, deputies,
and other employees whose compensation is payable from the County General Fund,
County Highway Fund, County Welfare Fund, County Health Fund, County Park and
Recreation Fund, Aviation Fund, or any other fund from which the County Auditor
issues warrants for compensation.
(I.C. 36-2-5-3) (Prior Code, § 2-4-7)
§ 30.42 MEETINGS.
   (A)   (1)   The fiscal body shall hold its meetings in the county seat, in
the Auditor’s office, or in another location provided by the County
Commissioners (executive) and approved by the fiscal body.
      (2)   (a)   Regular meetings. The fiscal body shall hold a regular
meeting in January of each year after its election for the purpose of
organizing and for such other business as may come before that body, and a
regular annual meeting as prescribed by I.C. 6-1.1-17-1, to adopt the regular
annual budget and tax rate.
         (b)   Special meetings. The fiscal body may hold a special meeting
when called by the County Auditor, by the President of the fiscal body, or by a
majority of the members of the fiscal body. However, the Auditor, President, or
members calling such meeting shall give 48 hours’ advanced written notice of
the meeting to each member of the fiscal body and shall publish at least one
day before the meeting the notice in accordance with I.C. 5-3-1 and I.C. 36-2-
3-7. This requirement of giving notice shall not apply to a meeting called to
deal with an emergency under I.C. 5-14-1.5-5.
         (c)   Court-ordered expenditures. If a court orders the Auditor to
make any expenditure of county money for a purpose for which an appropriation
has not been made, the Auditor shall immediately call an emergency meeting of
the fiscal body to discuss the matter. This must be done within three working
days of the receipt of the order by the Auditor, and notice of the meeting day,
time, and place is sufficient if given by telephone to the fiscal body members,
provided, however, such notice must still be given 48 hours in advance of such
meeting, and the notice of such meeting must be published in the newspaper at
least one day prior to the meeting.
(I.C. 36-2-3-7) (Prior Code, § 2-4-8)
   (B)   (1)   Unanimous consent of the members present is required to pass an
ordinance on the same day or at the same meeting which it is introduced.
(I.C. 36-2-4-7)
      (2)   This section does not apply to an ordinance of a county fiscal body
for additional appropriations.
(I.C. 36-2-4-7) (Prior Code, § 2-4-9)
   (C)   (1)   Within a reasonable time after an ordinance is adopted, the
Auditor shall record it in a book which he or she keeps for that purpose, and
this record must contain the signature of the presiding officer and the
attestation of the Auditor.
      (2)   The record itself, or a certified copy issued by the Auditor, shall
be presumptive evidence that the ordinance was adopted and took effect.
(I.C. 36-2-4-9) (Prior Code, § 2-4-10)
   (D)   (1)   The fiscal body’s meetings shall have the following order of
business:
         (a)   Call to order by the Auditor and roll call;
         (b)   Reading of the minutes of previous meeting, and approval by the
fiscal body. At such time, any corrections to those minutes must be brought up
for consideration, or they are waived;
          (c)   Old business;
         (d)   New business; and
         (e)   Adjournment.
      (2)   The following rules shall apply to all fiscal body meetings: After
discussion of any subject or matter any member may “call for the question” and
request a vote on the subject. The presiding officer shall determine if there
is any more discussion on the subject, and then may ask for a motion and second
to adopt such item or items. If there is no motion or second within five
minutes after the question is called for, as the presiding officer’s
calculation of such time, then the subject is deemed to have been rejected.
However, by a majority vote of the members in attendance at any particular
meeting, this requirement may be waived and the time enlarged, or the matter
continued to a later meeting as such majority should so determine.
(Prior Code, § 2-4-11)
   (E)   (1)   A simple majority vote shall be sufficient to enact any item
unless state law require something different in any particular instance. A
MAJORITY VOTE means a majority of all County Council members who are elected,
not just present at any particular meeting. Thus, a majority vote shall be four
out of seven members in attendance, if all attend; four out of six, if only six
members are in attendance at the time the vote is taken; three out of five, if
only five members are in attendance at the time said vote is taken; and three
out of four, if only four members are present at the time of said vote was
taken.
(I.C. 36-2-4-4)
      (2)   A TWO-THIRDS VOTE means at least two-thirds of all elected members
(which for the County Council shall mean five out of the seven County Council
members).
      (3)   There must be at least four members in attendance at any meeting of
the County Council in order for the Council to have a quorum and thus in order
for the County Council to properly conduct business (see I.C. 36-2-4-3(a)).
However, the County Council may, by a two-thirds vote, adopt a rule specifying
that a certain number of members greater than four constitute a quorum.
(I.C. 36-2-4-3(b))
(Prior Code, § 2-4-12)
   (F)   (1)   A discussion of all issues that are to come to a vote before the
County Council is the goal of such Presiding Officer, but when, in his or her
opinion, the discussion is becoming repetitive or unhelpful in clarifying the
issues for the members of the fiscal body, then the Presiding Officer shall
call for the question, at which point, asking for “aye” votes first, and then
“nay” votes. The Auditor shall record the votes. The presiding officer shall
likewise record the votes, and announce the result.
      (2)   (a)   If the Presiding Officer is not sure as to the result, he or
she may ask the Auditor to poll the members for their vote. Any such request
must be made immediately after the vote is taken or immediately before the vote
is asked for.
         (b)   The Auditor shall ask each member in turn how they voted and the
member shall so state to the Auditor (known as “polling” the Council) in a
voice loud enough so that members of the public in attendance and other members
and the Auditor may know. Such result shall be the final vote on any such
subject. Any request for polling the Council may be made by any Council member
as well.
(Prior Code, § 2-4-13)
   (G)   The County Council shall not take matters under advisement beyond that
particular meeting’s adjournment. The Presiding Officer may set one matter
aside until another matter is determined during that meeting, or take requests
out of order, but a final determination on all issues before the Council must
be made before that meeting is adjourned.
(Prior Code, § 2-4-14)
   (H)   For procedural matters that are not provided for herein or in I.C. 36-
2-4, Robert’s Rules of Order shall apply, and the county fiscal body shall
conduct his or her meetings in accordance with the terms thereof.
(Prior Code, § 2-4-15)
§ 30.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   The County Commissioners shall have the right to punish any finding of
contempt, as stated in §
30.20(F), by a fine of not more than $3 per day or by imprisonment for not more
than 24 hours or both.
(I.C. 36-2-2-15) (Prior Code, § 2-3-6)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 31.001 APPOINTMENTS BY COMMISSIONERS, COUNCIL.
   (A)   As set forth in §
30.20(G)(3)(d), all appointments by the Board of County Commissioners shall be
in writing and certified to by the County Auditor under the seal of the
Commissioners (I.C. 36-2-2-12). Either a photocopy or xerox copy of such
appointments bearing such Commissioners’ seal and signature of the Auditor
shall be conclusive proof that said person whose name appears thereon has been
appointed to the position stated therein.
(Prior Code, § 2-7-4)
   (B)   (1)   All appointments made by the County Council shall likewise be
both in writing, signed by the members of the County Council present at such
meeting (at which final action was taken to make such appointments), and
certified by the County Auditor, who shall put the county’s seal thereupon.
      (2)   Either a photocopy or xerox copy of such an appointment which bears
such county’s seal shall be conclusive proof that said person whose name
appears thereon has been appointed to the position stated therein. (No prior
statutory authority existed for such a manner of designating County Council
appointees; however, this code adopts such provisions under the powers provided
under Home Rule [see I.C. 36-1-3-1].)
(Prior Code, § 2-7-5)
§ 31.002 NON-EXCLUSIVE LISTING.
   The appointments of §§
31.025 through
31.029 is not all-inclusive of either the Board of Commissioners or the County
Council, and the failure to include an appointment or membership that may now
exist, or which may hereafter come into being, shall not abridge the right of
such appointment or to serve in such capacity.
(Prior Code, § 2-7-3)
§ 31.003 APPOINTMENT OF VACANCIES.
   With regard to any vacancy occurring in any of the following offices, the
vacancy shall be filled according to statute by the following persons:
 
Position Vacated  Filled By
County Council    Remaining members of County Council
Township Assessor County Assessor (subject to the approval of the State Board
                  of Tax Commissioners)
Township Trustee  Board of Commissioners
 
(Prior Code, § 2-7-7)
§ 31.004 OVERTIME PAY.
   (A)   This section shall apply to all county employees excepting therefrom
all deputy sheriffs, the County Jailer, and all hourly workers of the County
Highway Department. All employees of the Prosecuting Attorney for the 81st
Judicial Circuit are included herein if they are not otherwise excluded by
reason of being personal staff members involved in policy making decisions,
such as the full-time Deputy Prosecuting Attorney. By the terms and provisions
of the Fair Labor Standards Act, being 29 U.S.C. §§ 201 et seq., any and all
elected officials and department heads, such as the Veterans’ Officer, the
Civil Defense Officer, the 4-H Extension Agent, and like, are exempt from the
provisions of this act.
   (B)   (1)   Effective April 14, 1986, the county shall compensate all
employees on the basis of a fixed weekly salary for up to 40 hours worked in
each workweek. The employee’s regular scheduled hours of work will be 35 hours
each workweek; however, additional work will be necessary from time to time in
order to fulfill the functions of his or her position, and it is required that
such employee shall person such work as necessary. The employee’s fixed weekly
salary will cover up to five hours of such additional work each week. No
additional compensation shall be paid for such addition five hours per week.
      (2)   To be eligible for such job, such employee must be on the job
physically ready, and able for work to be assigned to such an employee;
further, such employee must actually work such period claimed.
      (3)   In order that the county be advised of its obligations to pay any
such overtime or grant compensatory time, the time card for such employee, so
signed by such employee, and approved in writing by his or her supervisor, must
be physically delivered to the County Auditor for payment in advance. The
employee must verify that the same is accurate.
      (4)   Compensatory time will be earned at a rate of one and one-half
hours for each hour actually worked over 40 in the established workweek of such
employee. The employee may accumulate up to 240 hours of compensatory time off,
and the employee will further receive monetary overtime compensation for
overtime hours actually worked which would cause such employee’s accumulation
to exceed 240 hours.
      (5)   Overtime shall be paid for all hours worked over 40 in a given
workweek. The amount of overtime pay will be one and one-half times the
employee’s regular hourly rate for all hours worked over 40.
      (6)   All pay for overtime and/or grants of compensatory time, which,
computed on a weekly basis, shall nevertheless be paid every two weeks in the
same manner as the employee’s regular compensation is paid. The overtime
compensation shall be based upon the regular pay rate at the time such hours
were worked. However, if an employee should work a certain number of hours at
one job with one pay rate, and additional hours at another job with a different
pay rate so as to justify overtime pay, then such overtime pay rate shall be
paid by paying the straight time hourly rate for the job such employee is
actually performing during such overtime hours, plus an additional overtime
premium equal to one-half of such employee’s weighted average hourly rate for
the week. The weighted average hourly rate will equal the total straight time
pay for the week, divided by the total number of hours worked as provided by
law.
      (7)   The employee may use earned compensatory time off with the approval
of such employee’s supervisor authorized to grant such approval. If it is not
possible to approve the particular compensatory time off requested by such
employee, time off will be granted within a reasonable period, normally within
30 days, unless the requested time off would unduly disrupt operations. The
employee will receive compensation based upon the regular pay rate in effect
when the compensatory time off is actually taken.
   (C)   The employer may elect to substitute monetary overtime for pay earned
as compensatory time off, either at the time overtime is actually worked or in
any later period. Monetary overtime compensation will be based upon the
employee’s regular pay rate at the time of payment.
   (D)   The county may also elect to apply earned compensatory time off to
hours the employee may have missed due to personal absence, short workweeks,
layoffs, or leave of absence, including medical leaves if not covered by paid
sick days, or authorized by a physician’s medical report. In periods of slack
work, it may be necessary to schedule time off, to which earned compensatory
time off will be applied.
   (E)   Upon forms provided by the County Auditor’s office, all employees
shall keep a regular, accurate record of their hours worked for each workday,
and the total hours worked by such employee for each and all workweeks. This
record shall include the time which said employee began work for that workday,
the time which employment ended for that workday, and any hours (or parts
thereof) of overtime for that day. Such time sheet shall be signed by the
employee who shall verify the correctness of its contents. Such time sheet
shall further be approved, in writing, by the person designated and authorized
by employee’s department head to make any such approval and processed by such
office which shall tabulate and verify the total hours to the Auditor, together
with any overtime pay and/or compensatory time to which such employee may be
entitled to. Such overtime pay shall be paid within 30 days of the submission
of same to the County Auditor.
   (F)   In addition thereto, the office employing such employee shall keep an
accurate record of all vacation days, sick days, and personal leave days of
each employee upon forms prescribed by the office of the County Auditor.
   (G)   All records hereunder shall be maintained for a minimum of three
years.
   (H)   No overtime shall be paid and/or compensatory time allowed for any
hours not shown upon the time sheets received by the office of the County
Auditor and it shall be the responsibility of the office employing any
particular employee to see that the same are turned in timely to the Auditor.
It is further the responsibility of each employee to make an accurate record of
the information required herein and the county shall be entitled to rely upon
any time sheet so signed by such employee as being an accurate representation
of such work record.
   (I)   This section shall be in effect on April 15, 1986, beginning at 12:01
a.m. and shall continue until further action by the Board of Commissioners.
   (J)   This section shall likewise apply to any employees employed after this
date.
   (K)   Any employee who may have another job and which such other job is
subject to the Fair Labor Standards Act must report the same to the Auditor in
writing and must notify such employee’s immediate supervisor of all hours
worked on said other job in order that the overtime requirements of the Fair
Labor Standards Act may be met. In such case, overtime shall be computed as
provided by the Fair Labor Standards Act.
(Prior Code, § 8-35-2) (Ord. 1986-, passed 4-15-1986)

§ 31.009 VACATION; SICK DAYS.
   (A)   Sick days.
      (1)   General. This section applies to each deputy, each full-time hourly
employee, and each salaried employee of the county.
      (2)   Definition. For the purpose of this section, the following
definition shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
         WORK YEAR. Begins with the first day that an employee is on the job
and gets paid in that position and shall end at 12:00 a.m. 365 days later (or
366 days later if a leap year is involved). For example, if an employee begins
work on January 12, 1989, then his or her work year is from January 12, 1989 to
12:00 a.m. on January 11, 1990.
      (3)   Accumulated sick days.
         (a)   Presently, every county employee accumulates two sick days per
month of service to the county. Under the present personnel policy of the
county, an employee may accumulate up to a total of 48 sick days. These days
may be carried over and held in reserve to be used as an employee may so need.
         (b)   After an employee has accumulated a total of 48 sick days, each
said employee may hereafter continue to accumulate two sick days per month to
be accumulated into a reserve for long-term or catastrophic illness. Each
employee may accumulate up to 96 days in the reserve for long-term or
catastrophic illness.
         (c)   Only those sick days not used at the end of each completed month
of service and only those sick days not needed to replenish the original 48
days of sick leave may be placed in the long-term or catastrophic illness
reserve. Once sick days are placed in the long-term or catastrophic illness
reserve, they may not be used to replace any of the original 48 sick days.
         (d)   The sick days held in reserve for long-term or catastrophic
illness are for the county employee use only and may not be used as a
substitute for any time off requested under the Family Medical Leave Act, being
29 U.S.C. §§ 2611 et seq.
         (e)   For an employee to be entitled to accumulate or use the reserve
sick days, an employee shall submit a written request to the County Board of
Commissioners for review and approval.
         (f)   In the event an employee leaves employment with the county for
any reason, voluntary or involuntary, said departing employee shall not be
entitled to any compensation for sick days accumulated in the reserve for long-
term or catastrophic illness.
         (g)   No sick days shall accumulate for any period that an employee is
absent under an unpaid leave of absence.
      (4)   Doctor’s note.
         (a)   For any and all days up to five days for each year that an
employee reports sick and asks to not come to work, such employee shall not be
required to have a doctor’s statement with him or her when the employee returns
to work to verify such illness. The employee’s honesty will be relied upon
(although this is subject to being checked independently by the employer or his
or her agent). For example, if an employee has 24 sick days accumulated and he
or she is off three days in the first of the year, but has a doctor’s excuse
for those three days, then the employee is off six days but does not have a
doctor’s excuse, the result would be that he or she would not receive pay for
the sixth day.
         (b)   All other days that an employee is off work because he or she
reports in sick, said employee must have a statement of a physician verifying
that fact and saying that, in the physician’s opinion, the employee should not
go to work or each of the days that the employee is off work during any such
year which exceeds five working days.
   (B)   Vacation days.
      (1)   [MISSING TEXT] satisfied that the supervisor and the supervisor’s
department can function for such extended period without such employee and
without it causing. an undue hardship upon the department. This shall be left
to the sole discretion of the department head.
      (2)   An employee must give his or her supervisor at least 14 calendar
days’ advance notice of his or her intention to take such vacation. The time
that such vacation is to be taken must be approved by the department head. If
two or more employees are seeking to take vacation during the same period, then
the department head has the right to deny the request of one or said employees.
This is to be left up to the discretion of the department head. Likewise, an
employee shall not take his or her vacation during peak period in which his or
her department is shorthanded or is extremely busy and the department head has
the right to refuse anyone’s request for any particular time if, in the
department head’s sole discretion, it is a peak period or such department is
shorthanded.
      (3)   Vacation time earned must be taken within the next 12 calendar
months once they are earned. For example, if an employee on January 12 is
entitled to three weeks vacation, he or she must schedule and take that
vacation time before January 11 of the next year. If he or she chooses not to
take such vacation but instead chooses to work, such employee shall lose such
vacation time and will only be paid his or her regular pay for the week or
weeks worked that he or she would otherwise be on vacation.
      (4)   If an employee quits his or her job or is fired and has earned
vacation days which he or she has not taken, then that employee should be paid
for those additional vacation days.
      (5)   No vacation days shall accumulate for any period that an employee
is absent under an unpaid leave or absence.
   (C)   Exclusions. This section shall not be binding upon the following
persons:
      (1)   Employees of the courts whose compensation is fixed by anyone other
than the County Commissioners and the County Council;
      (2)   The Director and employees of the Welfare Department, as it is now
a state agency;
      (3)   The County Health Officer, Health Nurse, and other specialized
personnel hired by the Board of Health which Board fixes the compensation or
such specialized personnel;
      (4)   The County Sanitarian, the County Veterans’ Officer, the Inspector
of Weights and Measures, and the curator of the County Museum;
      (5)   The manager of the airport;
      (6)   The County Attorney;
      (7)   Any elected official;
      (8)   The employees of the County Surveyor;
      (9)   All custodians; and
      (10)   The County Sheriff, deputy sheriffs, County Jailer, and any
employees or the County Sheriff.
(Ord. passed 1- -1989; Ord. 2002-07, passed 8- -2002)
§ 31.010 MILEAGE RATES.
   (A)   (1)   The County Board of Commissioners hereby establishes a per diem
rate of $50 per day for each day in which an employee of the county travels for
or attends to the official business of the county outside of his or her office
outside of the county.
      (2)   For purposes of this section, attending conferences or meetings
required in the ordinary course of business shall be an event that qualifies
for a per diem stipend.
   (B)   The County Board of Commissioners hereby establishes a mileage rate of
$0.42 per mile for all travel in which an employee of the county may engage in
while attending to the official business of the county outside or his or her
office. For purposes of this section, attending conferences or meetings
required in the ordinary course of business shall be an event that qualifies
for mileage reimbursement.
   (C)   Any and all claims for per diem and/or mileage reimbursement shall be
made through the established claims process which is managed and operated by
the Auditor of the county and shall require the approval of the County Board of
Commissioners before reimbursement is made.
   (D)   Nothing by way of this section shall act as an affirmative
representation or guarantee on the part of the County Board of Commissioners
that budgeted funds will be available to pay per diem or mileage expenses. Each
office holder shall be responsible to ensure that his or her budget has funds
properly appropriated and available to reimburse employees’ per diem or mileage
expenses.
(Ord. 2020-02, passed 2-18-2020)

§ 31.025 COUNTY ATTORNEY.
   (A)   For the County Attorney, the appointee must be licensed to practice
law in the state. The Attorney is not required, but normally expected, to be a
resident of the county.
   (B)   Inasmuch as the state code does not provide clear authority for this
position, although numerous citations imply such authority, such position is
hereby created by virtue of the powers granted unto to the Board of
Commissioners under Home Rule.
(I.C. 36-1-4-1 et seq.) (Prior Code, § 2-7-1)
§ 31.026 COUNTY HIGHWAY SUPERVISOR.
   (A)   For the County Highway Supervisor, there shall be an annual
appointment of one year. No statutory qualification’s set forth in state law,
however, the appointment of a certain person as such County Highway Engineer is
authorized under I.C. 8-1-7-3-10.
   (B)   Provided, however, if no appointment is made by the Commissioners to
this post, then the County Surveyor is, by statute, the Highway Supervisor and
shall receive additional compensation for this act as the Board of
Commissioners shall fix.
(I.C. 8-17-3-10 through 8-12-7-3) (Prior Code, § 2-7-1)
§ 31.027 HIGHWAY ENGINEER.
   For the County Highway Engineer, the position shall be filled by the Board
of Commissioners if it determines at any time that the services of a highway
engineer are needed. Qualifications are that such person must be a registered
professional engineer, licensed in the state, a state resident (or become a
resident during his or her term of employment), and experienced in highway
engineering and construction. The Commissioners may adopt additional
qualifications. This is not a required position.
(I.C. 8-17-5-1 through 8-17-5-12) (Prior Code, § 2-7-1)
§ 31.028 VETERANS’ SERVICE OFFICER.
   For the Veterans’ Service Officer, the position shall be made where the
Board of Commissioners determines that such a position is necessary.
(I.C. 10-17-1) (Prior Code, § 2-7-1-9)
§ 31.029 COUNTY SANITARIAN.

the state (State Board of Health).
(Prior Code, § 2-7-1)
§ 31.030 CHIEF DEPUTY EMPLOYEE.
   (A)   Certain elected officials are entitled to appoint one first or Chief
Deputy, and also may appoint the number of other full-time or part-time
deputies and employees authorized by the county fiscal body (County Council).
These officials are:
      (1)   County Auditor;
      (b)   County Treasurer;
      (c)   County Recorder; and
      (d)   County Superintendent of Schools.
(I.C. 36-2-16-4)
   (B)   In addition, other statutes provide for the appointment of a Chief
Deputy employee for other offices. These officials are all elective county
offices, except the offices of:
      (1)   Prosecuting Attorney;
      (2)   Clerk of the Circuit Court; and
      (3)   Judge of any Court.
(I.C. 3-13-7)
   (C)   In any of the instances enumerated in divisions (A) or (B) above,
where no Chief Deputy employee exists for such office as has become vacant, the
Judge of the Circuit Court shall appoint, as soon as it is reasonably possible,
an individual to fill such vacancy for the period of time between when the
vacancy occurs and when it is filled as provided for in I.C. 3-13-6.
(Prior Code, § 2-7-6)
§ 31.031 COUNTY ADMINISTRATOR.
   (A)   The position of County Administrator is hereby created pursuant to
I.C. 36-2-2-14.
   (B)   The County Administrator shall serve under the supervision of the
Board of Commissioners with such duties and responsibilities as set forth in
this section.
      (1)   The County Administrator shall report directly to the President of
the Board of Commissioners.
      (2)   The President of the Board of Commissioners shall be responsible
for exercising ordinary supervision over the County Administrator.
      (3)   The Board of Commissioners may, pursuant to I.C. 36-2-2-14(a),
withdraw any powers or duties assigned to the County Administrator by
resolution.
      (4)   The Board of Commissioners may, pursuant to I.C. 36-2-2-14(b)(8),
request from time to time that the County Administrator perform additional
duties.
   (C)   The County Administrator shall serve at the pleasure of the Board of
Commissioners.
(I.C. 36-2-2-14(a))
   (D)   The County Administrator shall receive such compensation and benefits
as may be determined by the Board of Commissioners subject to the usual budget
approval as established by statute.
   (E)   The County Administrator is hereby assigned the following offices,
positions, or duties under the control of the Board of Commissioners as allowed
by I.C. 36-2-2-14(a):
      (1)   He or she shall supervise assigned staff, including the
interviewing of candidates and making hiring decisions; conduct orientation for
new subordinate employees; plan, delegate, and control work assignments;
establish work goals; review salaries or assigned staff and recommend to the
Board of Commissioners such changes as may be appropriate; recommend personnel
actions; and keep supervisors and assigned staff informed of organizational
developments;
      (2)   He or she shall review and make appropriate recommendations at
least annually to the Board of Commissioners for the improvement of the
county’s policies, procedures, and practices on personnel matters;
      (3)   He or she shall maintain knowledge of human resource trends and
employment legislation and regulation and recommend such measures as may be
appropriate to maintain compliance by the county with the same;
      (4)   He or she shall maintain responsibility for the county’s compliance
with federal, state, and local legislation and regulations pertaining to all
personnel matters;
      (5)   He or she shall communicate to all officeholders, department heads,
supervisors, and personnel as appropriate when changes are made in the county’s
personnel policies and procedures and ensure compliance with them;
      (6)   He or she shall assist the Board of Commissioners in the annual
review, preparation, and administration of the county’s wage and salary
program;
      (7)   He or she shall coordinate or conduct exit interviews to determine
the reasons for separations from county employment;
      (8)   He or she shall consult with the County Attorney as appropriate on
personnel matters;
      (9)   He or she shall communicate and work directly with officeholders
and department heads to assist them in carrying out their responsibilities on
personnel matters;
      (10)   He or she shall recommend, evaluate, and participate in staff
development for the county;
      (11)   He or she shall develop and maintain a human resource information
system that meets the county’s personnel needs;
      (12)   He or she shall participate on committees and special projects as
assigned by the president of the Board of Commissioners or by resolution of the
Board of Commissioners;
      (13)   He or she shall administer leave time, including, but not limited
to, Family and Medical Leave Act leave time;
      (14)   He or she shall administer recruiting and job postings;
      (15)   He or she shall revise, update, and maintain the County Employee
Handbook and all required human resources and administrative forms;
      (16)   He or she shall create, update, review, and revise job
descriptions;
      (17)   He or she shall serve as a liaison between the Board of
Commissioners and officeholders and department heads; and
      (18)   He or she shall perform duties related to the above and foregoing
as assigned by the president of the Board of Commissioners or by resolution of
the Board of Commissioners.
   (F)   The County Administrator is hereby authorized to do the following
under the supervision of the Board of Commissioners as allowed by I.C. 36-2-2-
14(b), and this section shall be deemed to be a resolution requesting other
duties of the County Administrator for the purposes of I.C. 36-2-2-14(b)(8),
pending such further resolutions as the Board of Commissioners may from time to
time make:
      (1)   He or she shall supervise assigned staff, including the
interviewing of candidates and making hiring decisions; conduct orientation for
new subordinate employees; plan, delegate, and control work assignments;
establish work goals; review salaries or assigned staff and recommend to the
Board of Commissioners such changes as may be appropriate; recommend personnel
actions; and keep supervisors and assigned staff informed of organizational
developments;
      (2)   He or she shall review and make appropriate recommendations at
least annually to the Board of Commissioners for the improvement of the
county’s policies, procedures, and practices on personnel matters;
      (3)   He or she shall maintain knowledge of human resource trends and
employment legislation and regulation and recommend such measures as may be
appropriate to maintain compliance by the county with the same;
      (4)   He or she shall maintain responsibility for the county’s compliance
with federal, state, and local legislation and regulations pertaining to all
personnel matters;
      (5)   He or she shall communicate to all officeholders, department heads,
supervisors, and personnel as appropriate when changes are made in the county’s
personnel policies and procedures and ensure compliance with them;
      (6)   He or she shall assist the Board of Commissioners in the annual
review, preparation, and administration of the county’s wage and salary
program;
      (7)   He or she shall coordinate or conduct exit interviews to determine
the reasons for separations from county employment;
      (8)   He or she shall consult with the County Attorney as appropriate on
personnel matters;
      (9)   He or she shall communicate and work directly with officeholders
and department heads to assist them in carrying out their responsibilities on
personnel matters;
      (10)   He or she shall recommend, evaluate, and participate in staff
development for the county;
      (11)   He or she shall develop and maintain a human resource information
system that meets the county’s personnel needs;
      (12)   He or she shall participate on committees and special projects as
assigned by the president of the Board of Commissioners or by resolution of the
Board of Commissioners;
      (13)   He or she shall administer leave time, including, but not limited
to, Family and Medical Leave Act leave time;
      (14)   He or she shall administer recruiting and job postings;
      (15)   He or she shall revise, update, and maintain the county employee
handbook and all required human resources and administrative forms;
      (16)   He or she shall create, update, review, and revise job
descriptions;
      (17)   He or she shall serve as a liaison between the Board of
Commissioners and officeholders and department heads;
      (18)   He or she shall perform duties related to the above and foregoing
as assigned by the president of the Board of Commissioners or by resolution of
the Board of Commissioners;
      (19)   He or she may attend meetings of the Board of Commissioners and
make such reports as he or she deems appropriate; and
      (20)   He or she shall assist the Board of Commissioners as necessary
with the preparation of the budget submissions required of the Commissioners by
state law.
   (G)   If the County Administrator is absent from the Administrator’s office
due to illness, death, vacation, resignation, or removal, the President of the
Board of Commissioners or a qualified person appointed by the Board of
Commissioners shall act as County Administrator until the County Administrator
returns to the duties of the County Administrator or the executive appoints a
new County Administrator.
(I.C. 36-2-2-14(c))
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 11-4-2024)
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see I.C. 36-2-2-14(b)(1); (b)(2); (b)(4); (b)(5); (b)
(7); (b)(8)

Commissioners) according to custom and practice of the county.
