**** COUNTY RECORDER ****
§ 31.060 FEES CHARGED FOR RECORDING.
   (A)   The County Recorder shall tax and collect the fees prescribed by this
section for recording, filing, copying, and other services he or she render;
subsequently, the County Recorder shall pay these fees into the county treasury
at the end of each calendar month. The fees prescribed and collected under this
section shall be instead of and shall supersede all other recording fees
required by law to be charged for services rendered by the County Recorder.
   (B)   The County Recorder shall charge:
      (1)   Four dollars for the first page and $1.50 for each additional page
of any document he or she records if the pages are not larger than nine by 15
inches in size;
      (2)   Ten dollars for the first page and $3 for each additional page of
any document he or she record if the pages are larger than nine by 15 inches in
size;
      (3)   No fee for recording an official bond of a public officer, deputy,
appointee, or employee;
      (4)   One dollar for attesting to the release or assignment of an
instrument on the record of that instrument;
      (5)   One dollar for cross-reference of a recorder document;
      (6)   Three dollars per page for furnishing typewritten copies of
records;
      (7)   Fifty cents per page for furnishing copies of records produced by a
photographic process;
      (8)   One dollar for acknowledging or certifying to a document; and
      (9)   One dollar for each deed he or she records, in addition to other
fees for deeds, for County Surveyor’s Corner Perpetuation Fund for use as
provided for in I.C. 36-2-12-11(e).
   (C)   As used in this section, RECORD and RECORDING includes the functions
of recording, filing, and filing for record.
   (D)   The County Recorder shall post the fees set forth in division (B)
above in a prominent place within the County Recorder’s office where it will be
readily accessible to the public.
(Prior Code, § 2-9-1)
§ 31.061 TERM OF OFFICE.
   The Recorder is a constitutional office and the term of that office under
Article 6, Section of the Constitution of Indiana is four years and continues
until his or her successor is elected and qualified.
(I.C. 36-2-11-2) (Prior Code, § 2-9-2)
§ 31.062 OFFICE HOURS AND LOCATION.
   The County Recorder’s office shall be located in a building provided by the
County Commissioners. The office shall be open on every day of the year except
Sundays and legal holidays; however, the Recorder may close his or her office
on days specified by the county executive (Commissioners) according to the
custom and practice of the county.
(I.C. 36-2-11-4) (Prior Code, § 2-9-3)
§ 31.063 PAYMENT IN ADVANCE.
   The Recorder may demand the recording fees be paid before entering and
recording the instrument.
(I.C. 36-2-11-6) (Prior Code, § 2-9-4)
§ 31.064 RETURN OF RECORDED INSTRUMENT; PROPER RECORDING REQUIRED.
   (A)   Any time that the Recorder has received an instrument for recording,
the Recorder may return it to the person who presented it only after the fee
for recording the instrument has been paid.
(I.C. 36-2-11-7) (Prior Code, § 2-9-5)
   (B)   (1)   The Recorder shall record in books all instruments that are
proper for recording in the order in which they are received in his or her
office for record. Deeds and mortgages shall be recorded in separate books
(I.C. 36-2-11-8 through 36-2-11-12). An entry book shall be kept for this
purpose.
(I.C. 36-2-11-9)
      (2)   The Recorder shall index each volume of instruments by the name of
grantor, promiser, or covenanter in alphabetical order and cross indexed to the
proper grantee, promise, or covenantee. The Recorder shall further keep indexes
of each grantee, promise, or covenantee cross references to the proper grantor,
promisor, or covenantor.
(I.C. 36-2-11-12)
      (3)   The Recorder may record a deed, partition, or conveyance of land
only if it has been endorsed by the Auditor of the proper county as “duly
entered for taxation” or “not taxable.”
       (4)   Exceptions are as follows:
         (a)   The Recorder may receive for recording or filing any instrument
that conveys, creates, encumbers, assigns, or otherwise disposes of an interest
in or lien on property only if the name of the person and governmental agency,
if any, who prepared the instrument is printed, typewritten, stamped, or signed
in a legible manner at the conclusion of the instrument. An instrument complies
with this provision if it is in the following form, “This instrument prepared
by: (Name).”
(I.C. 36-1-11-15)
         (b)   This is not required of an instrument executed before July 1,
1959, or recorded before July 26, 1967, a judgment, order, writ of a court,
will, death certificate, or an instrument executed or acknowledged outside the
state.
(I.C. 36-2-11-15)
      (5)   (a)   The Recorder may record an instrument only if:
            1.   The name of each person who executed the instrument is legibly
printed, typewritten, or stamped immediately beneath his or her signature; or
the signature itself is printed, typewritten, or stamped;
            2.   The name of the notary and each witness is legibly printed,
typewritten, or stamped beneath their signature;
            3.   The name of each person who executes the instrument appears
identically in the body of the instrument, in the acknowledgment or jurat, in
his or her signature, and beneath his or her signature; or
            4.   A proper correcting affidavit made by a person upon personal
knowledge which properly corrects any mistakes or establishes any information
not contained in such other instrument, as required by statute.
(I.C. 36-2-11-16)
         (b)   This division (B)(5) shall not be required of an instrument
executed or acknowledged outside the state.
(I.C. 36-2-11-16(a)(4))
      (6)   All satisfactions, cancellations, and assignments of any kind which
are made on the margin of a record in the Recorder’s office shall be
immediately entered into the entry book, showing the particulars as required by
statute.
(I.C. 36-2-11-18)
      (7)   The recording of affidavits affecting real estate and memorandums
of lease must be made in accordance with the requirements of I.C. 36-2-11-19
and I.C. 36-2-11-20.
(Prior Code, § 2-9-6)
§ 31.065 RECORDING FARM NAMES.
   (A)   An owner of a farm may have the name of his or her farm and a
description of the land to which the name applies recorded in a register kept
for that purpose by the Recorder in the county where the land is situated. The
Recorder, under seal, shall present to the owner a proper certificate setting
forth the name and description of the farm.
   (B)   If the name is recorded as the name of the farm, the name may not be
recorded as the name of another farm in the same county. And, if all of a farm
with such a name is conveyed, the name is not conveyed unless stated in the
deed of conveyance.
   (C)   An owner of a farm may cancel the recorded name of the farm by making
the statement on the margin of the record of the name: “This name is cancelled
and I hereby release all rights thereunder.” The statement must be signed by
the owner and attested to by the Recorder.
(I.C. 36-2-11-17) (Prior Code, § 2-9-7)
§ 31.066 FEDERAL TAX LIENS.
   Notices of federal tax liens and certificates discharging same may be filed
in the Recorder’s office, and the Recorder shall maintain a file labeled
“Federal Tax Lien Notices,” and an index book labeled “Federal Tax Lien Index.”
All entries shall be promptly made.
(I.C. 36-2-11-21) (Prior Code, § 2-9-8)
§ 31.067 CERTAIN BANKRUPTCY MATTERS.
   The Recorder shall record a certified copy of a matter relating to
bankruptcy if federal law requires that the copy be filed in the county in
which lands the bankrupt are located in order to give notice of the bankruptcy.
The Recorder shall record the copy in order to give notice of the bankruptcy.
The Recorder shall record the copy in the miscellaneous records, and shall
index it in the same manner as deeds in the name of the bankrupt as grantor and
in the trustee in bankruptcy or receiver as grantee.
(I.C. 36-2-11-22) (Prior Code, § 2-9-9)
§ 31.068 LIST OF MORTGAGE RELEASES.
   The County Recorder shall, on or before the twentieth day of each month,
furnish the County Auditor a list of the mortgage releases recorded during the
prior month, stating the full name of the mortgagor, the book and page of the
mortgage, the amount being released, and the date of release.
(I.C. 36-2-11-24) (Prior Code, § 2-9-10)
§ 31.069 SEAL.
   The Recorder shall use the official seal when appropriate in attesting an
instrument. Before doing so, the Recorder shall file the impression of the seal
and verified description of that impression in the office of the Clerk of the
Circuit Court for recording into the Circuit Court order book. If the Recorder
has complied with this, then full faith and credit shall be given to such seal
without further attestation.
(I.C. 36-2-11-23) (Prior Code, § 2-9-11)
§ 31.070 RECORDER TO RETAIN DOCUMENTS DURING MICROFILM DEVELOPMENT.
   (A)   Whenever the Recorder makes microfilm copies of any instrument, the
Recorder shall keep that instrument and not release it to any person or persons
until the microfilm has been returned from the developers and the clearness of
the images viewed.
   (B)   If the images are not clear, the Recorder shall re-microfilm such
instruments.
   (C)   After a clear microfilm is obtained, then and only then shall the
Recorder release such instruments to the person whose name and address is
clearly typed or printed upon such document.
(Prior Code, § 2-9-12)
**** COUNTY SURVEYOR ****
§ 31.085 TERM OF OFFICE.
   The term of office of the County Surveyor, elected under Article 6, Section
2 of the Constitution of Indiana, is four years and continues until their
successor is elected and qualified.
(I.C. 36-2-12-2) (Prior Code, § 2-11-1)
§ 31.086 OFFICE HOURS.
   The County Commissioners shall furnish the County Surveyor an office in the
county seat, which shall remain open for business during regular business hours
on every day of the year, except Sundays and legal holidays, providing he or
she may close his or her office on days specified by the county executive
(County Commissioners) according to the custom and practice of the county.
(I.C. 36-2-12-3) (Prior Code, § 2-11-2)
§ 31.087 SUPPLIES, EQUIPMENT FURNISHED.
   (A)   The county executive (County Commissioners) shall provide the Surveyor
with all necessary supplied and equipment, including office supplies or the
type furnished to other offices; and surveying instruments and materials
necessary for the discharge of their duties.
   (B)   All such supplies remain the property of the county.
(I.C. 36-2-12-3) (Prior Code, § 2-11-3)
§ 31.088 SEAL.
   The Surveyor may procure and use an official seal.
(I.C. 36-2-12-5) (Prior Code, § 2-11-4)
§ 31.089 CERTAIN POWERS.
   (A)   A Surveyor may:
      (1)   Administer oaths necessary to the discharge of his or her duties;
      (2)   Administer and certify any oaths required to be taken by a
commissioner for the partition of real property, or a Commissioner or other
viewer to view, mark, locate, or relocate a public highway; and
      (3)   He or she may further take, and certify with his or her seal and
signature, acknowledgments of mortgages and deed for the conveyance of real
property.
(I.C. 36-2-12-6)
   (B)   The Surveyor shall keep and maintain a corner record book as required
by law and shall check and reference at least 5% of all corners shown in the
corner record book.
(Prior Code, § 2-11-5)
§ 31.090 ENTRY ON LANDS OF OTHERS.
   While doing his or her work as required by statute, a Surveyor is not
considered a trespasser and is liable only for the actual damages he or she may
cause to the property.
(I.C. 36-2-12-12) (Prior Code, § 2-11-6)
§ 31.091 POWERS, DUTIES.
   This list is not an exhaustive listing of the powers or duties of the County
Surveyor which are set out by case law and statute, but rather is meant to
serve the purpose of pointing out some of the more obvious areas.
(Prior Code, § 2-11-7)
§ 31.092 CHARGES TO COUNTY.
   There is no statutory schedule of fees legally chargeable by a County
Surveyor for locating corners. He or she may not charge the usual fees
chargeable by professional engineers and surveyors for a legal survey. (See
1957 Ind. Attorney General, No. 38.)
(Prior Code, § 2-11-8)
§ 31.093 OUTSIDE INCOME.
   A County Surveyor may, if he or she is a registered engineer or surveyor,
accept contracts in his or her individual capacity and charge for his or her
services, provided that there is no breach of, or conflict with, his or her
official duty, or violations of public policy statutes. He or she is not
required to turn over to the county fees which he or she earn as a private
surveyor during his or her tenure as County Surveyor. (See 1957 Op. Att. Gen.
38.)
(Prior Code, § 2-11-9)
**** COUNTY TREASURER ****
§ 31.105 TERMS OF OFFICE.
   The term of the office of County Treasurer elected under Article 6, Section
2 of the Constitution of Indiana is four years and continues until his or her
successor is elected and qualified.
(I.C. 36-2-10-2) (Prior Code, § 2-12-1)
§ 31.106 REMOVAL FROM OFFICE.
   The County Commissioners may remove the Treasurer from office if he or she
is delinquent and has been sued upon his or her official bond.
(I.C. 36-2-10-3) (Prior Code, § 2-12-2)
§ 31.107 OFFICE HOURS.
   The Treasurer shall keep the office open for business during regular
business hours on every day of the year except Sundays and legal holidays.
However, he or she may close his or her office on days specified by the County
Commissioners (executive) according to the custom and practice of the county.
(I.C. 36-2-10-4) (Prior Code, § 2-12-3)
§ 31.108 OATHS.
   The Treasurer may administer all oaths necessary in the discharge of the
duties of his or her office.
(I.C. 36-2-10-6) (Prior Code, § 2-12-4)
§ 31.109 RECEIPTS AND DISBURSEMENTS.
   The Treasurer shall receive monies to which the county is entitled and shall
disburse it on warrants which are issued and attested to by the County Auditor
(I.C. 36-2-10-9). He or she shall further issue receipts to each person from
whom he or she receives money.
(I.C. 36-2-10-7) (Prior Code, § 2-12-5)
§ 31.110 INSPECTION BY COMMISSIONERS.
   The records and office of the County Treasurer may be inspected by the
county executive (County Commissioners) at any time.
(I.C. 36-2-10-7) (Prior Code, § 2-12-6)
§ 31.111 WARRANTS.
   County warrants shall be recorded in the order in which they are presented
(I.C. 36-2-10-13) and separate accounts shall be kept on any interest that is
paid.
(I.C. 36-2-10-12) (Prior Code, § 2-12-7)
§ 31.112 MAINTENANCE OF CERTAIN ACCOUNTS.
   The Treasurer shall maintain a separate account for receipts for
expenditures from each specific county fund or appropriation and a general
account of all county receipts and expenditures.
(Prior Code, § 2-12-8)
§ 31.113 MONTHLY FINANCIAL REPORTS.
   Before the sixth day of each month, the Treasurer shall prepare a report
(original and three copies) showing certain items as required by I.C. 36-2-10-
16, upon such forms as furnished by the State Board of Accounts. Moreover, the
Treasurer shall keep the original document and file the three copies with the
County Auditor.
(Prior Code, § 2-12-9)
§ 31.114 ANNUAL, SEMI-ANNUAL SETTLEMENT.
   The statute refers to an “annual settlement” in its heading and then states
that the Treasurer shall make a “semi-annual settlement” with the County
Auditor under I.C. 6-1.1-27-2. There is a semi-annual settlement which is done
twice each year.
(Prior Code, § 2-12-10)
§ 31.115 DESIGNATION OF BANKS.
   The Treasurer may designate a national or state bank in the county as his or
her agent for collecting taxes that are not delinquent.
(I.C. 36-2-10-19) (Prior Code, § 2-12-11)
§ 31.116 UNCLAIMED PROPERTY.
   (A)   (1)   Within one year after the Treasurer receives monies from the
County Coroner under I.C. 36-2-14-11(b) the Treasurer shall deliver it to any
person legally entitled to it or may retain so much of it as is needed to pay
the expenses of the Coroner’s investigation and funeral of the deceased, and
shall sell all property in an execution sale.
      (2)   Moreover, if money is found upon a body is not claimed within one
year from the date the Treasurer receives said monies, he or she shall credit
it to the Common School Fund.
(I.C. 36-2-10-21)
   (B)   If the County Coroner finds money or other property on a body and does
not deliver it to the Treasurer as required by division (A) above, the
Treasurer shall, in his or her own name, bring a civil action against the
Coroner to collect it.
(I.C. 36-2-10-22) (Prior Code, § 2-12-12)
§ 31.117 TAX SALES.
   (A)   The Treasurer shall be present during all tax sales of personal or
real property, and all sales of surplus property held by the county. During
which sales, the Treasurer shall collect monies and give receipts from and to
successful bidders, recording the name and address of any person paying money
to such Treasurer.
   (B)   The Treasurer shall work in conjunction with the Auditor who shall, by
statute, serve as clerk of such sale.
(I.C. 6-1.1-24-5) (Prior Code, § 2-12-13)
**** AUDITOR ****
§ 31.130 FISCAL OFFICER.
   The Auditor is the fiscal officer of the county.
(I.C. 36-2-9-2) (Prior Code, § 2-13-1)
§ 31.131 TERMS OF OFFICE.
   The term of the Auditor is for four years and continues until his or her
successor is elected and qualified. He or she shall be elected under Article 6,
Section 2 of the Indiana Constitution.
(Prior Code, § 2-13-2)
§ 31.132 OFFICE HOURS.
   (A)   The Auditor shall keep the office of the Auditor open during the hours
between 8:30 a.m. and 4:30 p.m. on weekdays (Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday,
Thursday, and Friday) each week, and shall close the same on weekends (Saturday
and Sunday) and such official holidays as are determined by the County
Commissioners.
(I.C. 36-2-9-3)
   (B)   Any legal action required to be taken on any date that the Auditor’s
office is closed under §
70.03(A) will be timely taken if taken on the next day that such office is
open.
(Prior Code, § 2-13-3)
§ 31.133 DUTIES.
   (A)   The Auditor shall replace worn maps and plats which are located in his
or her office and which are so worn or defaced that the same are not fit for
use, and shall make an accurate copy of the legible part thereof, and retain
same the records of said Auditor. If a part of the map of plat is illegible,
the Auditor shall resort to the most accurate sources to complete such map or
plat.
(I.C. 36-8, I.C. 36-2-9-5)
   (B)   The Auditor shall further furnish standard forms for use in the
transaction of business under this section and for use in the performance of
services for which he or she receives a specific fee.
(I.C. 36-2-9-6)
   (C)   The Auditor shall perform the duties of the Clerk of the County
Commissioners (county executive) under the provisions of I.C. 36-2-2-11. If the
Auditor cannot perform those duties during a meeting of the County
Commissioners, and he or she does not have a deputy or his or her deputy cannot
attend, then the county executive may deputize a person to perform those duties
during the meeting and proceed with their meeting.
(I.C. 36-2-9-7)
   (D)   The Auditor shall further perform the duties as the Clerk of the
fiscal body (County Council), and keeper of its records (I.C. 36-2-9-8, I.C.
36-2-3-6). The Auditor shall preserve the fiscal body’s proceedings, recording
the “ayes” and “nays” on each vote appropriating money or fixing the rate of a
tax levy, and on all other matters.
   (E)   The Auditor may administer oaths in the performance of his or her
duties, the oath of office to an officer who receives his or her certificate of
appointment or election from the Auditor, and may take acknowledgments of deeds
and mortgages executed for the security of any trust funds he or she is
required to lend.
(I.C. 36-2-9-9)
   (F)   The Auditor, in the name of the state and on behalf of a county fund,
may sue principals or sureties on any obligation, whether the obligation is in
the name of the state or another person.
   (G)   The Auditor shall file the original of the County Treasurer’s monthly
report, under I.C. 36-2-10-16, with the records of the County Board of Finance.
Subsequently, the Auditor shall then present one copy to the County
Commissioners at their next regular meeting, and immediately transmit one copy
to the State Board of Account (I.C. 36-2-9-11). The Auditor shall further keep
accurate account with the County Treasurer. Whenever a receipt is given by the
Treasurer for money paid into the county treasury is deposited with the
Auditor, the Auditor shall:
      (1)   File the Treasurer’s receipt;
      (2)   Charge the Treasurer with the amount of the Treasurer’s receipt;
and
      (3)   Issue his or her own receipt to the person presenting the
Treasurer’s receipt.
(I.C. 36-2-9-11)
   (H)   The Auditor shall keep a separate account for each item of
appropriation made by the county fiscal body (County Council), and in each
warrant he or she draws on the county treasury, the Auditor shall specifically
indicate which item of appropriation the warrant is drawn against. The Auditor
may not permit an item of appropriation to be overdrawn or to be drawn down on
for a purpose other than the specific purpose for which it was made.
   (I)   The issuance of warrants for payment of money from the county treasury
and the penalty for violations; the examination and settlement of accounts and
demands chargeable to the county; the issuance of warrants to satisfy judgments
against the county and the calls for redemption of outstanding county warrants;
and the endorsement of instruments affecting real property title prior to
recording are provided for by statute.
(I.C. 36-2-9-14)
   (J)   The Auditor shall use permanent jet-black ink in the preparation of
all official records.
(I.C. 36-2-17-2)
   (K)   The Auditor shall keep a permanent record of all ordinances passed by
those bodies for whom he or she serves as Clerk (County Commissioner and County
Council).
 
   (L)   (1)   The Auditor shall, on behalf of the county, serve in the
capacity as Records Access Officer with regard to any request made of the
county for access to or release of public records of the county. They shall
prepare, and have available, forms for any person seeking such access or
release to fill out, sign, and present to them for approval.
      (2)   The Auditor shall determine if any of the information has been
exempted from disclosure (unless there is a court order or access to the
records is specifically required by state statute, federal statute, or court
order).
      (3)   Further, should copies be requested upon such form of any such
information which has not been exempted, he or she shall cause same to be made
within a reasonable time and he or she shall collect the fees as provided for
such service.
(I.C. 5-14-3-1)
   (M)   The Auditor shall serve as clerk of such tax sales.
(I.C. 6-1.1-24-5) (Prior Code, § 2-13-4) (Ord. 1984-1, passed 1-11-1984)
§ 31.134 GRIEVANCE OFFICER.
   (A)   (1)   The grievance procedure is established to meet the requirements
of the Revenue Sharing Act. It should be used by any individual who wishes to
file a complaint alleging discrimination on the basis of handicap in employment
practices and policies or the provision of programs, services, and benefits by
the county.
      (2)   The grievance should be in writing and contain as much information
as possible about the alleged discrimination (for example, supply the name,
address, phone number, location, and description of the problem, and the like).
Other arrangements for submission of a grievance, such as a personal interview
or tape recording, will be made available for the visually impaired or those
with motor impairments. It should be submitted by the person making such
grievance, or his or her designee, within 30 calendar days of the alleged
violation to the County Auditor, Lawrence County, Bedford, Indiana 47421, phone
number (812)-275-3111. Office hours are 8:30 a.m. through 4:30 p.m., Monday
through Friday, and closed Saturday and Sunday, and holidays.
      (3)   Within 15 working days after the receipt of the complaint, the
Auditor, as program coordinator, will respond in writing, or by a method
understood to the complainant, to the complainant and/or his or her designee.
The response will offer a resolution or explain the position of the county with
respect to the complaint.
      (4)   If the response by the Auditor does not satisfactorily resolve the
issue, the complainant and/or his or her designee may request a hearing, to be
held within 15 working days of receipt of the response, before the Board of
Commissioners of the county, which is the Handicapped Coordinating Committee,
for resolution (Courthouse, Bedford, Indiana, phone number (812)-275-3111.
Office hours are 8:30 a.m. through 4:30 p.m., Monday through Friday, and closed
Saturday and Sunday, and holidays).
      (5)   Within 30 calendar days of the hearing, the final resolution in
writing (or by method understood be the complainant) shall be delivered to the
complainant and/or his or her designee by the Board of Commissioners of the
county. Service shall be deemed to have been made if mailed to the address
shown on the written grievance of such person by certified mail, return receipt
requested (unless the county has received written notice of a change of address
of such person). Any and all persons making such complaints to the county must
keep the Board of Commissioners informed or any and all address changes.
      (6)   All complaints received by the Auditor and all responses from the
Auditor or the Board of Commissioners shall be kept in the files of the county
for a minimum of three years, and these documents may be requested by the
office of Revenue Sharing should an investigation into alleged discrimination
on the basis of handicapped status be initiated.
   (B)   The Auditor, and any of his or her duly appointed deputies, shall act
for and on behalf of the county as the officer in charge of dispersing special
grievance forms which shall request in writing the information set forth in
section 5-5-1 above.
   (C)   The Auditor shall then promptly submit the same to the Board of
Commissioners for action at its next regularly scheduled meeting; or if none,
then to said Commissioners within not less than 30 days at a special meeting.
   (D)   The Auditor, or any of his or her duly appointed deputies or
employees, shall further aid any and all persons who might come to his or her
office for assistance because they are handicapped because of an inability to
speak English. The Auditor shall do the following.
      (1)   First, the Auditor shall make every effort to determine the
language of the person being spoken. There are some foreign language teachers
at the high school, as well as other persons living in the community who are
bilingual or multilingual. Every effort shall be made to determine the language
being spoken by the person seeking the help.
      (2)   The use of people in the community who can speak that language and
interpret for that person shall be made as so to translate the person’s wishes.
Then the Auditor, as Compliance Officer hereunder, shall do all that his or her
office can to answer the questions of such person, provided that such questions
pertain to that person’s business with the county and is not just personal
business unrelated to the county.
      (3)   Arrangements have been made with the Indiana University Foreign
Language Department, through the various individual schools, for such
department to furnish an interpreter as the need may arise. Fees and mileage
have already been discussed and agreed upon in advance, so as not to be a time-
consuming problem in getting an interpreter for such person. The University has
promised to send an appropriate person to the county for the purposes of
interpreting if the same cannot be done over the phone. The Auditor is
authorized to enter into such agreements, on behalf of the Board of
Commissioners, as the need arises, so as to give prompt service to such a
handicapped person. The County Commissioners shall pay all fee and mileage for
such interpreters.
(Prior Code, § 2-13-5)

§ 31.135 DRAFTS.
   (A)   The Auditor of the county is hereby authorized to issue drafts from
time to time for payment of utility bills incurred by the county without
further authorization.
   (B)   The Auditor of the county is hereby authorized to issue drafts from
time to time for the payment of mailing costs to the U.S. Postmaster and
postage for each department as needed without further authorization.
   (C)   The Auditor of the county is hereby authorized to issue drafts from
time to time for payment of group health, hospitalization, dental, vision,
cancer, and life insurance, worker’s compensation, unemployment, sales and
gross income taxes, retirement, and all payroll deductions including electronic
transfer without further authorization.
   (D)   The Auditor of the county is hereby authorized to issue drafts from
time to time for the payment of employees of the county which said payments
constitute the county payroll without further authorization.
   (E)   The Auditor of the county is hereby authorized to issue drafts from
time to time for the payment of invoices presented to the county for materials,
equipment, goods, and services previously authorized by the Board of
Commissioners by accepting a valid bid, quote, or contract for such material,
equipment, goods and services without further authorization.
   (F)   The Auditor of the county is hereby authorized to issue drafts from
time to time for the registration fees and expenses for schooling and
conferences incurred by the persons who have permission to attend.
   (G)   The Auditor of the county is hereby authorized to issue drafts from
time to time for registration fee for vehicles, titles, and any excise tax that
may be due.
   (H)   The Auditor of the county is hereby authorized to issue a draft
payable, if required, to the Recorder of the county to pay for recordings
without first filing a claim for such payments.
   (I)   The Auditor of the county is hereby authorized to make disbursements
and payments to departments or vendors of departments of the county from grant
funds previously received and approved by the County Board of Commissioners,
this to include all county departments and the Board of Aviation Commissioners
of the county without further authorization.
   (J)   The Auditor of the county is hereby authorized to issue drafts from
time to time for the payments of expenses that must be paid because of
emergency circumstances.
(Ord. 2005-11, passed 8-9-2005)
§ 31.136 INTER-FUND TRANSFERS.
   (A)   The County Auditor be and hereby is granted all necessary authority to
make inter-fund transfers in order to transfer balances from one fund to
another in order to facilitate the claims process.
   (B)   The County Auditor shall keep records on any such transfers that may
occur and report to the County Council all appropriate information regarding
inter-fund transfers.
(Council Res. 2007-02, passed 1-30-2007)
**** BOARDS, COMMISSIONS ****
§ 31.150 HOSPITAL BOARD; ASSOCIATION; AUTHORITY.
   (A)   Appointment by the Commissioners. The County Hospital (Dunn Memorial
Hospital Board) shall consist of three Commissioners to serve as members of an
11-member Board, and it shall appoint either two or three other Board members,
depending on whether the hospital was acquired and equipped with the aid of a
hospital association provided under I.C. 16-22-2, or without the aid of such a
hospital association.
(I.C. 16-22-2) (Prior Code, § 2-7-1)
   (B)   Appointment by the Council.
      (1)   The County Hospital Governing Board (Dunn Memorial Hospital Board)
shall consist of either two or five members that are appointed by the County
Council, depending on whether or not the County Hospital was equipped without
the aid of a hospital association provided under I.C. 16-12-20 (in which case,
the County Council shall appoint five members).
      (2)   In the case in which the County Hospital was equipped with the aid
of a hospital association, the number of County Council appointments to the
Board is reduced to two. The members of this 11-person board shall have two-
year staggered terms (see I.C. 16-22-2).
(Prior Code, § 2-7-2)
   (C)   County Hospital Association. Pursuant to I.C. 16-22-6-2, the Board
hereby establishes, reestablishes, and/or re-constitutes, as appropriate, the
County Hospital Association (the Association), a body corporate and politic, to
exercise the powers set forth in the Act (I.C. 16-22-6 et seq.),or otherwise
granted by law. Pursuant to the Act, the Association shall be governed by a
board of directors, consisting of five members, to be appointed by the Board.
   (D)   Hospital Authority. There is hereby created a Hospital Authority of
the county which shall be a body corporate and politic for the purpose of
financing, acquiring, constructing, equipping, and leasing one or more projects
(as defined in I.C. 5-1-4) to participating hospitals located in the county and
to have all the powers now or hereafter granted to an authority under the Act.
(Prior Code, § 8-18-1)
(Res. 2009-02, passed 2-17-2009)
§ 31.151 BOARD OF FINANCE.
   (A)   The Board of Commissioners serve as “ex-officio” members.
   (B)   This Board is responsible for the supervision of all public funds of
the county, including safekeeping and deposit.
(I.C. 5-12-1-7) (Prior Code, § 2-7-1)
§ 31.152 BOARD OF REVIEW.
   A five-member Board made up of the County Assessor, ex-officio, County
Auditor, ex-officio, County Treasurer, ex-officio, and two freeholders
appointed by the Board of Commissioners, which appointees must reside in the
county and must have different political parties.
(I.C. 6-1.1-28-1) (Prior Code, § 2-7-1)
§ 31.153 ALCOHOLIC BEVERAGE BOARD.
   (A)   Appointment by the Commissioners. A three-member Board with one
appointee to be made by the Board of Commissioners, another by the County
Council, and another by the mayor of the largest city in the county. Not more
than two members shall have the same political party.
(I.C. 7.1-2-4-6) (Prior Code, § 2-7-1)
   (B)   Appointment by the Council. There shall be four members of the County
Alcoholic Beverage Board. One member shall be appointed by the County Council.
There are specific requirements as to the qualifications and there is a
limitation on the number of appointees who may be of the same political party.
(I.C. 7.1-2-4-6) (Prior Code, § 2-7-2)
§ 31.154 BOARD OF AVIATION COMMISSIONERS.
   (A)   Appointment by the Commissioners. Four of the seven members shall be
appointed by the Board of Commissioners; two members shall be appointed by the
mayor of the largest city in the county, and one member shall be appointed by
the County Council.
(Prior Code, § 2-7-1)
   (B)   Appointment by the Council. One member of the seven-member Board is to
be appointed by the County Council. There are specific restrictions as to the
number of persons belonging to the same political party. The members of this
Board may not hold elective office.
(I.C. 8-22-2-1) (Prior Code, § 2-7-2)
   (C)   Increase of members.
      (1)   The County Board of Aviation Commissioners shall hereafter consist
of five members as provided for by I.C. 8-22-2-1(g). Not more than three
members may be of the same political party.
      (2)   (a)   Following passage of this division (C), the County Board of
Commissioners shall take up the matter and appoint a fifth member of the County
Board of Aviation Commissioners pursuant to I.C. 8-22-2-1(b).
         (b)   The Board of County Commissioners shall undertake this as soon
as practicable during a regular meeting of said Board.
         (c)   The Board of County Commissioners may submit and consider
recommendations from the Board of Aviation Commissioners in making its
appointment of the fifth member to the Board of Aviation Commissioners.
(Council Res. 2018-03, passed 4-24-2018)
§ 31.155 BOARD OF PUBLIC WELFARE.
   (A)   There is established a County Board of Public Welfare by I.C. 12-1-3-
1. However, this Board is under the control the State Department of Public
Welfare (I.C. 12-1-3-3).
   (B)   The county has no control over such Department.
(Prior Code, § 2-7-1-10)
§ 31.156 BOARD OF HEALTH.
   Reference is made to I.C. 16-1-5-1 and 16-1-7-10 for alternative methods of
appointment to the Board of Health, with the choice as to which being
determined by certain other criteria set forth in the statute.
(Prior Code, § 2-7-1-11)
§ 31.157 BOARD OF PUBLIC RECORDS.
   A seven-member Board made up of the Judge of Circuit Court, President of the
Board of Commissioners, County Auditor, County Recorder, Clerk of Circuit
Court, Superintendent of Schools of the school district that includes the
county seat, and Clerk-Treasurer of the county seat (Bedford).
(I.C. 5-15-6-1) (Prior Code, § 2-7-1-13)
§ 31.158 BOARD OF TAX ADJUSTMENTS.
   (A)   Appointment by the Commissioners. The seven-member Board consists of
four freeholders appointed by the County Commissioners, one person appointed by
the County Council, one member appointed by the mayor of the largest city in
the county (now Bedford), and one appointee selected by the school board of the
school corporation with the largest taxable valuation (North Lawrence, at
present).
(I.C. 6-1.1-29-1) (Prior Code, § 2-7-1)
   (B)   Appointment by the Council. A County Council member shall serve as one
member on this seven-member Board.
(I.C. 6-1.1-29-1) (Prior Code, § 2-7-2)
§ 31.159 LIBRARY BOARD; CONTRACTUAL LIBRARY BOARD.
   (A)   County Library Board.
      (1)   Appointment by the Commissioners. Currently, the law on this point
is changing and nothing definite can be said other than the possibility of one
or more appointments by the Board of Commissioners. Pub. Law 127-12984 (non-
code), and I.C. 20-12-2.5-4 and I.C. 20-14-2.5-11 currently provides for at
least one appointment by the County Commissioners and one appointment
determined by the County Council. New legislation may limit the number or time
of appointment.
(Prior Code, § 2-7-1-17)
      (2)   Appointment by the Council. This Board shall consist of four
members, two of which are appointed by the county fiscal body (County Council)
and two are appointed by the County Auditor, if there is no County
Superintendent of Schools.
(I.C. 20-12-6-5, as amended by I.C. 20-12-2.5-11) (Prior Code, § 2-7-2-5)
   (C)   County Contractual Library Board. Current state law states that four
members of County Contractual Library Board, required by I.C. 20-14-6-5, shall
be appointed as follows:
      (1)   Two members appointed by the executive of the county (County
Commissioners) in which the County Contractual Library District is located.
      (2)   Two members appointed by the County Superintendent of Schools, or
if there is no county Superintendent, by the County Auditor of the county in
which the Library District is located.
(Prior Code, § 8-14-1)
§ 31.160 COMMUNITY CORRECTIONS ADVISORY BOARD.
   (A)   Pursuant to I.C. 11-12-2-2, the County Community Corrections Advisory
Board is hereby created (subject to the conditions of division (B)(6) below),
the membership consisting of the following:
      (1)   The County Sheriff;
      (2)   The Prosecuting Attorney;
      (3)   The Director of the County Welfare Department;
      (4)   The mayor of the most populous municipality in the county (at this
point in time, this would be the City of Bedford);
      (5)   One judge having criminal jurisdiction, appointed by the Judge of
the Circuit Court;
      (6)   One judge having juvenile jurisdiction, appointed by the Judge of
the Circuit Court;
      (7)   One attorney with substantial criminal defense practice, appointed
by the Judge of the Circuit Court; and
      (8)   The following members, appointed by the County Board of
Commissioners:
         (a)   One probation officer;
         (b)   One educational administrator;
         (c)   One representative of a private correctional agency if such an
agency exists in the county;
         (d)   One mental health administrator, or if there is none available
in the county, one psychiatrist, psychologist, or physician;
         (e)   One ex-offender, if available; and
         (f)   Four lay persons, at least one of whom must be a member of a
minority race if a racial minority resides in the county and a member of that
minority is willing to serve.
   (B)   (1)   Members of the Advisory Board appointed by the County
Commissioners shall be appointed for a term of four years; the criminal defense
attorney shall likewise be appointed for a term of four years. Other members
serve only while [MISSING TEXT]
         [MISSING TEXT] purposes of transacting business, a majority of the
membership constitutes a quorum. A vacancy in the membership does not impair
the right of a quorum to transact business.
      (4)   The Secretary shall keep a record of the date of the appointment
and expiration of all members, and shall furnish such list to the County
Commissioners in the month of December of each years, commencing December 1991.
      (5)   The Board of Commissioners and County Council shall provide
necessary assistance and appropriations to the Community Corrections Advisory
Board established for the county.
      (6)   The creation of the Community Corrections Advisory Board is
conditioned upon the County Commissioners, at a later time and by majority
vote, being able to also dissolve said Community Corrections Advisory Board.
This section would not be adopted if it were not for this provision being
included. Should this section ever be judicially determined to not permit the
County Commissioners to have such power, then this section shall automatically
come to an end and the County Community Corrections Advisory Board shall
automatically cease to exist at that time.
(Res. passed 12-18-1990)

§ 31.162 FAIRGROUNDS PRESERVATION COMMITTEE.
   (A)   The County Commissioners hereby establish the County Fairground
Preservation Committee, the same being considered a department established and
operated out of the Commissioners’ budget.
   (B)   Said Committee shall have regular members appointed by the constituent
members as follows:
      (1)   The County Commissioners: one member;
      (2)   The County Council: one member; and
      (3)   The County 4-H Clubs and Fair Association: one member.
   (C)   (1)   The term of each member shall be from January 1 through December
31 for each calendar year of the operation of the Committee.
      (2)   If the appointment is made later, then the appointment shall be
effective on the date of appointment by the constituent body. Each position
must be appointed or reappointed every year.
   (D)   The primary purpose of the Committee shall be to identify and
undertake projects that further the aims of restoration, preservation,
maintenance, or improvement of the physical plant and facilities at the County
Fairgrounds.
   (E)   Funding for this Committee shall come from the County Council. All
budgets and appropriations shall be subject to the regular county funding
processes followed by the county government each year.
   (F)   Funding for any particular project recommended by the Committee shall
be subject to approval by the Commissioners and allocation of funds by the
Council. The Auditor of the county shall establish appropriate budget lines for
the Committee.
   (G)   In that all of the projects contemplated hereby shall be pursued on
the real property of the County 4-H Clubs and Fair Association, the Committee
shall have the full authority to make and execute an interlocal agency
agreement, or any other written instrument needed to implement an approved
project.
   (H)   In that this Committee is being formed by the County Commissioners,
the Committee shall conduct its meetings and business, and make expenditures in
compliance with all applicable state laws as it relates to county governmental
entities.
   (I)   The County Fairgrounds Preservation Committee shall meet at least one
time per quarter in any given calendar year in which it is in existence and is
engaged in its work, or more often if the need so requires.
(Ord. 2021-11, passed 11-23-2021)
§ 31.163 911 ADVISORY BOARD.
   The County Commissioners approves the establishment of the County 911
Advisory Board (hereinafter referred to as the Advisory Board) in order to
develop policy and advice for the effective and efficient operation of the
County 911 system. The Board shall operate under the following guidelines.
   (A)   Service to the public shall be the guiding principle in all of the
Advisory Board’s endeavors, as it seeks to improve 911 operations by
establishing and maintaining practical and measurable operational standards for
dispatch services.
   (B)   The Advisory Board shall meet quarterly or at any time there is
pressing issues that must be addressed before the next scheduled quarterly
meeting.
   (C)   The Advisory Board shall consist of seven members as follows:
      (1)   The County Sheriff;
      (2)   A County Commissioner;
      (3)   The County Emergency Management;
      (4)   The City of Mitchell representative appointed by the Mayor of
Mitchell;
      (5)   The Chief of a County Rural Fire Department; and
      (6)   A emergency medical services member serving the county.
(Res. 2019-03, passed 7-9-2019)
§ 31.164 REDEVELOPMENT COMMISSION.
   (A)   (1)   There is hereby created a Board to be known as the County
Redevelopment Commission. All members of the County Redevelopment Commission
shall be appointed as provided for by statute. Each Redevelopment Commissioner
shall serve for one year from the first day of January after his or her
appointment and until his or her successor is appointed and has qualified,
except that the original Commissioners shall serve from the date of their
appointment until the first day of January in the second year after their
appointment. If a vacancy occurs, a successor shall be appointed in the same
manner as the original Commissioner, and the successor shall serve for the
remainder of the vacated term.
      (2)   Each Redevelopment Commissioner, before beginning his or her
duties, shall take and subscribe an oath of office in the form prescribed by
law, to be endorsed on the certificate of his or her appointment, which shall
be promptly filed with the Auditor of the county.
      (3)   Each Redevelopment Commissioner, before beginning his or her
duties, shall execute a bond payable to the state, with surety to be approved
by the Commissioners. The bond must be in a penal sum of $15,000 and must be
conditioned on the faithful performance of the duties of his or her office and
the accounting for all monies and property that may come into his or her hands
or under his or her control. The cost of the bond shall be paid by the
Redevelopment District.
   (B)   Such Commissioners shall have the qualifications prescribed by the
laws of the state as from time to time amended and shall qualify as therein
provided; and shall exercise and enjoy the rights and powers and assume the
duties and obligations conferred and imposed by said Act (see I.C. 36-7-14),
including, but not limited to, the following qualifications.
      (1)   A Redevelopment Commissioner must be at least 18 years of age and
must be a resident of the county. If a Commissioner ceases to be qualified
under this division (B), he or she forfeits his or her office.
      (2)   No Redevelopment Commissioner of the county shall receive a salary,
but such Redevelopment Commissioners are entitled to reimbursement for expenses
necessarily incurred in the performance of their duties.
      (3)   A Redevelopment Commissioner may not have a pecuniary interest in
any contract, employment, purchase, or sale made under the provisions of this
section and the Act.
      (4)   However, any property required for redevelopment purposes in which
a Commissioner has a pecuniary interest may be acquired, but only by gift or
condemnation. A transaction made in violation of this division (B)(4) is void.
(Ord. 2021-5, passed 4-13-2021)
Cross-reference:
   Department of Redevelopment, §
31.180
§ 31.165 PUBLIC DEFENDER BOARD.
   (A)   The County Public Defender Board is hereby established for the purpose
of providing legal representation to indigent defendants/respondents in
criminal, juvenile, probation violation, extradition, child support, civil
commitments, and other proceedings where the right to counsel has been
established by law.
   (B)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      BOARD. The County Public Defender Board created by this section.
      INDIGENT DEFENDANT/RESPONDENT. A person who requests legal representation
and is determined by the Court to be entitled to legal representation at public
expense.
      LEGAL REPRESENTATION.
         (a)   The services of an attorney provided to a defendant/respondent
in a matter originating in a state court in the county involving:
            1.   A person charged with a crime as defined in I.C. 35-31.5-2-75;
            2.   An act of delinquency as defined in I.C. 31-37-1-2;
            3.   A violation of a condition of probation established as a part
of a sentence in a juvenile or criminal matter;
            4.   Detention of a person subject to extradition to another
jurisdiction;
            5.   Proceedings to collect unpaid child support pursuant to I.C.
31-16-17-12;
            6.   Civil commitment and contempt proceedings; or
            7.   Other proceedings where the right to counsel at public expense
has been established by law.
      (b)   The term LEGAL REPRESENTATION includes services in connection with
all pre-trial, trial, and appellate proceedings in which an indigent defendant/
respondent has a right to counsel.
   (C)   (1)   The Board shall consist of three members: one member appointed
by the County Commissioners, and two members from different political parties
appointed by majority vote of the judges who exercise felony or juvenile
jurisdiction.
      (2)   (a)   The initial term of the member appointed by the County
Commissioners shall expire on ___________ 2011. The initial term of one member
appointed by the judges shall expire on ___________ 2012, and the initial term
of the other member appointed by the judges shall expire on ___________ 2013.
         (b)   After the initial term of each member, appointments shall be for
three-year terms. Members of the Board shall serve until their successor is
appointed. An appointment to fill a vacancy shall be made by the authority
appointing the member vacating the position and shall be for the remainder of
the unexpired term.
      (3)   The following persons shall be ineligible to serve as members of
the Board: a city, town, or county attorney, a law enforcement officer, a
judge, or a court employee.
      (4)   Board members shall serve without pay but may receive reimbursement
for expenses incurred in connection with the member’s duties if approved by the
Board.
      (5)   Two members of the Board shall constitute a quorum for the purpose
of conducting the business of the Board. Decisions of the Board shall be
approved by a majority of the members present.
      (6)   The Board shall meet at least quarterly or upon call of its
Chairperson or any two members of the Board.
      (7)   The Board shall elect its Chairperson by a majority vote of the
Board.
   (D)   The Board shall have the following powers and duties:
         (1)   (a)   Prepare a comprehensive plan for providing legal
representation to indigent defendants/respondents in the county in accordance
with I.C. 33-40-7-5.
            (b)   The comprehensive plan shall, at a minimum, provide for:
               1.   Legal representation to an indigent defendant/respondent at
the earliest possible point in time;
               2.   Legal representation to an indigent defendant/respondent by
the same attorney or attorneys through the pending of the matter to the
greatest extent possible; and
               3.   Professional development, continuing legal education, and
malpractice coverage for public defenders.
      (2)   Establish policies and procedure for the provision of competent
legal representation for indigent defendants/respondents in criminal, juvenile,
probation violation, extradition, child support, and criminal contempt, and
other matters pursuant to the comprehensive plan;
      (3)   Establish guidelines and procedures for the determination of
indigency and for the appropriate reimbursement for legal representation
provided at public expense in accordance with I.C. 33-40-7-11;
      (4)   Recommend an annual operating budget for the Agency and monitor the
expenditures of funds; and
      (5)   Prepare and submit to the County Council and the general public an
annual report on the operation of the Agency.
   (E)   Nothing contained herein shall be deemed to abridge the authority of
any judge of a state court of this county from appointing counsel for any
person entitled thereto under the Constitution of the United States or the
Constitution of the state.
   (F)   Nothing contained herein shall be deemed to create a right of
reimbursement pursuant to I.C. 33-40-6, except to the extent that any claims
for reimbursement comply with I.C. 33-40-6 and the standards of the State
Public Defender Commission.
(Ord. 04, passed 8-3-2010)
**** DEPARTMENTS ****
§ 31.180 DEPARTMENT OF REDEVELOPMENT.
   (A)   There is hereby created the Department of Redevelopment of the county
which shall be entitled to exercise all the rights, powers, privileges, and
immunities accorded such Department by the Act (see I.C. 36-7-14).
   (B)   Such Department of Redevelopment of the county shall be under the
control of a Board of five members to be known as the County Redevelopment
Commission.
   (C)   The Auditor of the county is charged by law for the performance of
duties in respect to the funds and accounts of the county, and shall perform
the same duties with respect to the funds and accounts of the Department of
Redevelopment, except as otherwise provided for in the Act.
(Ord. 2021-5, passed 4-13-2021)

§ 31.181 FORENSICS SERVICES CENTER.
   (A)   The County Forensics Services Center is hereby established.
   (B)   The County Coroner shall serve as Director of said Department at this
time. The County Commissioners reserve the right to change the Director of said
Department at its discretion without the need of any prescribed amount of prior
notice. Said Director of the Department shall be an “at will” employee as that
term is defined under state law.
   (C)   (1)   The Director of the Department shall develop and implement
regulations, rules, and procedures as may be needed for the operation of said
Department.
      (2)   Any and all regulations, rules, and procedures adopted shall be
subject to review and approval by the County Commissioners.
   (D)   The Department shall establish proper accounts through the County
Coroner’s budget and maintain said accounts through the County Auditor. All
accounts for the Department shall be established and maintained and used
consistently with the laws and rules overseen and regulated by the State Board
of Accounts.
   (E)   (1)   The Department is authorized to charge fees and make payments
associated with the use and maintenance of the Forensics Services Center.
      (2)   Said fees and payments shall be subject to review and modification
by the County Commissioners at its discretion.
(Ord. 2016-1, passed 1-12-2016)

§ 31.182 COUNTY HIGHWAY DEPARTMENT; OVERTIME.
   (A)   This section shall apply to all hourly employees of the County Highway
Department. Those not affected by this section shall be the County Highway
Superintendent, all Assistant County Highway Superintendents including, but not
limited to, those serving in the capacity of Assistant County Highway
Superintendent-District Foreman, Assistant County Highway Superintendent-Bridge
Foreman, and Assistant County Highway Superintendent-Bookkeeper, which persons
are excluded from this section by virtue of said persons being an employee of
the Board of Commissioners and all of whom are personal staff members of said
Board of Commissioners involved in policy making positions.
   (B)   Not regulated or covered by this section are the existing county
policies on vacation, holidays, sick pay, severance pay, and fringe benefits.
   (C)   (1)   Effective April 14, 1986, the Board of Commissioners shall pay
each employee, as defined in division (A) above, overtime pay calculated on the
basis of one and one-half times such employee’s regular hourly rate for all
hours worked over 40.
      (2)   To be eligible for such overtime pay, such employee must be on the
job physically, and ready and able for work to be assigned to such employee;
further, such employee must actually work such period claimed.
      (3)   In order that the county be advised of its obligations to pay any
such overtime, the time card for such employee, so signed by such employee and
approved in writing by his or her authorized supervisor, must be physically
delivered to the County Auditor for payment in advance. The employee must
verify that same is accurate.
      (4)   All pay for overtime, while computed on a weekly basis, shall
nevertheless be paid every two weeks in the same manner as the employee’s
regular compensation is paid. The overtime compensation shall be based upon the
regular pay rate at the time such hours were worked. However, if an employee
should work a certain number of hours at one job with one pay rate and
additional hours at another job with a different pay rate, shall be paid by
paying the straight time hourly rate for the job such employee is actually
performing during such overtime hours, plus an additional overtime premium
equal to one-half their weighted average hourly rate for the week. The weighted
average hourly rate will equal the total straight time pay for the week divided
by the total number of hours worked as provided by law.
   (D)   Upon forms provided by the County Auditor’s office, all employees
shall keep a regular, accurate record of their hours worked for each workday,
and the total hours worked by such employee for each and all workweeks. This
record shall include the time which said employee began work for that workday,
the time which employment ended for that workday, and any hours (or parts
thereof) of overtime for that day. Such time sheet shall be signed by the
employee who shall verify the correctness of its contents. Such time sheet
shall further be approved, in writing, by the County Highway Superintendent and
turned over to the Assistant County Highway Superintendent in charge of
bookkeeping who shall tabulate and verify the total hours to the Auditor,
together with any overtime pay to which such employee may be entitled to. Such
overtime pay shall be paid within 30 days of the submission of same to the
County Auditor.
   (E)   In addition thereto, the County Highway Superintendent’s office shall
keep an accurate record of all vacation days, sick days, and personal leave
days of each employee upon forms prescribed by the office of the County
Auditor.
   (F)   All records hereunder shall be maintained for a minimum of three
years.
   (G)   No overtime shall be paid for any hours not shown upon the time sheets
received by the office of the County Auditor, and it shall be the
responsibility of the County Highway Superintendent to see that the same are
turned in a timely manner to the Auditor. It is further the responsibility of
each employee to make an accurate record of the information required herein and
the county shall be entitled to rely upon any time sheet so signed by such
employee as being an accurate representative of such work record.
   (H)   This section shall be in effect on April 15, 1986, beginning at 12:01
a.m. and shall continue until further action by the Board of Commissioners.
   (I)   It is recognized that the law regarding overtime under the Fair Labor
Standards Act, being 29 U.S.C. §§ 201 et seq., is in a state of flux with the
Board of Commissioners having only rough drafts of the law upon which to base
this section. It may become necessary to change this section from time to time
to comply with provisions of the law not yet know to the Board of
Commissioners.
   (J)   This section shall likewise apply to any employees employed after the
date listed in division (H) above.
   (K)   (1)   Any employee who may have another job and which other job is
subject to the Fair Labor Standards Act, being 29 U.S.C. §§ 201 et seq., must
report the same to the Auditor in writing and must notify the Assistant County
Highway Superintendent in charge of bookkeeping immediately of all hours worked
on said other job in order that the overtime requirements of the Fair Labor
Standards Act may be met.
      (2)   In such case, overtime shall be computed as provided by the Fair
Labor Standards Act.
(Prior Code, § 8-34-2) (Ord. 86-, passed 4-15-1986)
§ 31.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any Auditor who knowingly violates §
31.133, by statute, commits a Class A misdemeanor under state law.
(I.C. 36-2-9-13) (Prior Code, § 2-13-4)

§ 32.01 PUBLIC PURCHASES; METHODS OF DISPOSING PROPERTY.
   (A)   Public purchases.
      (1)   Definitions. For the purpose of this division (A), the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
         DISPOSING AGENT. The board or officer of a political subdivision or
agency having the power to award contracts for which public notice is required,
with respect to property of the political subdivision or agency.
(I.C. 36-1-11-2)
         PURCHASING AGENT. The board or officer having the power to award
contracts for the purchase or lease of materials for a political subdivision or
agency.
(I.C. 36-1-9-2)
      (2) Purchase price.
         (a)   If the purchase price exceeds $25,000, the agent who is
authorized to do the purchasing for any board, office, association, or
commission of the county must comply with all of the provisions of I.C. 36-1-9-
1, preparing specifications where necessary, receiving bids, letting contracts
at a fixed price, and notice published in accordance with the provision of I.C.
5-3-1-1 through 5-3-1-9.
         (b)   If the purchase price is less than $25,000, the agent who is
authorized to do the purchasing for any particular board, office, association,
or commission must comply with the provision of I.C. 36-1-9-4, which provides
for an alternative of inviting quotes from at least three different persons.
Further, if no valid quotes are received for an item, the agent may purchase or
lease that item on the open market without further invitation or reception of
quotes, but the amount of purchase from one person is limited to $15,000.
(I.C. 36-1-9-5)
      (3)   Emergencies. In the case of emergency, the purchasing agent, upon a
declaration of emergency, may purchase or lease materials without giving notice
or receiving bids, if quotes are invited from two or more persons known to deal
in the materials required. The purchasing agent must record the declaration of
emergency and the names of those person invited to quote in the minutes of the
political subdivision or agency.
(I.C. 36-1-9-11)
      (4)   Open market. Where no items are purchased upon to open market as
provided for in division (A)(5) below, then all purchases must have a specific
contract specifying quantity, number of items ordered, date of delivery, method
of delivery, and maximum time for performance beyond which such order shall
automatically terminate.
      (5)   Bids. All bids provided for in this section shall be upon forms
provided for by the State Board of Accounts, and shall be accompanied by the
properly executed non-collusion affidavit (I.C. 36-1-9-8) and bond or certified
check, as I.C. 36-1-9-6 requires (certified checks for non-residents and bond
for state residents).
(Prior Code, § 2-5-1)
   (B)   Sale, disposal of real estate, personal property.
      (1)   A disposing agent, as defined in division (A)(1) above, who sells
or transfers personal property must comply with I.C. 36-1-11-6 which requires a
public auction for one item with an estimated value of $500, or more than one
item with an estimated total value of $1,000. Moreover, I.C. 36-1-11-6 also
permits the public or private sale of one item worth less than $500, or more
than one item with an estimated total value of less than $1,000. Worthless
property, however, may be demolished or junked.
(I.C. 36-1-11-6)
      (2)   Whenever a disposing agent purchases new property with a condition
that property of a similar nature is to be traded in or exchanged as a part of
the purchase and in reduction of the purchase price, the exchange or trade-in
may be made without advertising the disposal of such property for sale.
(I.C. 36-1-11-9)
      (3)   A transfer or exchange of property may be made with a governmental
entity upon terms agreed upon by both governmental entities as evidenced by the
adoption of a substantially identical resolution by each entity. Such transfer
may be made for any amount of real property, cash, or other personal property,
as agreed upon by entities.
(I.C. 36-1-11-8)
      (4)   (a)   Approval of sale of real estate of any value is required by
the Board of Commissioners, and if the appraised value is $10,000 or more, then
the County Council must likewise approve such sale.
         (b)   In any event, a public hearing must first be had, regardless of
the sale price.
(I.C. 36-1-11-3)
      (5)   Provided, however, that there is an alternative method of disposal
which may be used only if permission is obtained from the County Commissioners,
which is set forth in I.C. 36-1-11-12.
(Prior Code, § 2-5-2)
§ 32.02 COUNTY CREDIT CARD.
   The county holds a credit card for Wal-Mart. The following policy shall be
followed for its use.
   (A)   The credit card is to be held by the County Auditor’s office.
   (B)   (1)   Any department that uses the card shall sign the card in and out
with date and time noted.
      (2)   The department shall then immediately return the card along with a
receipt and completed claim form to the County Auditor’s office.
   (C)   Any department that fails to comply with this policy will forfeit the
right to use the credit card and will be subject to pay any and all interest
added to credit card account.
(Document, “Credit Card Policy”)
§ 32.03 PAYMENTS TO MEMBERSHIP ORGANIZATIONS.
   (A)   The Board of Commissioners is authorized to budget, and the County
Council is authorized to appropriate funds from the General Fund or from other
funds, to provide membership for the county and for the elected and appointed
officials and members of the county’s boards, councils, departments, or
agencies in local, regional, state, and national associations of a civic,
educational, or governmental nature, which has, as their purpose, the
betterment and improvement of county government operations.
   (B)   The Board of Commissioners is further authorized to budget and the
County Council is further authorized to appropriate funds to pay the expense of
duly authorized representatives to attend the meetings and functions of
organizations to which the county belongs.
(Prior Code, § 8-12-2) (Ord. 1982-3, passed 9-27-1983)
**** FEES ****
§ 32.15 COLLECTION OF CHECK SERVICE FEES BY PROSECUTING ATTORNEY.
   (A)   The Prosecuting Attorney of the county is authorized to collect a
check service fee on behalf of any individual, person, firm, corporation, or
any other legal entity who is or may be the holder or payee of any check or
draft that has been turned over to the office of the Prosecuting Attorney for
prosecution or collection.
   (B)   The Prosecuting Attorney of the county is authorized to collect said
check service fee from the maker, drawer, or any other responsible individual
on any check or draft that has been returned to the payee named thereon or
holder thereof by a financial institution for non-payment.
   (C)   The Prosecuting Attorney is hereby authorized to retain all check
service fees so collected, and to deposit the same into a trust checking
account, established and maintained by the Prosecuting Attorney, or his or her
designate.
   (D)   All funds so collected, less any amounts returned to the payees or
holders who turned over said checks or drafts to the office of the Prosecuting
Attorney for the prosecution or collection, are hereby declared to be General
Fund miscellaneous revenues.
   (E)   The office of the Prosecuting Attorney shall maintain such books,
records, and accounts as may, from time to time, be prescribed by the State
Board of Accounts; in the absence or failure of the State Board of Accounts to
prescribe such books, records, or accounts, in writing, the Prosecuting
Attorney may establish such records or accounts as he or she, in his or her
discretion, deem necessary and appropriate to properly account for said funds;
all of such books, records, and accounts shall be open at all times for
examination by the State Board of Accounts.
   (F)   Monthly, the office of the Prosecuting Attorney shall turn over to the
Auditor of the county all check service fees collected during the preceding
month less the amounts returned to the payees or holders who turned over check
or drafts to the office of the Prosecuting Attorney for prosecution or
collection.
   (G)   The Prosecuting Attorney may, at the time of turning over such funds
to the County Auditor, file with the Auditor a request for an additional
appropriation to the budget of the Prosecuting Attorney of the county in the
amount not to exceed that of the funds turned over to the Auditor and to be
acted upon by the County Council at its next scheduled meeting.
   (H)   The collection of any check service fee or the amount of the fee to be
charged, within the statutory limits of I.C. 35-43-5-5(e), or the percentage of
said fee returned to the payees or holders and the percentage of said fee
retained by the office of the Prosecuting Attorney, shall be discretionary with
the office of the Prosecuting Attorney.
(Prior Code, § 8-33-1) (Ord. 86-1, passed 1-7-1986)
§ 32.16 ENHANCED ACCESS SERVICES FOR COPYING COSTS.
   (A)   There is established an enhanced access user’s fee (hereinafter
“enhanced access fee”) for providing enhanced access to electronic data or
other county services in accordance with I.C. 5-14-3 et seq.
   (B)   The enhanced access fee shall be uniform to all purchasers without
regard for the purpose or use of the data therefrom. The enhanced access fee
imposed shall depend upon the products and/or services requested by the
purchaser. The enhanced access fee shall be paid by any person or entity that
requests enhanced access to the county’s electronic data or other county
services. The prices for the products and services offered by the county shall
be as set out in division (F) below.
   (C)   Nothing in this section shall be construed to require the county or
any of its departments, elected officials, or employees to provide enhanced
access to public documents or county services.
   (D)   In the event that the fees for enhanced access established by the
county are contrary to any charge established by state statutes, the state
statute shall apply.
   (E)   Pursuant to the provisions of I.C. 5-14-3-3(e), no person other than
those authorized by the county may reproduce, store, grant access to, deliver,
or sell any information obtained by or through enhanced access services from
any department or office of the county to any other person. In addition, any
person who receives information from the county through enhanced access
services shall not be permitted to use any mailing lists, addresses, or
databases for the purpose of selling, advertising, or soliciting the purchase
of information or services, or to sell, loan, give away, or otherwise deliver
the information obtained by the request to any other person.
   (F)   The enhanced access fees are as follows.

§ 32.17 OVERSIZED COPIES FEES.
   (A)   (1)   The County Surveyor is authorized to assess a fee of $10 per
page for oversized color copies of plats and other oversized copies he or she
makes using the Canon IPF 785 copier.
      (2)   The County Surveyor is authorized to assess a fee of $5 per page
for oversized black and white copies of plats and other oversized copies he or
she makes using the OCE Plotwave 340 plotter/scanner.
      (3)   The County Surveyor is authorized to assess a fee of $0.25 per page
for scanning copies of plats and other oversized he or she makes using the OCE
Plotwave 340 plotter/scanner.
   (B)   All fees collected for oversized color and black and white copies and
scans shall be deposited in accordance with the rules and regulations of the
State Board of Accounts and shall be maintained in the County Surveyor’s
Oversized Color Copy Fund.
   (C)   The County Surveyor is further authorized to use funds collected from
the assessment of this fee for purchase of any and all supplies and/or the
maintenance of any and all equipment used in the production of oversized copies
and scans.
(Ord. 2017-01, passed 1-24-2017)
§ 32.18 BACKGROUND CHECK SERVICES.
   (A)   The County Sheriff’s Department is hereby authorized to impose a fee
of up to $5 per eligible request to provide criminal background check services
to the citizens of the county.
   (B)   All monies received by the County Sheriff’s Department shall be
maintained and accounted for in accordance with the rules and regulations of
the State Board of Accounts through the office of the Auditor of the county.
   (C)   Additionally, the County Sheriff’s Department shall have an account
specially designated through the office of Auditor of the county, whereby said
Department can obtain deposited funds through the regular county appropriation
process to pay for expenses associated with the provision of criminal
background check services.
(Ord. 2005-06, passed 4-5-2005)
§ 32.19 FINGER PRINTING SERVICES.
