of up to $5 per eligible request to provide fingerprinting services to the
citizens of the county.
   (B)   All monies received by the County Sheriff’s Department shall be
maintained and accounted for in accordance with the rules and regulations of
the State Board of Accounts through the office of the Auditor of the county.
   (C)   Additionally, the County Sheriff’s Department shall have an account
specially designated through the office of Auditor of the county, whereby said
Department can obtain deposited funds through the regular county appropriation
process to pay for expenses associated with the provision of fingerprinting
services.
(Ord. 2005-05, passed 4-5-2005)
§ 32.20 REAL PROPERTY ENDORSEMENT.
   (A)   The Board of Commissioners of the county authorizes the County Auditor
to collect a fee of $10 for each legal description of each parcel contained in
the deed for which the Auditor makes a real property endorsement.
   (B)   The definition of DEED is stated as follows: A signed and usually
sealed instrument containing some legal transfer, bargain, or contract.
(Ord. 2019-02, passed 6-11-2019)
§ 32.21 SHERIFF’S SALE PROGRAM.
   (A)   The Sheriff’s Sale Program is approved and established to provide the
procedure for the Sheriff to contract for those administrative, technical,
clerical, and related services that are reasonable and appropriate for the
Sheriff to effectively prepare for, manage, and implement foreclosure sales.
   (B)   The Sheriff is authorized to negotiate and execute a contract with a
provider to obtain such administrative, technical, clerical, and related
services (the “Sheriff Sale Services”) in order for the Sheriff to conduct the
Sheriff’s Sale Program.
   (C)   The Sheriff Sale Services contract shall provide for the delivery of
such services by a contractor (the “contractor”) in compliance with all
applicable statutory provisions for the conduct of foreclosure sale proceedings
and the Sheriff’s Sale Program. The Sheriff Sale Services contract shall also
provide for the payment of a fee, not to exceed the amount in division (F)
below for each cause number scheduled in the Sheriff’s Sale Program, to the
contractor for such services.
   (D)   The Sheriff is hereby authorized to charge a fee of the amount in
division (F) below per cause number of property in the Sheriff’s Sale Program
(the “foreclosure costs fees”) and to deposit such foreclosure costs fees
collected by or on behalf of the Sheriff in the Sheriff Sale Fund.
   (E)   The foreclosure costs fees shall be payable at the time of filing the
praecipe under I.C. 32-29-7-3(i), which shall be a charge for the Sheriff’s
sale in addition to other statutory costs and fees.
   (F)   The estimated costs herein are estimates of the County Sheriff for the
administration of foreclosure sales as required by I.C. 32-29-7. The fees are
authorized by I.C. 32-29-7-3.
 
Contractual services $180 per cause number
Sheriff’s cost     $120 per cause number
 
(Ord. 2022-19, passed 11-6-2022)
§ 32.22 GUN RANGE FEES.
   (A)   The County Sheriff’s Department be and hereby is directed to collect
the sum of $75 per day for use of the county gun range by other law enforcement
agencies and individuals who may request use thereof.
   (B)   All funds received for use of the gun range shall be deposited into
the Firearms Funds of the County Sheriff’s Department and utilized in the
maintenance of the gun range.   
(Ord. 2018-07, passed 9-18-2018)
§ 32.23 SEX AND VIOLENT OFFENDERS FEES.
   (A)   The County Sheriff is hereby authorized to assess and collect the
annual sex offender registration fee of $50 for all citizens for whom
registration is required by state law.
   (B)   The County Sheriff is hereby authorized to assess and collect the
change of address fee of $5 per change for all citizens for when a change of
address is required by state law.
   (C)   The County Sheriff shall collect, account for, and distribute all
funds taken in by this section to the County Auditor as required by I.C. 36-2-
13-5.6.
   (D)   In the event of non-payment of either of the fees set forth above, the
County Sheriff shall make a report of the same to the Board of County
Commissioners.
   (E)   The Board of County Commissioners may then refer the matter to the
County Attorney to institute collection proceedings through Small Claims Court
or any other manner permitted by state law.
   (F)   All collection actions shall be undertaken in the name of the county.
   (G)   The Board of County Commissioners also reserve the right to assess and
collect reasonable attorneys’ fees and associated costs in pursuit of unpaid
registration fees and change of address fees.
(Council Ord. 2007-04, passed 10-23-2007; Ord. 2024-09, passed 10-22-2024)
§ 32.24 USE OF COURTHOUSE FEES.
   (A)   The County Board of Commissioners establishes a user fee of $25 per
day or night that the County Courthouse is used beyond or outside of regular
business hours to any individual or group who make proper arrangements for use
of said facility.
   (B)   The Auditor of the county shall establish an account called Courthouse
User Fee Fund in which any and all user fees collected shall be deposited.
   (C)   All user fees collected for use of the Courthouse shall be deposited
into the Courthouse User Fee Fund and shall be used to defray the cost of
janitorial services, maintenance, or security of the building as may be
actually incurred for the after hours use of the Courthouse.
   (D)   The County Board Commissioners reserve the right to amend or change
the amount of the user fee charged and collected as the costs of janitorial and
security services may change from time to time.
   (E)   The County Board of Commissioners also expressly reserve unto itself
the right to waive assessment of the user fee if said Board finds that it is in
the best interests of the public to not assess a user fee for a particular
individual or group using the Courthouse after hours.
(Ord. 98-07, passed 9-1-1998)
§ 32.25 PROPERTY TAX ABATEMENT FILING FEE.
   (A)   The County Council directs the County Treasurer and the County Auditor
to assess and collect the fees called for pursuant to I.C. 6-1.1-12.1-14 for
all approved tax abatements.
   (B)   The County Auditor shall follow the formula set out at I.C. 6-1.1-
12.1-14(c) in determining the fee to be collected from the property owner of an
approved property tax abatement.
   (C)   (1)   Upon receipt of the fee assessed to the property owner by the
County Treasurer and County Auditor, said fee shall be distributed as follows:
 
County Economic Growth Council           50%
County Economic Redevelopment Commission 50%
 
      (2)   Said receiving entities shall hold, manage, and utilize said funds
received as prescribed by state law.
(Council Res. 2019-10, passed 12-23-2019)

   (B)   Nursing health services. These fees are stated in terms of “current
fees” and this fee may change without notice due to the cost of the vaccines,
medical supplies, or protective material required to provide the services
stated. When the fees change (increase or decrease) the supporting documents
will be kept on file to justify the change in the fee.
   (C)   Maximum fee schedule. The fees set forth in this section are maximum
fees. Specific fees charged shall be determined by and limited in accordance
with any applicable state and federal laws, rules, and regulations pertaining
thereto.
   (D)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
      AMERICAN HEART ASSOCIATION (AHA) BASIC LIFE SUPPORT (BLS). AHA BLS
Provider is a course intended to educate health professionals on the
recognition and reaction to medical events necessitating the use of
cardiopulmonary resuscitation, use of an automated external defibrillator, and
choking first aid.
      STOP THE BLEED (STB). A joint national campaign and training initiative
by the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services, FEMA, and the Uniformed
Services University of Health Sciences. The goal of this training is to reduce
preventable death by bleeding before emergency medical services arrive.
(Ord. 2023-06, passed 7-18-2023; Ord. 2024-05, passed 8-13-2024)
**** ENHANCED EMERGENCY TELEPHONE SYSTEM FEE ****
§ 32.40 ESTABLISHED.
   (A)   Under the authority of and subject to the provisions of I.C. 36-8, an
emergency telephone system fee is established for the funding of the
installation and operation of the enhanced emergency telephone system in the
county.
   (B)   The fee shall be collected from the telephone service users of the
county and administered in accordance with the state code, as outlined at I.C.
36-8, and the following terms set forth in this subchapter.
(Ord. 94-8, passed 8-23-1994)
§ 32.41 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ENHANCED EMERGENCY TELEPHONE SYSTEM. A telephone system that uses the three-
digit “911” number to send automatic number identification and automatic
location identification for reporting police, fire, medical, or other emergency
situations.
   EXCHANGE ACCESS FACILITY.
      (1)   The access from a particular service users premises to a telephone
system, which shall include:
         (a)   An access line;
         (b)   A private branch exchange (PBX) trunk;
         (c)   A centrex line trunk equivalent, all of which are provided by
the service supplier; and
         (d)   A mobile telephone system access trunk, whether the trunk is
provided by a telephone company or a radio common carrier.
      (2)   The term does not include:
         (a)    A service supplier owned and operated telephone pay station
line;
         (b)   A wide area telecommunication service [MISSING TEXT]
   SERVICE SUPPLIER. A person who provides exchange telephone service to a
service user.
   SERVICE USER. A person to whom exchange telephone service is provided.
(Ord. 94-8, passed 8-23-1994)
§ 32.42 MONTHLY FEE.
   A monthly fee of $1.40 per month per telephone access line or other exchange
access facility shall be collected by the service supplier for each month or
part of a month an exchange access facility is in service as a part of its
normal billing process.
(Ord. 94-8, passed 8-23-1994)
§ 32.43 SERVICE SUPPLIER FEE.
   Each service supplier that collects the enhanced emergency telephone system
fee on behalf of the county shall be entitled to a 3% administrative fee as
compensation for collecting said monthly fee.
(Ord. 94-8, passed 8-23-1994)
§ 32.44 REMITTAL FEE.
   Each service supplier shall remit all monthly fees collected, except for the
3% administrative fee, to the Treasurer of the county within ten days of the
last day of each month. At the same time the fees are remitted, the service
supplier shall provide a fee collection report to the Auditor of the county on
a form approved by the Auditor.
(Ord. 94-8, passed 8-23-1994)
§ 32.45 DELINQUENT FEE REPORT.
   (A)   During January of each year, each service supplier that collects the
enhanced emergency telephone system fee for the county shall provide a
delinquent fee report to the County Treasurer.
   (B)   The report shall list the name, address, and amount due from each
service user who is two or more months delinquent in paying the fee.
   (C)   The service supplier shall also indicate the amount of delinquent fees
for which each person included on the list is liable.
(Ord. 94-8, passed 8-23-1994)
§ 32.46 COMMENCEMENT OF FEE COLLECTION.
   Each service supplier shall commence collection of the enhanced emergency
telephone system fee as soon as it is administratively practicable after the
effective date of this subchapter.
(Ord. 94-8, passed 8-23-1994)
§ 32.47 STATE CODE CONTROLLING.
   (A)   This subchapter shall be applied, construed, and administered in
accordance with I.C. 36-8-16 et seq., and any amendment thereto.
   (B)   In the event that I.C. 36-8-16 et seq. shall be amended by the state
in such a manner as to become inconsistent, or cause a conflict with the
provisions of this subchapter, then, and in that event, this subchapter shall
be amended to include and adopt those provisions of the state code amending
I.C. 36-8-16 et seq., as if the same were fully set forth and incorporated
herein.
(Ord. 94-8, passed 8-23-1994)
**** INTERNAL CONTROL STANDARDS ****
§ 32.60 PURPOSE.
   The county hereby adopts the Uniform Internal Control Standards for the
State Political Subdivision developed by the State Board of Accounts pursuant
to I.C. 5-11-1-21 and I.C. 5-11-1-27 as the minimum internal control standards
for the county. This subchapter shall also include all amendments or
modifications that may be adopted or implemented in the future.
(Ord. 2022-07, passed 6-21-2022)
§ 32.61 WRITTEN NOTICE.
   Pursuant to I.C. 5-11-1-27(1), any public official, department head, or
employee with actual knowledge or reasonable cause to believe that there has
been a misappropriation of public funds or public property is to immediately
send written notice of the misappropriation to the State Board of Accounts and
the Prosecuting Attorney, regardless of the amount or value.
(Ord. 2022-07, passed 6-21-2022)
§ 32.62 MATERIALITY THRESHOLD.
   The materiality threshold, at which point the county shall report incidents
of material variances, losses, shortages, to the State Board of Accounts is:
   (A)   A one-time cash loss of at least $500;
   (B)   A recurring cash loss of at least $50 average per month;
   (C)   A value of $500 for a one-time loss of assets; or
   (D)   A creation of additional liabilities of at least $1,000 average per
month.
(Ord. 2022-07, passed 6-21-2022)
§ 32.63 ITEMS NOT REPORTED.
   All items not reported to the State Board of Accounts shall be resolved at
the Board of Commissioners’ level with appropriate documentation retained by
the County Auditor.
(Ord. 2022-07, passed 6-21-2022)
§ 32.64 MATERIALITY.
   When an irregular variance, loss, shortage, or theft is determined material
pursuant to the county’s policy on materiality, the Auditor and/or the
Treasurer shall report the incident to the State Board of Accounts. On the
State Board of Accounts’ website there is a notification link, which allows
public officials to report via e-mail material irregular variances, losses,
shortages, or thefts. Telephone and in-person reporting is also acceptable.
Reports will be followed up with a return e-mail or call to gather additional
information as necessary. All reports of irregular variances, losses,
shortages, or thefts are maintained by the State Board of Accounts.
(Ord. 2022-07, passed 6-21-2022)
§ 32.65 DECLARATION.
   Below is the County Ethics Policy/Internal Control/Materiality Declaration,
as adopted and approved by the County Commissioners.
LAWRENCE COUNTY ETHICS POLICY/INTERNAL
CONTROLS/MATERIALITY DECLARATION
It is the policy of Lawrence County (“County”) that its employees uphold the
highest standards of ethical, professional behavior. To that end, these employees
shall dedicate themselves to carrying out the mission of the County and shall:
1.  Hold paramount the safety, health, and welfare of the public in the performance
    of professional duties.
2.  Act in such a manner as to uphold and enhance personal and professional honor,
    integrity, and the dignity of the profession.
    Treat with respect and consideration all persons, regardless of race, religion,
3.  gender, sexual orientation, maternity, marital or family status, disability,
    age, or national origin.
4.  Engage in carrying out the County’s mission in a professional manner.
5.  Collaborate with and support other professionals in carrying out the County’s
    mission.
6.  Build professional reputations on the merit of services and refrain from
    competing unfairly with others.
7.  Recognize that the chief function of the County at all times is to serve the
    best interests of its citizens.
    Accept as a personal duty the responsibility to keep up to date on emerging
8.  issues and to conduct themselves with professional competence, fairness,
    impartiality, efficiency, and effectiveness.
    Respect the structure and responsibilities of all elected officials; provide
9.  them with facts and advice as a basis for their making policy decisions, and
    uphold and implement policies or ordinances adopted by the Commissioners or
    County Council.
    Keep the citizens/taxpayers of the County informed about issues affecting them,
10. without offering editorial comments concerning local government officials while
    serving in the capacity of County employee. Notwithstanding every individual’s
    right of expression as a private citizen.
11. Conduct organizational and operational duties with positive leadership
    exemplified by open communication, creativity, dedication, and compassion.
12. Exercise whatever discretionary authority they have under the law to carry out
    the mission of the County.
13. Serve with respect, concern, courtesy, and responsiveness in carrying out the
    County’s mission.
    Demonstrate the highest standards of personal integrity, truthfulness, honesty,
14. and fortitude in all activities in order to inspire confidence and trust in
    such activities.
15. Avoid any interest or activity that is in conflict with the conduct of their
    official duties.
16. Respect and protect privileged information to which they have access in the
    course of their official duties.
17. Strive for personal and professional excellence and encourage the professional
    developments of others.
 
(Ord. 2022-07, passed 6-21-2022)
§ 32.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who violates the terms and conditions of §
32.16 by failing to pay or violating §
32.16(E) shall be guilty of an infraction and may be fined up to $2,500.
Additionally, the person who uses information in a manner contrary to §
32.16(E) may be prohibited from obtaining further information or data through
enhanced access services.
(Ord. 2014-04, passed 5-5-2014)
CHAPTER 33: TAXATION
Section
   
33.01   Adjusted gross income tax
   
33.02   Excise surtax, wheel tax
   
33.03   Innkeeper’s tax
§ 33.01 ADJUSTED GROSS INCOME TAX.
   (A)   The county adjusted gross income tax, as provided in the County
Adjusted Gross Income Tax Law, is hereby adopted for and on behalf of the
county at the county rate of 1% for resident county taxpayers and one-fourth of
1% for all other county taxpayers in that county. (Bill 1144, Passed June 1,
1973)
(Prior Code, § 8-21-1)
   (B)   (1)   The county (“county”) hereby imposes an additional rate of the
county adjusted gross income tax at the rate of 50% in accordance with state
law to raise income tax revenue to provide property tax relief to political
subdivisions in the county.
      (2)   Property tax relief shall take the form of:
         (a)   Fifty percent for local property tax replacement credits at a
uniform rate to all taxpayers in the county. The local property tax replacement
credits shall be treated for all purposes as property tax levies.
[MISSING TEXT]
         (e)   The County Council acknowledges that some taxpayers in the
county that do not pay the property tax relief rate will receive a property tax
replacement credit that is funded with tax revenue from the property tax relief
rate.
   (C)   (1)   The county hereby imposes an additional rate of the county
adjusted gross income tax at rate of 0.25%, in accordance with I.C. 6-3.5-1.1-
25, to provide funding for public safety in the county (the “public safety
rate”).
      (2)   (a)   The County Auditor shall distribute income tax revenues
attributable to the public safety rate to the county and to each municipality
in the county.
         (b)   The County Auditor shall distribute the portion of the certified
distribution that is attributable to the public safety rate to the county and
to each municipality in the county in an amount equal to the result of the
portion of the certified distribution that is attributable to the public safety
rate, multiplied by a fraction equal to:
            1.   The attributed allocation amount (as defined in I.C. 6-3.5-
1.1-15) of the county or municipality for the calendar year; and
            2.   Then divided by the sum of the attributed allocation amounts
of the county and each municipality in the county for the calendar year.
[MISSING TEXT]
(Council Ord. 2009-03, passed 7-28-2009; Council Ord. 2009-04, passed 7-28-
2009)
§ 33.02 EXCISE SURTAX, WHEEL TAX.
   (A)   The county hereby adopts and imposes the motor vehicle excise surtax,
which shall apply to the following vehicles:
      (1)   Passenger vehicles;
      (2)   Motorcycles;
      (3)   Trucks with a declared gross weight that does not exceed 11,000
pounds; and
      (4)   Motor driven cycles.
   (B)   The motor vehicle surtax to be imposed shall be at the rate of $25 per
vehicle described in division (A) above.
   (C)   The county further adopts and imposes the county wheel tax, which
shall apply to the following vehicles:
      (1)   Buses;
      (2)   Recreational vehicles;
      (3)   Semi-trailers;
      (4)   Tractors;
      (5)   Trailers; and
      (6)   Trucks.
   (D)   The following vehicles are exempt from the wheel tax established by
this section:
      (1)   Vehicles owned by the state;
      (2)   Vehicles owned by a state agency of the state;
      (3)   Vehicles owned by a political subdivision of the state;
      (4)   Vehicles subject to the motor vehicle excise surtax imposed by this
section; and
      (5)   Buses owned and operated by a religious or non-profit youth
organization and used to haul persons to religious services or for the benefit
of their members.
   (E)   The county wheel tax to be imposed shall be at the following rates:
      (1)   For buses: $25 per bus;
      (2)   For recreational vehicles: $25 per vehicle;
      (3)   For semi-trailers: $25 per semi-trailer;
      (4)   For tractors: $25 per tractor;
      (5)   For trailers: $5 per trailer; and
      (6)   For trucks: $25 per truck.
   (F)   The State Bureau of Motor Vehicles shall collect the county motor
vehicle excise surtax and the county wheel tax by and through its established
procedures on behalf of the county. The State Bureau of Motor Vehicles shall be
entitled to assess a service fee for assisting the county in collection of
these taxes.
   (G)   The County Council shall review the rates hereby established for the
County Motor Vehicle Excise Surtax and the County Wheel Tax within five years
from the date of passage.
(Council Ord. 2014-09, passed 11-25-2014)
§ 33.03 INNKEEPER’S TAX.
   The County Council now fixes the County Innkeeper’s Tax at the rate of 5% on
the gross retail income derived from lodging income in the county as allowed
for by I.C. 6-9-18-3(c).
(Ord. 2004-09, passed 11-23-2004)
CHAPTER 34: LAW ENFORCEMENT AND PUBLIC SAFETY
Section
Police Department
   
34.01   County Sheriff
   
34.02   County Police Reserve
   
34.03   Pre-basic law enforcement course fee
   
34.04   Overtime pay, compensation; Sheriff’s Department
**** POLICE DEPARTMENT ****
§ 34.01 COUNTY SHERIFF.
   (A)   (1)   A SHERIFF is an independently elected public officer and is made
responsible, by common and statutory law, as the keeper of the peace within his
or her jurisdiction. It is an office provided for by the constitution.
      (2)   The Sheriff is elected every four years in the general elections,
as began in 1950, and every four years thereafter, by amendment to the Indiana
State Constitution (Article 6, Section 11, added November 2, 1948). The
Constitution further provides that no person shall be eligible to be Sheriff
for more than eight out of 12 years.
      (3)   I.C. 36-2-13-5 set further certain powers and duties for County
Sheriffs. The statute states:
“(aThe Sheriff shall:
         Arrest, without process, persons who commit an offense within his or
    (1) her view, take them before a court of the county having jurisdiction,
         and detain them in custody until the cause of the arrest has been
         investigated;
    (2) Suppress breaches of the peace, calling the power of the county to his
         or her aid, if necessary;
    (3) Pursue and jail felons;
    (4) Execute all process directed to him or her by legal authority;
    (5) Serve all process directed to him or her from a court or the County
         Executive;
    (6) Attend and preserve order in all courts of the county;
         Take photographs, fingerprints, and other identification data as he or
    (7) she shall prescribe of persons taken into custody for felonies or
         misdemeanors.
(b)  A person who:
    1.  Refuses to be photographed;
    2.  Refuses to be fingerprinted;
    3.  Withholds information; or
    4.  Gives false information.
As prescribed in subsection (a)(8), commits a Class C misdemeanor. (I.C. 17-3-
5-2, 17-3-5-3, recodified as I.C. 36-2-13-5 by Acts 1980, P.L. 212, Section 1;
P.L. 344-1983, Section 1.)”
 
(Prior Code, § 2-10-1)
   (B)   The Sheriff is required to attend meetings of the County Commissioners
or County Council only if requested to do so by the Commissioners or Council.
(Prior Code, § 2-10-2)
   (C)   The Board of Commissioners has no power over the action of the
Sheriff, nor does it seek to control same. The Sheriff is not required to come
to the Commissioners’ meetings at this time.
(Prior Code, § 2-10-4)
§ 34.02 COUNTY POLICE RESERVE.
   (A)   There is hereby established a County Police Reserve which shall
consist of not more than 12 Reserve Police officers and which shall be
designated by the name “Lawrence County Sheriff’s Reserve” or the “County
Sheriff’s Reserve.”
   (B)   In accordance with I.C. 36-8-3-20 which provides for the establishment
of a Police Reserve, that such members of the County Sheriff’s Reserve shall be
appointed by the Sheriff of the county subject to approval by the Sheriff’s
Merit Board, up to the maximum number provided herein which the said Sheriff
deems necessary to supplement the effective functioning of the Sheriff’s
regular police force; that such police reserves shall not be members of the
Sheriff’s regular police force but shall have all of the same police powers of
such regular members except as limited by any rule or rules of the County
Sheriff’s Department; and, that said police reserves may not participate in any
pension program provided for regular members of the County Sheriff’s
Department.
   (C)   The members of said County Sheriff’s Reserve shall receive no salary
or wages for their services; nor will the county be responsible for their
services; nor will the county be responsible to furnish uniforms to members of
the said police reserve, although the Board of Commissioners of the county do
reserve the right to provide for furnishing uniforms should it deem it
necessary and appropriate in the future.
   (D)   All members of the County Sheriff’s Reserve shall be protected by life
and also liability insurance at the expense of each member of said Police
Reserves and not at the expense of the county; although the Board of
Commissioners of the county does reserve the right to provide life and
liability coverage for each member of the said Police Reserve at any time the
Board should determine such action to be necessary and appropriate, including,
but not limited to, those occasions when the said Board would find it more
economical and/or expeditious to purchase additional insurance coverage under
an existing county insurance plan for later reimbursement by members of the
said Police Reserve of such expenses to the county.
(Prior Code, § 8-17-2) (Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-21-1978)
§ 34.03 PRE-BASIC LAW ENFORCEMENT COURSE FEE.
   (A)   The County Police Department shall be required to assess and collect a
fee of $40 per officer for each officer enrolling in and participating in the
pre-basic law enforcement course sponsored and presented by said Department.
   (B)   All fees collected by the County Police Department by means of this
section shall be deposited into the County Police Department Training Fund and
shall be used solely to defray the costs and expenses incurred in conducting
the pre-basic law enforcement course.
(Ord. 96-4, passed 10-15-1996)
§ 34.04 OVERTIME PAY, COMPENSATION; SHERIFF’S DEPARTMENT.
   (A)   Included under this section are all Deputy Sheriffs and the County
Jailer, and all assistant matrons. While the Jailer was not included in the
list of Deputy Sheriffs for which the FOP (Springmill Lodge #172) was
negotiating, nevertheless the Commissioners determine that it would be in the
best interest of continuity if they were bound by the same provisions as any
Deputy Sheriff. Not covered by this section is the Sheriff, his or her first
Deputy, and the matron (head matron) who is excluded as a personal staff
member.
   (B)   Not regulated or covered by this section are the existing county
policies on vacation, holidays, sick pay, severance pay, and fringe benefits.
   (C)   Not regulated or covered by this section are the existing county
policies on vacation, holidays, sick pay, severance pay, and fringe benefits.
   (D)   To be eligible for compensation such employee must be on the job
physically, and ready and able for work to be assigned to such employee;
further, such employee must actually work such period claimed.
   (E)   In order that the county be advised of its obligations to pay any such
overtime or grant compensatory time, the time card for such employee, so signed
by such employee and approved in writing by his or her authorized supervisor,
must be physically delivered to the County Auditor for payment in advance. The
employee must verify that same is accurate.
   (F)   (1)   A 28-day period is hereby established for all persons covered
hereunder as a work cycle and such persons, hereinafter referred to as
“employee,” shall work for a maximum of 171 hours in such 28-day calendar
period. After having worked 160 hours in said 28-day period, then for each hour
between 160 hours and 171 hours, such employee shall be entitled to the same
number of hours off from work on a basis of one-for-one without any additional
pay.
      (2)   As referred to in division (F)(2) above, compensatory time will be
earned at a rate of one and one-half hours for each one hours actually worked
over 171 hours in the 28-day cycle so established. The employee may accumulate
up to 480 hours of compensatory time off, and the employee will further receive
monetary overtime compensation for overtime hours actually worked, which would
cause such employee’s accumulation to exceed 480 hours.
      (3)   Overtime shall be paid for all hours worked over 171 in such 28-day
work cycle so established for the purpose of calculating overtime. The amount
off overtime pay will be one and one-half times the employee’s regular hourly
rate for all hours worked over 171 in any 28-day cycle so established by the
County Commissioners.
      (4)   (a)   All pay for overtime and/or grants of compensatory time
which, computed on a weekly basis, shall nevertheless be paid every two weeks
in the same manner as the employee’s regular compensation is paid. The overtime
compensation shall be based upon the regular pay rate at the time such hours
were worked.
         (b)   However, if an employee should work a certain number of hours at
one job with one pay rate, and additional hours at another job with a different
pay rate so as to justify overtime pay, then such overtime pay rate shall be
paid by paying the straight time hourly rate for the job such employee is
actually performing during such overtime hours, plus an additional overtime
premium equal to one-half of such employee’s weighted average hourly rate for
the week. The weighted average hourly rate will equal the total straight time
pay for the week, divided by the total number of hours worked as provided by
law.
      (5)   The employee may use earned compensatory time off with the approval
of such employee’s supervisor authorized to grant such approval. If it is not
possible to approve the particular compensatory time off requested by such
employee, time off will be granted within a reasonable period, normally within
30 days, unless the requested time off would unduly disrupt operations. The
employee will receive compensation based upon the regular pay rate in effect
when the compensatory time off is actually taken.
   (G)   The employer may elect to substitute monetary overtime for pay earned
compensatory time off, either at the time overtime is actually worked or in any
later period. Monetary overtime compensation will be based upon the employee’s
regular pay rate at the time of payment.
   (H)   The county may also elect to apply earned compensatory time off to
hours the employee may have missed due to personal absence, short workweeks,
layoffs, or leave of absence, including medical leaves if not covered by paid
sick days, or authorized by a physician’s medical report. In periods of slack
work, it may be necessary to schedule time off, to which earned compensatory
time off will be applied.
   (I)   Upon forms provided by the County Auditor’s office, all employees
shall keep a regular, accurate record of their hours worked for each workday,
and the total hours worked by such employee for each and all workweeks. This
record shall include the time which said employee began work for that workday,
the time which employment ended for that workday, and any hours (or parts
thereof) of overtime for that day. Such time sheet shall be signed by the
employee who shall verify the correctness of its contents. Such time sheet
shall further be approved, in writing, by the person designated and authorized
by employee’s department head to make any such approval and processed by such
office which shall tabulate and verify the total hours to the Auditor, together
with any overtime pay and/or compensatory time to which such employee may be
entitled to. Such overtime pay shall be paid within 30 days of the submission
of the same to the County Auditor.
   (J)   In addition thereto, the office employing such employee shall keep an
accurate record of all vacation days, sick days, and personal leave days of
each employee upon forms prescribed by the office of the County Auditor.
    (K)   All records hereunder shall be maintained for a minimum of three
years.
   (L)   No overtime shall be paid and/or compensatory time allowed for any
hours not shown upon the time sheets received by the office of the County
Auditor and it shall be the responsibility of the office employing any
particular employee to see that the same are turned in timely to the Auditor.
It is further the responsibility of each employee to make an accurate record of
the information required herein and the county shall be entitled to rely upon
any time sheet so signed by such employee as being an accurate representation
of such work record.
   (M)   This section shall be in effect on April 15, 1986, beginning at 12:01
a.m. and shall continue until further action by the Board of Commissioners.
   (N)   It is recognized that the law regarding overtime under the Fair Labor
Standards Act, being 29 U.S.C. §§ 201 et seq., is in a state of flux with the
Board of Commissioners having only rough drafts of the law upon which to base
this section. That it may become necessary to change this section from time to
time to comply with provisions of the law not yet known to the Board of
Commissioners.
   (O)   This section shall likewise apply to any employees employed after this
date.
   (P)   Any employee who may have another job and which other job is subject
to the Fair Labor Standards Act must report the same to the Auditor in writing
and must notify such employee’s immediate supervisor of all hours worked on
said other job in order that the overtime requirements of the Fair Labor
Standards Act, being 29 U.S.C. §§ 201 et seq., may be met. In such case,
overtime shall be computed as provided by the Fair Labor Standards Act.
(Prior Code, § 8-36-2) (Ord. 1986-, passed 4-15-1986)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 35.01 PUBLIC RECORDS ACCESS EXEMPTIONS.
   (A)   The Board of Commissioners of the county exempts the following public
records from disclosure, unless access to the records is specifically required
by state or federal statute, or is ordered by a court under the rules of
discovery:
      (1)   Those declared confidential by state statute;
      (2)   Those declared confidential by rules adopted by a public agency
under specific authority to classify public records as confidential granted to
the public agency by statute;
      (3)   Those required to be kept confidential by federal law;
      (4)   Records containing trade secrets, or confidential financial
information;
      (5)   Investigatory records of law enforcement agencies;
      (6)   Test questions, scoring keys, and other examination data used in
administering a licensing examination, examination for employment, or academic
examination before the examination is given or if it is to be given again;
      (7)   Scores of tests or license examinations, if the person is
identified by name and has not consented to the release of their scores;
      (8)   Records that contain intra-agency or inter-agency advisory or
deliberative material that are expressions of opinion or are of a speculative
nature, and that are communicated for the purpose of decision making;
      (9)   Diaries, journals, or other personal notes serving as the
functional equivalent of a diary or journal;
      (10)   Personal files of public employees, except for:
         (a)   The name, compensation, application for employment or
appointment, job title, business address, business telephone number, job
description, education and training background, previous work experience, or
dates of first and last employment of present or former officers or employees
of the agency;
         (b)   Information relating to the status of any formal charges against
the employee; and
         (c)   Information concerning disciplinary actions in which final
action has been taken and which resulted in the employee being disciplined or
discharged; provided, however, all personnel file information shall be made
available to the affected employee whose file it is, or to any representative
of such employee as such employee may direct by a written statement that is
executed by such employee and which signature is duly notarized before a notary
public.
      (11)   Administrative or technical information that would jeopardize a
recordkeeping or security system;
      (12)   Computer programs, computer codes, computer filing systems, and
other software that are now owned by the public agency or entrusted to it, or
in the future may be owned by such public agency or entrusted to it;
      (13)   Records specifically prepared for discussion or developed during
discussion in an executive session under I.C. 5-14-3-4;
      (14)   The identity of a donor of a gift made to a public agency, if the
donor requires non-disclosure of their identity as a condition of making the
gift;
      (15)   Any other record which current or future legislation allows a
public agency to exempt. Further action by the county is unnecessary; and
      (16)   The work product of an attorney appointed by the county to
represent the county, or an individual at the request of the Board of
Commissioners.
   (B)   This section shall apply to all records of the County Sheriff and the
Prosecuting Attorney of the 81st Judicial Circuit, except if the Sheriff may be
required to provide information pursuant to I.C. 5-14-3-5 as amended; and
   (C)   The form for request of access to public records which the Auditor
shall supply any such person so seeking such information shall be as follows:
FORM FOR REQUEST OF ACCESS TO PUBLIC RECORDS
The undersigned requests the right to inspect and copy the records of the
following public agency: (Here list name of public agency and identify the
records to the extent possible so as to aid in the identification of the records
by the Records Access Officer):
(If this space is not sufficient, check this box and continue to the back side of
this form. You must sign both sides of the form if you are using both sides for
your request.)
Except as provided for in I.C. 5-14-3-4.
 1. The undersigned requests to:
   a. O Inspect the above,
   b. O Copy the above, or
   c. O Both
(Please select an option above.)
     This request shall be made to the Records Access Officer in writing and they
     shall have twenty-four (24) hours in which to respond, provided, however,
     that whenever the request is received on a Friday or on a day preceding a
 2. legal holiday, such Records Access Officer shall have until the same hour on
     the next business day in which to release, copy, or refuse disclosure. The
     County Attorney shall serve as Records Access Officer, but if they are not
     available, then the Auditor shall so serve as such officer.
 3. The fees for copying are as follows:

        determined by the total number of sheets needed.
   c. Any other record - the actual cost of copying.
           All of the above listed fees shall be paid to such Records Access
     i. Officer upon their approval of such request and prior to any such
           copies being made.
     This request is subject to the provisions of §
 4. 35.01 of the Lawrence County Code. A copy of the County Code is on file with
     and may be inspected at the Lawrence County Auditor’s office during normal
     working hours when the office is otherwise open for business.

(Prior Code, § 8-2-1)

§ 35.02 COUNTY-OWNED VEHICLES.
   (A)   All county employees who are assigned county-owned motor vehicles for
use in the performance of their job related duties shall maintain a log or
other records evidencing each day that said employee utilizes said vehicle for
purposes of commuting from home to work or from work to home.
   (B)   Only such employees who are required to use a county-owned motor
vehicle in the performance of their duties or are required to be on call after
regular business hours should be given the use of a county-owned motor vehicle.
   (C)   Each employee having use of a county-owned motor vehicle shall be
assessed $1.50 for each one-way commute that said employee makes in said
county-owned motor vehicle. If a county employee utilizes a county-owned motor
vehicle to go to work and to return home from work, then for each such day,
said employee will be assessed a total sum of $3 per day.
   (D)   (1)   On a monthly basis, each employee having access of a county-
owned motor vehicle shall be required to turn a log or record of the use of
said motor vehicle to his or her supervisor.
      (2)   Said supervisor shall deliver the records to the office of County
Auditor for purposes of tracking such use.
   (E)   The County Board of Commissioners hereby directs that for each such
county employee using a county-owned motor vehicle, said employee shall not use
said vehicle for personal purposes other than for commuting or de minimis
personal use.
(Res. 2001-11, passed 7-31-2001)
§ 35.03 CONFLICT OF INTEREST.
   (A)   No public official or county employee shall accept a gratuity of any
amount greater than $200 from a vendor conducting business with the county. A
gratuity of any amount $200 or less for a single event is considered not
substantial. Gratuities include invitations to events where business is often
conducted and items customarily conveyed therein, including, but not limited
to, dinners, conferences, and golf outings at which county officials and
employees may be the guests of vendors that do business with the county.
   (B)   This conflict of interest policy establishes a rule in addition to
those imposed by the state and federal governments regarding financial
incentives for public servants and elected officials. Any conflict with state
or federal laws or regulations of this policy are to be interpreted in favor of
the state or federal regulation setting precedent.
(Res. 2017-03, passed 2-21-2017)
**** HANDICAP COMPLIANCE ****
§ 35.15 FACILITIES FOR HANDICAPPED MUST BE FURNISHED.
   (A)   The handicap non-discrimination administrative requirements for
facilities within the county include:
      (1)   Publication of notice stating the Handicapped Non-Discrimination
Policy;
      (2)   Designation of a responsible employee or office;
      (3)   Adoption of a grievance procedure;
      (4)   Completion of a self-evaluation plan; and
      (5)   Preparation of the preparation plan.
   (B)   The public notice of the revenue sharing handicapped regulations is as
follows:
PUBLIC NOTICE - REVENUE SHARING HANDICAPPED
REGULATIONS
   This notice is published pursuant to the provisions of C.F.R. Title 45 of
the Revenue Sharing Regulations as published in the Federal Register on October
17, 1983. Section 51.55 prohibits discrimination against qualified individuals
because of their handicapped status.
   THE BOARD OF COMMISSIONERS OF THE COUNTY OF LAWRENCE, Lawrence County,
Bedford, Indiana, does hereby advise the public, employees, and job applicants
that it does not discriminate on the basis of handicapped status in admission
or access to, or treatment or employment in, its programs and activities.
   Further, the Board of Commissioners of the County of Lawrence has designated
the Office of the Auditor of Lawrence County as the contact to coordinate
efforts to comply with this effort. Inquiries should be directed to:
 Auditor’s Office
 Courthouse
 Bedford, Indiana 47421
 Phone No. (812) 275-3111
 Office Hours: 8:30 A.M. thru 4:30 P.M.
  Mondays, Tuesdays, Wednesdays, Thursdays, and Fridays
 Closed: Saturdays, Sundays, and Holidays
 
(Prior Code, § 4-1-1)
§ 35.16 GRIEVANCE PROCEDURE.
   (A)   This procedure is established to meet the requirements of the Revenue
Sharing Act, being 29 U.S.C. §§ 201 et seq. It should be used by any individual
who wishes to file a complaint alleging discrimination on the basis of handicap
in employment practices and policies or the provision of programs, services,
and benefits by the county.
   (B)   The grievance should be in writing and contain as much information as
possible about the alleged discrimination (such as supply the name, address,
phone number, location, and description of the problem, and the like). Other
arrangements for submission of a grievance, such as a personal interview or
tape recording, will be made available for the visually impaired or those with
motor impairments. It should be submitted by the person making such grievance,
or his or her designee within 30 calendar days of the alleged violation to the
County Auditor, Lawrence County, Bedford, Indiana 47421, telephone number
(812)-275-3111. Office hours are 8:30 a.m. through 4:30 p.m., Monday through
Friday (closed Saturday and Sunday, and holidays).
   (C)   (1)   Within 15 working days after the receipt of the complaint, the
Auditor, as Program Coordinator, will respond in writing, or by a method
understood to the complainant, to the complainant and/or his or her designee.
      (2)   The response will offer a resolution or explain the position of the
county with respect to the complaint.
   (D)   If the response by the Auditor does not satisfactorily resolve the
issue, the complainant and/or his or her designee may request a hearing, to be
held within 15 working days of receipt of the response, before the Board of
Commissioners of the county, which is the Handicapped Coordinating Committee,
for resolution (Courthouse, Bedford, Indiana, telephone number (812)-275-3111.
Office hours are 8:30 a.m. through 4:30 p.m., Monday through Friday [closed
Saturday and Sunday, and holidays]).
   (E)   (1)   Within 30 calendar days of the hearing, the final resolution in
writing (or by a method understood to the complainant) shall be delivered to
the complainant and/or his or her designee by the Board of Commissioners of the
county.
      (2)   (a)   Service shall be deemed to have been made if mailed to the
address shown on the written grievance of such person by certified mail, return
receipt requested (unless the county has received written notice of a change of
address of such person).
         (b)   Any and all persons making such complaints to the county must
keep the Board of Commissioners informed or any and all address changes.
   (F)   All complaints received by the Auditor and all responses from the
Auditor or the Board of Commissioners shall be kept in the files of the county
for a minimum of three years, and these documents may be requested by the
office of Revenue Sharing should an investigation into alleged discrimination
on the basis of handicapped status be initiated.
(Prior Code, § 4-2-1)
§ 35.17 SELF-EVALUATION.
   The Board of Commissioners shall cause a self-evaluation to be made and
provided according to law.
(Prior Code, § 4-3-1)
**** BULK FORM COPIES ****
§ 35.30 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BULK FORM COPY. An aggregation of:
      (1)   Copies of all recorded documents received by the County Recorder
for recording in a calendar day, week, month, or year;
      (2)   The indices for finding, retrieving, and viewing all recorded
documents received by the County Recorder for recording in a calendar day,
week, month, or year; or
      (3)   Both divisions (1) and (2) above.
   BULK USER. An individual, a corporation, a partnership, a limited liability
company, or an unincorporated association that receives bulk form copies under
a contract with a County Recorder.
   COPY. A reproduction including an image of a recorded document or indices
created by:
      (1)   Duplicating electronically stored data onto a disk, tape, drum, or
any other medium of electronic data storage; or
      (2)   Reproducing on microfilm.
   INDICES. All of the indexing information used by the County Recorder for
finding, retrieving, and viewing a recorded document.
   RECORDED DOCUMENT. A writing, a paper, a document, a plat, a map, a survey,
or anything else received at any time for recording or filing in the public
records maintained by the County Recorder or the County Recorder’s designee.
(Ord. 2017-03, passed 8-22-2017)
§ 35.31 MANNER, FORM OF PRODUCTION.
   It is hereby established that the County Recorder shall provide bulk form
copies to a bulk user only by electronically transmitting the copies using an
electronic transfer process.
(Ord. 2017-03, passed 8-22-2017)
§ 35.32 PROCEDURE FOR REQUESTING BULK FORM COPIES.
   (A)   A bulk user must submit a written request to the County Recorder that
identifies the requested bulk form copies with reasonable particularity. Unless
the request is refused under §
35.35, upon receipt of a valid written request the County Recorder or the
County Recorder’s designee shall provide the bulk copies to the bulk user by
the method or methods established by this subchapter. The bulk form copies
shall be provided within a reasonable time after the latter of the following
events:
      (1)   The Recorder’s archival process is completed and bulk form copies
become available in the County Recorder’s office; and
      (2)   The bulk form user executes a contract that meets the requirements
of §
35.35 with:
         (a)   The County Recorder; and
         (b)   If the County Recorder uses a third party to provide bulk copy
services, the County Recorder’s designee.
   (B)   The County Recorder or the County Recorder’s designee shall work with
reasonable diligence to ensure that bulk form copies are timely produced to the
bulk user.
(Ord. 2017-03, passed 8-22-2017)
§ 35.33 FEES CHARGED FOR BULK FORM COPIES.
   (A)   The following fee schedule is hereby adopted:
      (1)   Thirteen cents per page of a copy of a recorded document, including
the instrument’s book and page, if applicable; and
      (2)   Thirteen cents per recorded document for a copy of the indices used
by the County Recorder for finding, retrieving, and viewing a recorded
document.
   (B)   The fees charged by the County Recorder are subject to the following
requirements:
      (1)   The County Recorder shall pay the fees into the county treasury at
the end of each calendar month;
      (2)   The fees prescribed and collected under this section supersede all
other fees for bulk form copies required by the County Recorder to bulk users;
      (3)   All revenue generated by the County Recorder under this section
shall be deposited into the County Recorder’s Perpetuation Fund and used by the
Recorder in accordance with I.C. 36-2-7-10(f); and
      (4)   The Recorder shall periodically update and verify the cost study
referred to in division (A) above.
(Ord. 2017-03, passed 8-22-2017)
§ 35.34 BULK USER CONTRACT TERMINATION.
   (A)   (1)   A bulk user must enter into a contract with the County Recorder
and, if the County Recorder uses a third party to provide bulk copy services,
the County Recorder’s designee in order to receive bulk for copies. The
contract must be in writing and must require that the bulk user agrees not to
do any of the following:
         (a)   Except as provided in §
35.35, provide, transfer, or allow the transfer of any copy of a recorded
document obtained by the bulk user under this section to a third party;
         (b)   Engage in unauthorized access to recorded documents; and
         (c)   Engage in unauthorized alteration of recorded documents.
      (2)   A contract required under this division (A) may not include any
restrictions on a bulk form user’s use of the bulk form copies other than those
contained in this section.
   (B)   If a bulk user does not comply with a contract, the County Recorder
may terminate the contract, immediately stop providing bulk form copies to the
bulk user, and refuse to provide the bulk copies required by the bulk user if
all termination provisions and procedures in the contract have been met by the
County Recorder. The County Recorder may refuse subsequent requests from a bulk
user for bulk form copies in the following circumstances:
      (1)   The bulk user is a person that has had a previous bulk copy
contract terminated by the County Recorder because the Recorder determined that
the bulk user failed to comply with the contract; and
      (2)   The bulk user is a corporation or limited liability company in
which a person has a majority or controlling interest, and:
         (a)   The person requested bulk form copies under a previous contract
with the County Recorder; and
         (b)   The contract was terminated by the County Recorder because the
Recorder determined that the person failed to comply with the contract.
(Ord. 2017-03, passed 8-22-2017)
§ 35.35 RESALE OF BULK FORM COPIES.
   (A)   (1)   A bulk user that is licensed under I.C. 27-1-15.6-6(d) or holds
a certificate of authority under I.C. 27-7-3-6 may provide bulk form copies
related to a specific order a title search (as defined in I.C. 27-7-3-2) when
operating as:
         (a)   A title plant for the issuance of title insurance (as defined in
I.C. 27-7-3-2); or
         (b)   A title company (as defined in I.C. 27-7-3-2).
      (2)   A bulk user that meets the requirements of this division (A) may
charge its customers a fee for using the bulk fee for using the bulk for copies
obtained by the bulk user that may not exceed the costs incurred by the bulk
user obtaining the bulk form copies. A bulk user that meets the requirements of
this division (A) may not resell, provide, transfer, or allow the transfer of
any copy of a recorded document, whether in bulk form or as individual copies
or images, to any other bulk user title plant.
   (B)   A bulk user that does not meet the requirements of division (A) above
is prohibited from selling, offering for sale, advertising for sale, soliciting
a purchase of, loaning, giving away, allowing subscription service to, or
otherwise transferring, provide, or allowing the transfer of bulk form copies
for commercial purposes to a third party, whether the copies are in bulk form
or as individual copies or images.
(Ord. 2017-03, passed 8-22-2017)
