**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 50.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   CLOSELY BUILT UP AREAS. Any areas situated outside the corporate limits of
any city or town upon which areas are located either residential or business
buildings. Provided, however, that it shall not included any tract of land,
situated outside the corporate limits of any city or town, consisting of ten
acres or more and upon which tract of land is located only one building which
is used for residential purposes. Further, that it shall not include any tract
of land situated outside the corporate limits of any city or town which tract
of land is used for farming or agricultural purposes.
   COMBINED SEWER. A sewer receiving both surface water run off and sewage.
   PRIVATE SEWAGE DISPOSAL SYSTEM. Any sewage disposal system not constructed,
installed, and maintained, operated, and owned by a municipality or a taxing
district established for that purpose.
   PUBLIC SEWER. Any sewer constructed, installed, maintained, operated, and
owned by a municipality or a taxing district established for that purpose. A
county sewer installed for the purpose of carrying surface water run off and
subsoil drainage shall not be considered a PUBLIC SEWER under this definition.
   SANITARY SEWER. A sewer which carries sewage and to which storm, surface,
and ground waters are not intentionally admitted.
   SEWAGE. Any combination of human excreta and wastewater from water closets,
laundries, sinks, bathing facilities, and other objectionable wastewaters.
   SEWAGE DISPOSAL SYSTEM. Any arrangement of devices and structures used for
receiving, treating, and disposing of sewage.
   SEWER. A pipe of conduit for carrying sewage.
(Prior Code, § 8-25-1)
**** ON-SITE SEWAGE DISPOSAL SYSTEMS ****
§ 50.15 TITLE.
   This subchapter, and all ordinances supplemental or amendatory hereto, shall
be known as the “On-site Sewage Disposal Ordinance of Lawrence County,” and may
be cited as such, and will be referred to herein as “this subchapter.”
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.16 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of this subchapter is to provide minimum standards for the
prevention and suppression of disease and health risks associated with the use
of on-site sewage disposal systems, and to otherwise promote public safety and
welfare, and protection of the environment.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.17 AUTHORITY.
   The Health Officer of the county, as hereinafter defined, and the Health
Officer’s agents and representatives are hereby authorized to issue permits,
collect permit and incidental fees, perform inspections, and order or otherwise
compel correction of violations of this subchapter, and are otherwise
authorized to perform all actions necessary for the administration and
enforcement of this subchapter.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.18 ADOPTION OF REGULATIONS BY REFERENCE.
   (A)   The regulations of the State Department of Health, as found in 410
I.A.C. 6-8.3 et seq., 410 I.A.C. 6-10.1 et seq., and Bulletin S.E. 13, 1998
edition, et seq. are hereby incorporated by reference in this subchapter and
shall include any later amendments to those regulations as the same are
published in the State Register of the Indiana Administrative Code with
effective dates as fixed therein. [H.I.]
   (B)   Copies of 410 I.A.C. 6-8.1 et seq., 410 I.A.C. 6-10 et seq,, and
Bulletin S.E. 13, 1998 edition, et seq. are available and on file in the office
of the County Board of Health and the County Auditor.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.19 DEFINITIONS.
   In addition to or to otherwise supplement those definitions contained in 410
I.A.C. 6-8.3 et seq., 410 I.A.C. 6-10.1 et seq., and Bulletin S.E. 13 et seq.
which are incorporated herein by reference this subchapter shall include the
following definitions.
   BEDROOM EQUIVALENT. A room with a window and closet, a jetted bathtub,
swimming pool, computer room, game room, business or office in the home, second
kitchen facility, or basement plumbing. Each of the preceding BEDROOM
EQUIVALENTS will be counted as a bedroom when calculating septic tank capacity
and soil absorption field size.
   BOARD. The County Board of Health.
   BUSINESS BUILDING. A building or structure utilized primarily for a
commercial establishment including, but not limited to, office buildings,
apartments, condominiums, motels, mobile home parks, churches, campgrounds,
schools, hospitals, nursing homes, restaurants, and the like, at locations
where such facilities may be approvable.
   DUPLEX. A single dwelling under one roof as defined in 410 I.A.C. 6-8.1-
7 with two separate living quarters for two separate families.
   HEALTH OFFICER. The Health Officer of the county.
   MULTIPLE FAMILY UNITS. Any building or place used or intended to be used as
a place of seasonal or permanent human habitation or for sleeping for more than
two families.
   ON-SITE SEWAGE DISPOSAL SYSTEM. Any sewage disposal facility not owned by a
municipality or sanitary district or administered by the State Department of
Health and shall include septic tanks, aeration tanks, elevated sand mounds,
finger systems, or other subsurface absorption fields.
   PLANNED DEVELOPMENT. Any land development, which requires the specific
zoning classification entitled PLANNED DEVELOPMENT.
   PUBLIC WATER SUPPLY. A system that provides piped water for human
consumption to at least 15 service connections or at least 25 people, at least
60 days out of the year.
   RESIDENCE. A dwelling, as defined in 410 I.A.C. 6-8.1-7.
   SANITARY PRIVY. A privy so located, constructed, and maintained, as defined
in the State Department of Health Bulletin S.E. 11.
   SET-ASIDE. An area that has been soil tested and deemed suitable by the
County Health Department for replacement or addition to an on-site sewage
disposal system according to 410 I.A.C. 6-8.1 et seq. on the same site should
the original on-site sewage disposal system fail or become inadequate due to
modifications to the dwelling.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.20 SYSTEM REQUIREMENTS.
   Where a sanitary sewer system is not available, all persons owning, leasing,
or otherwise occupying property shall comply with 410 I.A.C. 6-8.1 et seq. and
410 I.A.C. 6-10 et seq. and the following provisions for an on-site sewage
disposal system.
   (A)   No person shall throw, run, drain, seep, or otherwise dispose into any
of the surface waters or groundwaters of the county, or cause, permit, or
suffer to be thrown, run, drained, allowed to seep, or otherwise disposed into
such waters any organic or inorganic matter from an on-site sewage disposal
system that would cause or contribute to health hazard or water pollution.
   (B)   Privies shall comply with the State Department of Health Bulletin S.E.
11. Bulletin S.E. 11 is herein incorporated by reference as part of this
section and two copies are filed in the office of the County Auditor, and the
County Board of Health for public inspection. In the case of self-contained
chemical toilets, no pit will be required. If a privy, chemical toilet,
incinerating toilet, composting toilet, or any other toilet that is not
connected to plumbing fixtures is utilized and any additional wastewater is
generated from the residence, duplex, multiple family unit, or business
building, an on-site sewage disposal system in compliance with this subchapter
must be utilized.
   (C)   Should an on-site sewage disposal system fail, the failure shall be
corrected by the owner or occupant of the property served by such system within
the time limit set by the Health Officer.
   (D)   Whenever a sanitary sewerage system becomes available and is within
500 feet of the property line of developed property in the county that contains
a residence, duplex, multiple family unit, or business building served by an
on-site sewage disposal system, chemical toilet, composting toilet,
incinerating toilet, privy, or any other toilet not connected to plumbing
fixtures, a direct connection of the building sewer shall be made to said
sanitary sewer and any septic tanks, seepage pits, outhouses, privy pits, and
similar sewage disposal and treatment facilities shall be abandoned and filled
in a safe and sanitary manner. The direct connection to a sanitary sewerage
system shall be made within 180 days of issuance of orders of connection.
   (E)   Whenever a new residence, duplex, multiple family unit, or business
building is to be constructed in an area where a sanitary sewerage system is
available as provided in division (D) above, a connection shall be made to the
sanitary sewer prior to construction of any such project.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.21 MINIMUM BUILDING SITE SIZE.
   (A)   In order to be considered for an on-site sewage disposal system, a
proposed building site shall have room for the approved septic absorption field
and an additional set-aside area for installation of a second on-site sewage
disposal system in the event of a failure or other need for future repair to
the original system.
   (B)   The set-aside area must be located in an area on the building site
that has been soil tested and deemed suitable by the County Health Department
for sustaining an on-site sewage disposal system.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.22 PERMIT PROCESS.
   (A)   Application. Before the commencement of construction, alteration, or
repair of an on-site sewage disposal system, the owner shall apply in writing
to the County Health Department for a permit to construct, alter, or repair an
on-site sewage disposal system. The application shall consist of:
      (1)   An application form;
      (2)   A fee;
      (3)   A soil analysis report conducted by an ARCPACS or IRSS recognized
soil scientist;
      (4)   A house plan;
      (5)   A system drawing which addresses the demands of the site in
accordance with 410 I.A.C. 6-8.3 et seq. and this subchapter, and also includes
the location of the set-aside area; and
      (6)   A list of system components, as well as any other information
required by the Health Officer or in 410 I.A.C. 6-8.1-48 and 410 I.A.C. 6-10-6,
previously incorporated herein by reference.
   (B)   Fees.
      (1)   A permit fee as determined by the County Board of Health shall be
payable as provided by the fee ordinance of the County Health Department.
      (2)   The fee for an on-site sewage disposal permit shall be paid at the
time the application is submitted.
   (C)   Permit.
      (1)   A permit for the installation of an on-site sewage disposal system
issued after the adoption of this subchapter shall lapse and be void if work
has not been started within one year after permit issuance. A soil survey more
than one year old may be reviewed by the County Health Department to determine
if additional soil analysis and information is required.
      (2)   (a)   No part of an on-site sewage disposal system for a residence,
duplex, multiple family unit, or business building shall be located closer than
25 feet to a surface water drain tile, streambed, or surface water drain. The
rules and regulations of the Drainage Board will apply for any legal ditch or
drain.
         (b)   All parts of the on-site sewage disposal system must be kept at
least 50 feet from any water well or spring, 50 feet from an open sinkhole, and
25 feet from a closed sinkhole.
      (3)   No on-site sewage disposal system shall serve more than one
residence or mobile home, more than one duplex, multiple family unit, or
business building unless permitted by 410 I.A.C. 6-10 et seq.
      (4)   Any residence, duplex, multiple family unit, or business building
that will be served by an on-site sewage disposal system and that has public
water lines located within 500 feet of its property line shall be required to
connect to such public water utility.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.23 INSTALLATION.
   (A)   Installer license.
      (1)   No person shall construct, install, replace, repair, or alter any
part of any on-site sewage disposal system in the county until the person is
licensed by the County Health Department as a licensed installer for the type
of system to be installed.
      (2)   In order to become licensed, an individual must:
         (a)   File an application for license;
         (b)   Demonstrate knowledge of the applicable laws, rules, and
regulations before becoming licensed by passing a proficiency examination
conducted by the County Health Department, or any other exam sanctioned by the
County Health Department (for example, the Indiana On-site Wastewater
Professional Association’s (IOWPA) exam);
         (c)   Present proof of liability insurance issued by a company
registered in the state showing coverage for the entire term of the license.
The liability policy shall be sufficient to indemnify persons for whom faulty
work may be performed or a minimum of $300,000;
         (d)   Present a surety bond issued by a company registered in the
state showing coverage in the amount of $10,000. Said surety bond shall be
filed with the County Health Department, who will control the proceeds of the
same in the event a claim is made against said bond; and
         (e)   Pay an initial fee as set forth in the County Health Department
fee ordinance.
      (3)   Licenses issued hereunder shall be valid for a term of one year
beginning January 1 and expiring December 31 of the same year and shall be
renewed annually. The license shall bear the name and address of the licensed
installer and the expiration date and shall not be transferable. Any license
that is not renewed within one year of the expiration date shall be considered
void and the installer must re-apply for license and pass the proficiency test.
      (4)   The installer shall maintain in his or her possession a copy of the
license at all times when installing on-site sewage disposal systems.
      (5)   A licensed installer shall be on the site at all times during
construction, and shall be deemed responsible for the installation. A licensed
installer may supervise other construction workers as necessary to assist in
the installation.
      (6)   A property owner wishing to install, repair, or otherwise work on
the on-site sewage disposal system serving his or her own dwelling shall be
required to demonstrate knowledge of the applicable laws, rules, and
regulations by taking the proficiency exam conducted by the County Health
Department or any other exam sanctioned by the County Health Department (for
example, the Indiana On-site Wastewater Professional Association’s (IOWPA)
exam).
   (B)   Fees.
      (1)   A fee for an installer license shall be submitted prior to issuance
of the license. An annual renewal fee shall also be assessed for continuation
of the license and shall also be submitted prior to issuance of the renewed
license. These fees shall be set forth in the County Health Department fee
ordinance.
      (2)   A property owner as described in division (A)(6) above shall be
exempt from the license fee.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.24 INSPECTIONS.
   The County Board of Health, its agent, or the Health Officer or his or her
agent shall be permitted to enter upon all properties for purposes of
inspection, observation, measurement, sampling, and testing necessary to ensure
compliance with this subchapter and to verify no on-site sewage disposal system
has failed.
   (A)   When deemed necessary, inspections by the Health Officer or his or her
agent may be conducted before permit issuance or construction.
   (B)   No construction of the on-site sewage disposal system may take place
if the on-site sewage disposal system site is disturbed or altered after the
on-site soil analysis by the addition of fill material (other than construction
necessary for the on-site sewage disposal system itself) or by cutting,
scraping, compaction, or the removal of soil, until a new soil analysis has
been conducted and a modified permit has been issued.
   (C)   Each septic site must be protected from construction traffic by being
roped or otherwise cordoned off.
   (D)   The County Health Department is to be given two business days notice
prior to the covering of any part of the on-site sewage disposal system in
order to be able to conduct a final inspection of the system.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.25 PETITION FOR REVIEW.
   The County Board of Health shall hear appeals incidental to the issuance and
revocation of permits and/or licenses if, within 15 days following the date of
receipt of an issued permit and/or license, permit and/or license modification,
notice of permit and/or license denial or notice of permit, and/or license
revocation, and person aggrieved by such action files a petition for review
concerning such action with the Board.
   (A)   A petition for review shall state:
      (1)   The name, address, and telephone number (if applicable) of the
person making the request;
      (2)   Identify the interests of the petitioner which is affected by the
permit and/or license issuance, denial, modification, or revocation;
      (3)   Identify the persons whom the petitioner represents;
      (4)   State with particularity the issues proposed to be considered; and
      (5)   Include proposed terms or conditions which, in the judgment of the
petitioner, would be appropriate to carry out the requirements of law and 410
I.A.C. 6-10.1 et seq. or 410 I.A.C. 6-8.3 et seq. governing such permits and/or
licenses.
   (B)   The procedures established in I.C. 4-21.5, the Administrative
Procedure and Orders Act, shall apply to the conduct of the hearing.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.26 ENFORCEMENT.
   (A)   Any person found to be violating any provision of this subchapter
shall be served by the County Board of Health or the duly appointed Health
Officer with a written order stating the nature of the violation and providing
a reasonable time limit, but not less than 30 days nor more than 90 days, for
correction of any violations of this subchapter. The written order shall be
served either by certified mail or personal service by the Health Officer of
the county or his or her designate.
   (B)   (1)   Application of this subchapter or any part of this subchapter is
intended to be consistent with 410 I.A.C. 6-8.3 et seq. and 410 I.A.C. 6-10-
1 et seq.
      (2)   Any inconsistency in the direct application of this subchapter with
said regulations shall be resolved in favor of enforceability of those
regulations.
   (C)   (1)   Following the recommendations of the hearing officer, there
shall be a fine of up to $25,000 imposed upon any person or entity that fails
to obtain a septic permit prior to the installation of an on-site sewage
disposal system in the county.
      (2)   This fine shall also apply to installers and developers as well as
the home or business owner who fail to ensure that a septic permit is obtained
prior to installation of the on-site sewage disposal system or construction of
any buildings or structures on the affected property.
   (D)   (1)   There shall be sliding scale fine of up to $25,000 imposed upon
any person or entity that fails to notify the County Health Department for a
final inspection of an on-site sewage disposal system.
      (2)   This fine shall apply to installers as well as the home or business
owners of the affected property.
   (E)   To the extent the provisions of 410 I.A.C. 6-10 et seq. and 410 I.A.C.
6-8.1 et seq. is inconsistent with each other, then that interpretation
provided by 410 I.A.C. 6-10 et seq. shall apply for on-site sewage disposal
system serving business buildings and multiple family units, and that
interpretation provided by 410 I.A.C. 6-8.1 shall apply for on-site sewage
disposal system serving residences and duplexes.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.27 REMEDIES.
   (A)   The Health Officer may, in the name of the County Commissioners, bring
actions in the County Circuit Court or Superior Courts of the county for
mandatory and injunctive relief for the enforcement of and to secure compliance
with any order or orders made by the Health Officer or to otherwise provide for
the enforcement of this subchapter.
   (B)   (1)   Any such action for mandatory or injunctive relief may be joined
with an action to recover the penalties, costs, and expenses provided in this
subchapter.
      (2)   In the event any legal action is necessary to enforce this
subchapter, the Health Officer may seek recovery of costs and expenses
reasonably incurred to enforce the provisions of this subchapter including, but
not limited to, reasonable attorney’s fees.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
§ 50.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who shall continue any violation of §§
50.15 through
50.27 beyond the time limit provided for correction of any violations of §§
50.15 through
50.27 or who shall fail to perform any duty lawfully enjoined or who shall
fail, neglect, or refuse to obey any lawful order given by the Health Officer
shall be punished for the first offense by a fine of $1,000 for the first day,
and $500 for each day the violation continues. Each day a violation of §§
50.15 through
50.27 continues shall constitute a separate offense for which a separate fine
may be levied.
(Ord. 2006-04, passed 9-18-2006)
CHAPTER 51: GARBAGE
Section
Trash Containers
   
51.01   Certain acts, persons prohibited
   
51.02   Definitions
   
51.03   Use of trash containers
 
   
51.99   Penalty
Cross-reference:
   Landfill operations, Chapter 114
**** TRASH CONTAINERS ****
§ 51.01 CERTAIN ACTS, PERSONS PROHIBITED.
   (A)   (1)   No individuals, partnerships, firms, businesses, corporations,
or associations not having his, her, or its current residence in the county
shall, by any means, use any trash container located in the county, or the
county landfill, for the purpose of dumping or depositing any items of trash,
garbage, refuge, bricks, building stone, car springs, refrigerators, stoves, or
any other items.
      (2)   Said trash containers and the County Landfill are for the benefit
of county residents and taxpayers only.
      (3)   The prohibition of this section shall likewise apply to any
commercial hauler of trash or refuge.
(Prior Code, § 8-4-1)
   (B)   (1)   No person, firm, partnership, corporation, or association shall
put, throw, dump, or leave any tin cans, old iron, brush, boxes, tools,
machinery, vehicles, rubbish, brick, wood, logs, glass, glassware, glass
bottles, glass jars, broken glass, broken glassware, dead animals, or fowls, or
debris of any character whatsoever in, upon, or within, or along any county
road or highway in the county.
      (2)   No person shall in any way dispose of, dump, bury, cover, discard,
or throw away any hazardous waste anywhere in the county, as the term hazardous
waste is defined by the State Board of Health and its various divisions.
(Prior Code, § 8-5-1)
(Ord. 1973-2, passed 6-18-1973; Ord. 76-1, passed 4-19-1976; Ord. 1984-4,
passed 7-23-1984; Ord. passed 10-31-1989) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   LANDFILL. The county landfill during hours when the same is open to the
public.
   PERSON. Includes singular or plural, as well as any firm, partnership,
corporation, or association.
   TRASH CONTAINERS. Any metal container placed by the county at various points
in the county for the collection of trash.
(Prior Code, § 8-5-1) (Ord. 1973-2, passed 6-18-1973; Ord. 76-1, passed 4-19-
1976; Ord. passed 10-31-1989)
§ 51.03 USE OF TRASH CONTAINERS.
   (A)   (1)   No person, firm, partnership, corporations, or association shall
put, throw, dump, or leave any refrigerator, stove, electric or gas appliance,
stone, gravel, bricks, or mortar or sand inside or beside or alongside any
trash container in the county.
      (2)   Any such person, firm, partnership, corporation, or association
desiring to dispose of any such items shall take same to the county landfill
and shall deposit same inside the county landfill in the area designated for
such deposits.
   (B)   No dead animals or fowls shall be deposited in any trash container in
the county by any person, firm, partnership, corporation, or association. Such
as those as the operator of the landfill will permit shall only be deposited at
the county landfill in the county in the properly-designated area.
   (C)   No person, firm, partnership, corporations, or association shall put,
throw, dump, or leave any trash or rubbish or refuse of any kind beside a trash
container in the county. If the trash containers in any area are full, then any
person, firm, partnership, corporation, or association shall dump the same in a
trash container in another location which has capacity to accept same, or shall
dump the same in the county landfill in the properly designated area.
   (D)   No person, firm, partnership, corporation, or association shall
engaged in either the full-time or part-time business of commercial trash and/
or rubbish hauling and/or disposal for any person, firm, partnership,
corporation, or association other than themselves or itself, nor shall they
put, throw, dump, or leave any trash, rubbish, refuse, appliance, or article of
any kind or nature in any trash container in the county, but instead shall
deposit the same at the county landfill in the properly designated area.
   (E)   (1)   No person shall commit or do any of the following acts:
         (a)   Examine the contents of any said trash containers;
         (b)   Remove all or any part of the contents from said trash
containers (this includes, but is not limited to, bottles, cans, toys, tools,
and any other item);
         (c)   Overturn, upset, damage, or move any of said trash containers;
or
         (d)   1.   Pilfer the contents of any part of any trash container or
from any sacks, boxes, or other containers of trash which may be within the
immediate vicinity of any of the said trash containers, regardless of the fact
that the same would have been unlawfully deposited there.
            2.   For purposes of this division (E), any area within 150 feet of
any such trash container shall be construed to be “immediate vicinity.”
      (2)   The term PILFER, for purposes of this division (E), includes the
following terms: rummage through, tear apart, examine, remove, take from bags
or sacks or other containers, rip, tear, or cut open bags or sacks or other
containers of trash, or to undo or open same in any other way, or attempt to do
any of these acts, and in any way determine or attempt to determine what the
contents of the trash or objects that someone else may have discarded or thrown
away.
(Prior Code, § 8-5-2) (Ord. 1973-2, passed 6-18-1973; Ord. 76-1, passed 4-19-
1976; Ord. passed 10-31-1989)
§ 51.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating the provisions of §
51.01(A) shall be fined in the sum of $500 together with any court costs
occasioned by the proceedings instituted against them.
(Prior Code, § 8-4-1)
   (C)   Any person, firm, partnership, corporation, or association who
violates any provision of §§
51.01(B) through
51.03 shall, upon conviction, be fined a sum of not less than $50, nor not more
than $500 for each separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 8-5-3)
(Ord. 1973-2, passed 6-18-1973; Ord. 76-1, passed 4-19-1976; Ord. 1984-4,
passed 7-23-1984; Ord. passed 10-31-1989)

CHAPTER 70: TRAFFIC REGULATIONS
Section
General Provisions

70.04   Stop intersections; yield intersections
   
70.05   Travel restrictions; severe weather

Load Limits
   
70.20   Load limits on bridges
   
70.21   Highway load limits
   
70.22   Truck hauling permit
 
   
70.99   Penalty
**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 70.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this title, the following definitions shall apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   INTERSECTION. The part of the street roadway which lies between the lateral
boundary lines of two or more streets when two or more streets cut across or
overlap each other.
   MOTOR VEHICLE. Every vehicle which is capable of propelling itself
(excluding the engine and other cars of a train). This term shall also include
motorcycles of either two-wheel, three-wheel, or four-wheel varieties, and
other small types of motor assisted transportation, such as city bikes.
   PEDESTRIAN. Any person who is walking, standing, or sitting and who is not a
driver of or a passenger in a motor vehicle; the term shall also apply to any
person who is not an operator of or passenger upon a bicycle.
   PERSON. Every natural man, woman, or child, as well as a partnership,
corporation, and unincorporated association or similar type of ownership or, or
use of, property.
   PUBLIC WAY. A portion of real estate, normally improved, upon which persons
operate motor vehicles, and which is open to the public.
   RIGHT-OF-WAY. The right to the use of a public way.
   ROAD, STREET, OR HIGHWAY. All the width of the surface of every public way
that is maintained regularly by the county, and which has been officially
accepted into its highway system.
   SHERIFF’S DEPARTMENT. Any Sheriff, Deputy Sheriff, or Sheriff Reserve
Member.
   STOP. To quit moving.
   STOP, STOPPING, OR STANDING. Where prohibited, means the stopping of a motor
vehicle, or once stopped, to permit the same to remain in such position and
place for any time, whether the motor vehicle has a person or persons inside
it, or upon it, or not.
   STOP STREET. Every street, road, or highway which, when it intersects
another street, road, or highway, is required by law to come to an absolute
stop before proceeding to enter the intersection.
   THROUGH STREET. Every street, road, or highway over which vehicular traffic
from intersecting streets is required to yield the right-of-way.
(I.C. 9-4-1-14)
   TRAFFIC CONTROL DEVICE. Any stop sign, yield sign, speed limit sign, road
weight limit sign, bridge weight limit sign, or any other type of sign or
automatic signal which operates to direct the stopping, starting, movement,
right-of-way of one person’s movement, as opposed to another person, or
otherwise restrict motorists in their use of highways, bridges, and
intersections, which signs have been placed upon said county highways by the
county, the state, or any other political subdivision.
   YIELD RIGHT-OF-WAY. When one slows, and if necessary stops completely, in
order to permit another to proceed to enter into an intersection before them,
or take a path of travel which would otherwise have caused a toughing of the
persons involved, and/or their motor vehicles, but for such slowing or
stopping.
   YIELD SIGN. A triangular sign with the letters “Yield” imprinted thereon.
When placed alongside and facing a certain direction of travel, it means that
other persons have the right-of-way and no person should not interfere with
their right.
(Prior Code, Chapter 5-1)
§ 70.02 MAXIMUM SPEED LIMITS.
   Due to the fact that many of the county’s roadways have aged, with
insufficient right-of-way, the same having been laid out for slower means of
transportation, such as horses and wagons, the maximum speed limit throughout
all of the county is 35 mph, except where designated otherwise. No person
should attempt to drive the county roadways any faster than that. The roadways
are not designed for any greater speeds. Further, there are some areas (in and
around curves in the roadway) when even that speed may not be safe, depending
on conditions.
(Prior Code, § 5-2-1) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 70.03 IMPOUNDING VEHICLES.
   (A)   Any member of the Sheriff’s Department is authorized to remove any and
all motor vehicles or other things of whatever nature from any public street,
road, or highway in the county, to any other place which shall include any
garage or storage area maintained by the county when such motor vehicle or
other thing of whatever nature is found to be left unattended upon the streets,
roads, or highways of the county where:
      (1)   A motor vehicle or other item is found upon a public highway
unattended, and where the same is, in the Sheriff’s officer’s sole opinion,
obstructing traffic or endangering the users of such street, road, or highway;
      (2)   When there is a disabled motor vehicle upon any street, highway, or
roadway in the county which, in the sole opinion of the Sheriff’s officer,
constitutes an obstruction to the normal and safe flow of traffic, and the
person in charge of the vehicle is, by reason of physical injury, apparent use
of controlled substance or substances, of any other reason, incapable of
providing for such vehicles’ removal; or
      (3)   When any motor vehicle is parked in such a manner that any portion
thereof extends over or touches the paved surface of the roadway so as to be a
hazard or impediment to moving traffic.
(Prior Code, § 5-3-1)
   (B)   If such motor vehicle is not returned to the owner or person clearly
entitled to its use within three days of the date and hour such vehicle was so
impounded, then a written report of such removal shall be made, signed by such
Sheriff’s officer, and mailed to the Bureau of Motor Vehicles, Registration
Division. A copy of this notice shall also be given any person who is the
operator of a private garage who has possession of such vehicle.
(Prior Code, § 5-3-2)
§ 70.04 STOP INTERSECTIONS; YIELD INTERSECTIONS.
   (A)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      INTERSECTION. The area embraced within the prolongation or connection of
the lateral curb lines, or, if none, then the lateral boundary lines of the
roadways of two highways which join one another at or approximately at right
angles, or the area within which vehicles traveling upon different highways
joining at any other angle may come in conflict.
      MOTOR VEHICLE. Every automobile or vehicle, as well as all motorized
bicycles and non-motorized bicycles.
      STREET. Includes the word ROAD, ROADWAY, DRIVE, and HIGHWAY.
      THROUGH HIGHWAY. Every street or highway or portion thereof at the
entrances to which vehicular traffic from intersecting streets or highways is
required by law to yield the right-of-way to vehicles on such THROUGH HIGHWAY
in obedience to either a “Stop” sign or a “Yield” sign, when such signs are
erected as provided in this title.
(I.C. 9-13-2-178)
      YIELD INTERSECTIONS. All streets or highways or portion thereof which
bears a “Yield” sign. All motor vehicles on any public highway which bears a
“Yield” sign shall first slow such motor vehicle, even coming to a complete
stop, if necessary, in order to yield the right-of-way to those persons having
the right-of-way and operating on a through highway.
(Prior Code, § 5-4-1)
   (B)   Whenever the Board of Commissioners shall pass any ordinance which
designates any street as a stop street, or any intersection a four-way stop
intersection or three-way stop intersection, then the County Highway
Superintendent, when notified, shall be responsible for the prompt obtaining of
“Stop” signs and the erection of any such sign in accordance therewith.
(Prior Code, § 5-4-4)
   (C)   (1)   No person shall remove, damage, or possess any “Stop” sign or
other traffic control device in the county without the specific authorization
of the Board of Commissioners.
      (2)   The liability of the county is threatened every time some person
steals, knocks down, or takes any such directional sign away from its location,
or turns it in such a manner as not to be able to be observed by the
pedestrians, bicycle or motorcycle riders, and drivers of vehicles which it is
designed to protect. Thus, the enforcement of this law is urged.
(Prior Code, § 5-4-5)
   (D)   (1)   Every person operating a motor vehicle upon a county highway who
approaches an intersection marked with a “Stop” sign for traffic upon such
person’s roadway, shall first bring his or her motor vehicle to a complete stop
and shall look all ways before entering or crossing such intersection with any
through highway.
      (2)   All persons operating a motor vehicle on a through highway shall
have the preferred right-of-way over any person operating a motor vehicle upon
a street where said stop street intersects such through highway.
(Prior Code, § 5-4-6)
   (E)   Any person who comes upon an intersection which they know to be a stop
intersection is directed to proceed carefully through such intersection, due to
a large number of traffic control sign violations that remain unsolved.
(Prior Code, § 5-4-7)
   (F)   (1)   Any person who sees or otherwise has notice that a traffic
control sign has been removed for any reason shall promptly report such fact to
the County Sheriff’s Department.
      (2)   The County Sheriff’s Department shall promptly notify the county
highway garage and the persons receiving such notice shall promptly investigate
the matter and put up new signs to replace any that have been stolen or
damaged.
(Prior Code, § 5-4-8)
   (G)   (1)   The county does hereby offer a reward of $500 to any person or
persons who furnish information leading to the arrest and conviction of any
person or persons for the offense of damage, removal, or possession of any
county “Stop” sign or traffic control device.
      (2)   If information of more than one person is used for the successful
arrest and conviction of such person, then the reward money shall be shared
equally between the persons offering such information.
      (3)   Should one person be found to have damaged, removed, or possessed
more than one such traffic control device, nevertheless, only one reward of
$500 shall be paid, and not $500 per traffic control device.
      (4)   Said reward shall be paid from the County General Fund as soon as
same can properly be advertised and Councilmanic action can be taken.
(Prior Code, § 5-4-10) Penalty, see §
70.99

travel restriction categories recommended by the State Department of Homeland
Security.
      (2)   Attached to and incorporated herein under division (B) below is a
diagram listing the level and restrictive conditions established hereby.
   (B)   Emergency levels.

§ 70.06 PRIVATE ROADWAYS.
   (A)   Not all roads, streets, or highways in the county are included in the
County Highway System for maintenance by the county.
(Prior Code, § 3-1-1)
   (B)   All persons are cautioned by the Board of Commissioners to check very
carefully to make sure that the real estate you might purchase be located upon
a county roadway included in the system. Do not presume that the contractor or
seller can get the roadway accepted without someone first, at his or her own
expense, bringing that roadway up to certain minimum standards as are set
forward in division (C) below.
(Prior Code, § 3-1-2)
   (C)   No exceptions will be made to this section. If a citizen understands
that the roadway in question is going to be taken in, the citizen shall make
sure that he or she has adequate, satisfactorily guarantees from the contractor
or seller. The fact that the county may have, in the past, maintained any
portion of any roadway does not mean that the county will always do so.
(Prior Code, § 3-1-3)
   (D)   (1)   There are roads and highways which are shown on certain old maps
as being “state roads” or “public roads” or “county roads.” However, the
Commissioners have the right to close any roadway where the same is considered
to be either impractical or unsafe. (See Burns Indiana Statutes Annotated, § 8
8-17-14A-1)
(Prior Code, § 3-1-4)
      (2)   This question will not normally be answered by an abstract of title
or a title insurance policy. Those have to do with the merchantability of the
title to certain real estate, not whether same adjoins a roadway maintained by
the County Highway Department.
(Prior Code, § 3-1-5)
      (3)   (a)   Citizens shall check with the County Highway Superintendent’s
office in the Courthouse to make sure that the road or street he or she is
dealing with is presently contained in the County Highway Maintenance System.
         (b)   This is no guarantee that the road will always be maintained by
the county, but it will keep any misunderstanding from coming about at a later
time.
(Prior Code, § 3-1-6)

§ 70.20 LOAD LIMITS ON BRIDGES.
   (A)   The county contracts with the state for making, constructing, and
erecting of load limit signs for all bridges with a maximum load of less than
16 tons shown on such report of AECON, Inc. as revised and presented to the
Board of Commissioners on May 14, 1984.
   (B)   (1)   The maximum load limits for all such bridges as shown upon said
report be and the same are hereby adopted by the Commissioners and made the
absolute maximum load limits for each such representative reports.
      (2)   Such report as revised is this so adopted and incorporated by
reference herein and made a part hereof. A copy of such report is on file with
the County Auditor, Courthouse, Bedford, Indiana, and is available for
inspection during regular Courthouse hours.
(Prior Code, § 8-9-2)
(Ord. 1984-3, passed 6-11-1984) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 70.21 HIGHWAY LOAD LIMITS.
   (A)   The Board of Commissioners reserve the right to hereafter pass
ordinances fixing load limits on any specific roadway or highway and all
roadways and highways in the county.
(Prior Code, § 8-10-1)
   (B)   All weight limits shall be posted.
(Prior Code, § 8-10-2)
   (C)   This shall not prevent the County Commissioners from thereafter
lowering weight limits or increasing weight (load) limits on any particular
road as the Commissioners shall from time to time determine to be in the best
interest of the County Highway System.
(Prior Code, § 8-10-3)
§ 70.22 TRUCK HAULING PERMIT.
   (A)   All persons, companies, corporations, partnerships, and other legal
entities shall be required to obtain a truck hauling permit prior to operating
any motor vehicle upon any road within the jurisdiction of the County Highway
System if the total gross vehicle weight is in excess of the posted weight
limit for said road, as hereinafter provided.
   (B)   This section shall apply to all highways in the County Highway System
under the jurisdiction of the Board of Commissioners, except highways in the
State Highway System and the state-maintained routes thereof through cities and
towns.
(Ord. 2003-11, passed 10-21-2003)
§ 70.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person so driving any motor vehicle upon, on or over the roads,
streets, or highways of the county, as stated in §
70.02, shall be fined $50 and costs for any speed up to nine mph over the speed
limit, and $100 and costs for any speed that exceeds the figure of ten mph over
the speed limit which is applicable at that point.
(Prior Code, § 5-2-2)
   (C)   (1)   Any person who violated any section of §
70.04 for which no penalty is otherwise provided shall, upon conviction, be
fined in the sum of $200 together with the costs of the action.
(Prior Code, § 5-4-9)
      (2)   Any person so damaging or destroying any such “Stop” sign shall, as
described in §
70.04(C), upon conviction, be fined in the sum of $1,000, and in addition
thereto they shall be liable for any criminal law violations which would have
occurred by the unauthorized use of such property, or either or both.
(Prior Code, § 5-4-5)
   (D)   (1)   Any person who shall take, remove, knock or pull down, damage,
destroy, in whole or in part, deface, paint over, or obscure the lettering or
numbers upon any such sign, as described in §
70.20, or any person who shall attempt to take, remove, knock or pull down,
damage, destroy, in whole or in part, deface, paint over, or obscure the
lettering or numbers upon any such sign, may be fined not more than $1,000.
      (2)   It is a defense, under division (D)(1) above that the person who
commits a violation of §
70.20 shall:
         (a)   Report said violation of §
70.20(A) to the Sheriff’s Department, within 24 hours of said violation by
giving his or her name, home address, and telephone number to said Sheriff;
         (b)   Report the violation of §
70.20(A) to the Highway Department of the county within 72 hours of such damage
by giving his or her name, home address, and telephone number to said
Department;
         (c)   Pay the costs of repair and/or replacement of such sign as
determined by the County Highway Superintendent within 14 days of being
notified in writing of such cost of replacement. The 14-day time period shall
begin to run on the day after such notification of costs has been served upon
them, either personally or by certified mail; and/or
         (d)   Any person who shall cross any such posted bridge with a total
gross tonnage in excess of the maximum load limit so posted may be fined not
more than one $1,000.
(Prior Code, § 8-9-2)

SCHEDULE I. INTERSECTIONS.
   (A)   (1)   Motor vehicular traffic on the inner part of the Public Square
in Bedford, Indiana, and seeking to exit onto 15th Street, J Street, or 15th
Street, I Street, must first yield to the motor vehicular traffic traveling on
15th Street, J Street, or 15th Street, I Street.
      (2)   When entering the roadway, which is situated around the center part
of the Public Square from 15th Street, J Street, or 15th Street, I Street, in
Bedford, all entering traffic may yield to the traffic already proceeding
around the inside of the Public Square.
(Prior Code, § 5-4-3)
   (B)   (1)   Presently, the intersection of county road L6 S2W and Old S.R.
37 has been designated as a two-way stop intersection under Chapter 5-4-2,
Appendix Schedule A of the Code of Ordinances of the County of Lawrence.
      (2)   From the date of passage of this division (B), the intersection of
county road L6 S2W and Old S.R. 37 shall hereinafter be designated a four-way
stop intersection.
   (C)   (1)   The County Board of Commissioners designates the intersection of
Anderson Road and Bennett Road in Marshall Township, Lawrence County, Indiana
as a three-way stop intersection. This intersection shall be added to Schedule
A listing of Three-Way Stop Intersections under Chapter 5-4-2 of the Lawrence
County Code.
      (2)   The County Board of Commissioners does now hereby direct the County
Highway Superintendent to take all necessary steps to place appropriate “Stop”
signs at appropriate locations at said intersection in order that all motorists
traveling through said intersection are required to stop before proceeding
through.
   (D)   (1)   The intersection located at Stone Crest Drive and Bennett Road
shall be designated a three-way stop. The County Highway Superintendent is
hereby directed to install the necessary “Stop” signs at said intersection.
      (2)   The current configuration at the intersection of Pond Lane/
Williams-Silverville Road/Ridge Road has a “Yield” sign at the northeast corner
of said intersection on Ridge Road. The County Highway Superintendent is hereby
directed to move the “Yield” sign to the southwest corner of said intersection
on Williams-Silverville Road.
      (3)   The current configuration at the intersection of Twin Ponds Road/
Stumphole Bridge Road has a “Yield” sign at the northeast corner of said
intersection at Twin Ponds Road. The County Highway Superintendent is hereby
directed to remove the existing “Yield” sign and place a “Stop” sign at the
southwest corner of said intersection on Stumphole Bridge Road.
   (E)   The intersection located at Walner Quarry Road and Bartlettsville Road
shall be designated a three-way stop. The County Highway Superintendent is
hereby directed to install the necessary “Stop” signs at said intersection.
   (F)   (1)   The intersection located at Walner Quarry Road and
Bartlettsville Road is hereby taken off the list of designated three-way stops
in the county. The north-south thoroughfare known as Bartlettsville Road shall
hereafter not have “Stop” signs stopping the flow of traffic at the
intersection with Walner Quany Road.
      (2)   The intersection of Walner Quarry Road with Bartlettsville Road
shall continue to have a “Stop” sign stopping traffic at Walner Quarry Road
before it enters onto Bartlettsville Road.
   (G)   The intersection of B.J. Boulevard, as it merges into B.J. Boulevard
and Corrielane, shall have a “Stop” sign posted.

SCHEDULE III. DIRECTION OF TRAFFIC.
   (A)   There shall be one-way traffic around the roadway contiguous to the
Public Square in Bedford, Indiana, in a counter-clockwise manner, with traffic
proceeding in a westerly direction on the north side of the square, proceeding
in a southerly manner direction on the west side of the square, proceeding
easterly on the south side of the square, and proceeding in a northerly
direction on the east side of the square.
(Prior Code, § 5-5-1)
   (B)   No person shall operate any motor vehicle, drive or pedal any two-
wheel, or three-wheel, four-wheel (or more) motorcycles, motor bikes, city
bikes, or any other means of transportation, nor shall he or she push or pull,
or cause to be pushes or pulled, any wagon or other means of conveyance or
transportation, in any direction other than that set forth in division (A)
above.
(Prior Code, § 5-5-2)
   (C)   Any person found to have violated the provisions of division (B) above
shall be fine $100 for each such violation and shall further pay the costs of
such action.
(Prior Code, § 5-5-3)
SCHEDULE IV. WEIGHT LIMITS.
   (A)   No person or persons shall cause or permit any vehicle with a maximum
load, including the weight any articles or items being carried by, on or within
such vehicle, to exceed the weight limit for the following bridge or bridges.

   (B)   Any person who violates this schedule shall pay a fine in the sum of
$1,000 together with the cost of any action.
(Prior Code, § 8-11-2)
CHAPTER 72: PARKING SCHEDULES
Schedule
   
I.   Parking at the Courthouse Plaza
SCHEDULE I. PARKING AT THE COURTHOUSE PLAZA.
   (A)   The County Board of Commissioners designate the county-owned parking
lot at the northeast corner of 17th and J Streets as a “permit parking” parking
lot.
   (B)   No person shall stop, stand, or park in the said parking lot without a
permit as authorized in this schedule.
   (C)   A person who stops, stands, or parks in the said parking lot without a
permit shall receive a fine of $50. Each day a violation continues shall
constitute a separate offense. A person fined under this schedule shall pay the
fine within 30 days of the date of the fine. If a person fined under this
schedule fails to pay within 30 days of the date of the fine, he or she shall
owe court costs in addition to the amount of the fine.
   (D)   Employees of the county and the state shall have 30 days from the date
of passage of this schedule to obtain parking permits.
   (E)   The County Commissioners, by and through the County Auditor, shall set
up the process by and through which permits shall be issued and managed.
   (F)   In the event that a person currently employed by the county or the
state obtains a permit and subsequently leaves said employment, said person
shall surrender his or her permit immediately and without delay upon leaving
said employment.
   (G)   In the event that a person not currently employed by the county or the
state obtains such employment, said person may obtain a parking permit as
provided for under this schedule.
   (H)   Department heads and office managers shall be responsible for
obtaining permits from the Auditor for their employees and for returning
permits to the Auditor when their employees leave employment.
   (I)   The County Sheriff, and his or her duly authorized deputies, are
hereby empowered and authorized to enforce this schedule and to issue county
ordinance violation tickets for violations of this schedule. Furthermore, the
County Sheriff, and his or her duly authorized deputies, are hereby empowered
and authorized to remove or have removed any vehicle that is stopped, standing,
or parked in the said parking lot. The owners of said vehicles shall be
responsible for the costs of removal and impoundment.
   (J)   The Auditor is hereby directed to publish this schedule in its
entirety within 30 days of passage in the Bedford, Indiana Times-Mail, and to
post copies of the schedule publicly at the County Courthouse and the
Courthouse Plaza. The Auditor shall forward a copy of this schedule to each
tenant and county office located in the Courthouse Plaza.
   (K)   The Auditor is hereby directed to procure signage for the said parking
lot indicating that the lot is “Permit Only” and that stopping, standing, or
parking in said lot without a permit may result in a fine of $50 plus court
costs for each violation.
   (L)   The Auditor is hereby directed to procure parking permits to issue to
qualified individuals.
(Ord. 2018-03, passed 6-12-2018; Ord. 2018-05, passed 7-24-2018)

Section
Nuisances

**** NUISANCES ****
§ 90.01 MOWING WEEDS.
   (A)   Duty of county.
      (1)   The County Commissioners shall cause the County Highway Department
to cut down and remove, within the limits of the county highway right-of-way,
all briars, thistles, burrs, tree sprouts, docks, willows, sumac, reeds,
cattails, tall grass, marijuana, Indian or wild hemp or loco weed, shrubs, and
all other obnoxious growth within the limits of the county rights-of-way
between June 15 and September 1 each year (I.C. 36-2-18). This is for the
protection of the property owner only.
      (2)   The County Commissioners shall further cause the County Highway
Department to cut and trim down to a height not to exceed five feet once in
each calendar year in all cases where hedges and live fences, or any other
natural growths, are found at a highway intersection or adjacent to any curve
where the view of the highway may be intercepted. Such hedges or live fences
shall be maintained at a height of not to exceed five above the level of the
center of the traveled road bed in the highway adjoining, and such altitude
shall be maintained throughout the year for a distance of 50 feet where the
obstructions consist of natural growths, except trees.
(I.C. 32-26-4-1)
      (3)   (a)   Where boundary lines have never been established by competent
authority or by grant, then the highway is established by use, and the width of
the road so established by use is limited by case law to that portion actually
traveled and excludes any berm or shoulder (Evans v. Bowman, 1915, 183 Ind.
264; 108 NE 956; Anderson v. City of Huntington, 1907, 40 Ind. App. 130, 81 NE
223).
         (b)   Any and all persons who own and/or lease real estate which is
adjacent to a county road right-of-way shall mow and/or cut any and all weeds,
grass, trees, and shrubs as often as is necessary as to not allow any weeds,
grass, and/or shrubs which may be located upon such person’s real estate and
which is within five of such county road right-of-way to extend to a height of
greater than 24 inches at any time.
         (c)   Because of the fact that most rights-of-way for county roads in
the county have came about by “user,” there is no prescribed right-of-way width
for the majority of them. Thus, the property owner’s responsibility may extend
to mowing the edge of such traveled portion of the highway, depending upon
whether there has ever been a grant of right-of-way to the county for the
county road describing a definite width (Board of Commissioners of Monroe
County v. Hatton, 1981, ___ Ind. App. ___; 427 NE2d 696).
         (d)   Any person who has any question as to where the county road
right-of-way is located is required to make the proper inquiries of the County
Highway Department, and the County Highway Department will advise such person
whether or not the county’s right-of-way for the that particular road extends
beyond the traveled surface of the roadway. If it does not, then that person so
owning and/or leasing such real estate is required to keep same trimmed and
mowed as provided for above all the way to the edge of the paved portion of the
highway, which is also the edge of such county road right-of-way.
(Prior Code, § 8-1-1)
   (B)   Responsibility for accidents. Any person or persons so mowing or
trimming his or her real estate in compliance with the above provisions of the
county code shall do so in a careful, cautious manner, and shall not mow or cut
his or her properties in such a manner as might permit or cause any rocks or
debris to be thrown towards persons legally using such county roadways. Such
persons shall be responsible for any and all injuries and damages their actions
might cause, either directly or indirectly, and either proximately, or as an
intervening cause. Further, he or she shall cause such mowing and trimming to
be done cautiously and carefully, and in such a manner that no part of the
mowing or trimming apparatus or the person doing the mowing or trimming extends
upon the county highway right-of-way, due to the possibility of accidents and
injuries.
(Prior Code, § 8-1-2)
§ 90.02 ADVERTISING.
   It is unlawful for any persons, firms, partnerships, organizations,
corporations, associations, or any other entity of any kind whatsoever, to
place, or cause to be placed upon the rights-of-way of the county roads located
in the county, any sign, billboard, advertisement, directional indicator
towards and area for sale or other activity, junked cars, junked trucks,
fencing, or any other type of personal property of any kind whatsoever, within
the boundaries of the rights-of-way of the county road within the county.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-1) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who fails to comply with §
90.01(A)(3)(b) shall be fined not less than $500, to which may be added the
costs of said action, or said County Highway Department may, by written notice,
inform such owner or owners of such real estate, to be served by the Sheriff of
the county, or any of his or her duly appointed deputies, or served by
certified mail, to cut the same down or mow or trim the same to a height not
greater than 24 inches from the ground within seven days after such notice is
served upon him or her. If such owner, once served, has failed to do so, or
should service not be able to be obtained upon such owner due to change of
address, or any other reason, then the County Highway Department may cause the
same to be mowed and trimmed, and the actual cost of the same shall become a
lien upon such real estate in the year in which the same is charged against
such real estate, and the same shall be collected, and such lien enforced by
sale, if necessary, in the same manner as real property tax sales are now
valid.
(Prior Code, § 8-1-1)
   (C)   Any persons, firms, partnerships, organizations, corporations, or
associations, violating the provisions of §
90.02 shall be fined $500, for each separate infraction, together with the
costs of any action. Further, that such violator be required to reimburse the
county for all expenses incurred by said county in the removal or any such
personal property as described herein.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-2)
(Ord. 1982-2, passed 7-2-1983)
