**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 113.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ARTIST. Any person who performs body piercing or tattooing.
   BLOOD. Human blood.
   BLOOD-BORNE PATHOGENS. Pathogenic microorganisms that are present in human
blood and can cause disease in humans. These pathogens include, but are not
limited to, the following:
      (1)   HBV;
      (2)   HCV; and
      (3)   HIV.
   BODY PIERCING. The perforation of any human body part other than an earlobe
for the purpose of inserting jewelry or other decoration or for some other non-
medical purpose.
   BODY-PIERCING FACILITY. Any place used for the purpose of body piercing.
   BRANDING. The burning of skin.
   CLEANED. Removal of all visible dust, soil, or any other foreign material.
   CONTAMINATED. The presence or reasonably anticipated presence of blood or
OPIM (other potentially infectious materials) on an item or surface.
   DECONTAMINATED. The use of physical or chemical means to remove, inactivate,
or destroy blood-borne pathogens on a surface or item which does not require
sterilization to the point where they are no longer capable of transmitting
infectious particles and the surface or item is rendered safe for handling,
use, or disposal.
   DEPARTMENT. The County Department of Health. The County Board of Health
shall be considered part of the DEPARTMENT, except for the purpose of
conducting any type of administrative hearing for the appeal of any decision of
the DEPARTMENT or Health Officer.
   HBV. Hepatitis B virus.
   HCV. Hepatitis C virus.
   HEALTH OFFICER. The duly appointed Health Officer as set forth in I.C. 16-
20-2.
   HIV. The human immunodeficiency virus.
   INFECTIOUS WASTE. Waste that epidemiological evidence indicates is capable
of transmitting a dangerous communicable disease. INFECTIOUS WASTE includes,
but is not limited to, the following:
      (1)   Contaminated sharps or contaminated objects that could potentially
become contaminated sharps;
      (2)   Infectious biological cultures, infectious associated biological,
and infectious agent stock;
      (3)   Pathological waste;
      (4)   Blood and blood products in liquid and semi-liquid form; and
      (5)   Other waste that has been intermingled with infectious waste.
   OPERATOR. Any person who owns, possesses, controls, or has a financial
interest in a body piercing or tattoo parlor.
   OTHER POTENTIALLY INFECTIOUS MATERIALS OR OPIM. The following:
      (1)   Semen;
      (2)   Vaginal secretions;
      (3)   Cerebrospinal fluid;
      (4)   Synovial fluid;
      (5)   Pleurial fluid;
      (6)   Pericardial fluid;
      (7)   Peritoneal fluid;
      (8)   Amniotic fluid;
      (9)   Saliva in dental procedures;
      (10)   Any body fluid that is visibly contaminated with blood; and
      (11)   All body fluids where it is difficult or impossible to
differentiate between body fluids.
   PARENTERAL. Piercing the mucous membranes or the skin barrier through such
events as needlesticks, human bites, cuts, or abrasions.
   PERSON. Any person who is a customer of a body-piercing facility or tattoo
parlor.
   PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT. Specialized clothing or equipment worn for
protection against contact with blood or OPIM.
   SCARIFICATION (SCARRING). Includes, but is not limited to, laying the skin
wide open, via a needle; saturating the area; and the placing of autoclave sand
or other substance into the area to build up a scar.
   SECURE AREA. An area designated and maintained to prevent the entry of
unauthorized persons.
   SEMI-LIQUID BLOOD, BLOOD PRODUCTS. Blood, blood products that have
intermediate fluid properties and are capable of flowing in a manner similar to
liquid.
   STERILIZE. The use of a physical or chemical procedure to destroy all
microbial life, including highly resistant bacterial endospores.
   STORE. The containment of infectious waste in a secure area, in such a
manner as not to constitute collection, treatment, transport, or disposal.
   TATTOO.
      (1)   Any indelible design, letter, scroll, figure, symbol, or other mark
placed with the aid of needles or other instruments;
      (2)   Any design, letter, scroll, figure, or symbol done by scarring upon
or under the skin; or
      (3)   Scarring or branding.
   TATTOO PARLOR. Any room or space where tattooing is provided or where
business of tattooing is conducted.
   UNIVERSAL PRECAUTIONS. An approach to infection control in which all human
blood and certain body fluids are treated as if known to be infectious for HIV,
HBV, HCV, and other blood-borne pathogens.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.02 PROHIBITIONS.
   No person shall do any of the following:
   (A)   Operate a business that offers tattooing or body-piercing services,
unless the County Health Department has approved the business, under 410 I.A.C.
1-5;
   (B)   Perform a tattooing or body-piercing procedure, in a manner that does
not meet the safety and sanitation standards established under Rule 5, 410
I.A.C. 1-5; or
   (C)   Perform tattooing or body-piercing procedure in a manner that does not
meet the standards for appropriate disinfection and sterilization of invasive
equipment or parts of equipment used in performing the procedures established
by this chapter, under Rule 5, I.A.C. 1-5.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 113.03 AGE LIMITATION.
   No person shall perform a tattooing/body-piercing procedure on an individual
who is under 18 years of age.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 113.04 IDENTIFICATION.
   (A)   No individual who is under age 18 years of age shall knowingly show or
give false information concerning his or her name, age, or other identification
for the purpose of obtaining a tattooing or body-piercing service for himself
or herself.
   (B)   Tattoo/body-piercing parlors should require a birth certificate and
picture ID of an individual receiving tattooing services for their own records
and protection.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 113.05 TATTOO, BODY-PIERCING OPERATOR; ESTABLISHMENT TRAINING
RESPONSIBILITIES.
   (A)   Training responsibilities. An individual or entity that is a tattoo/
body-piercing operator shall comply with the following training
responsibilities:
      (1)   Ensure that the training described in the State Occupational Safety
and Health Administration’s Blood-borne Pathogens Standard (as found in 20
C.F.R. § 1910.1030) is provided to all tattoo and body-piercing artists, anyone
employed by the tattoo/body-piercing facility, or anyone acting on behalf of
the tattoo/body-piercing facility who has a reasonably anticipated risk for
skin, eye, mucous membrane, or parenteral contact with blood or OPIM;
      (2)   Ensure that training on the handling of infectious waste is
provided to all tattoo and body-piercing artists, anyone employed by the
tattoo/body-piercing business, or anyone acting on behalf of the tattoo/body-
piercing business who has a reasonably anticipated risk for skin, eye, mucous
membrane, or parenteral contact with blood or OPIM; and
      (3)   Ensure that a record of training described in divisions (A)(1) and
(A)(2) above is maintained. The records shall be made available to the
Department for inspection upon request.
   (B)   Operator responsibilities.
      (1)   The tattoo/body-piercing business operator shall ensure that tattoo
and body-piercing artists, anyone employed by the tattoo/body-piercing
business, or anyone employed by the tattoo/body-piercing business, or anyone
acting on behalf of the business who has a reasonably anticipated risk for
skin, eye, mucous membrane, or parenteral contact with blood are provided
personal protective equipment and expendables needed to implement the
precautions required by this rule and the State Occupational Safety and Health
Administration’s Blood-borne Pathogens Standard (as found in 29 C.F.R. §
1910.1030).
      (2)   (a)   The tattoo/body-piercing operator shall display written
materials prepared or approved by the Department explaining universal
precautions and patron’s rights under this division (B).
         (b)   The materials shall include information on how to report
information regarding the Department’s duties to investigate.
      (3)   The tattoo/body-piercing operator shall ensure that no illicit
drugs or alcohol are consumed in the tattoo/body-piercing parlor.
      (4)   The tattoo/body-piercing operator shall ensure that no tattoo or
body piercing shall be affixed to or performed on any person who is
intoxicated.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.06 OPERATING POLICIES.
   The tattoo/body-piercing operator shall develop a written policy in
compliance with this chapter and the requirements of the State Occupational
Safety and Health Administration’s Blood-Borne Pathogen Standard (as found in
29 C.F.R. § 1910.1030) that:
   (A)   Requires the use of universal precautions when performing tattooing,
body piercing, and/or any activity or duty that includes any reasonably
anticipated skin, eye, mucous membrane, or parenteral contact with blood or
OPIM;
   (B)   Includes the safe handling of infectious waste; and
   (C)   Provides sanctions, including discipline and dismissal, if warranted,
for failure to use universal precautions and/or handle infectious waste safely.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.07 DYES, PIGMENTS.
   (A)   All dyes or pigments in tattooing shall be from professional suppliers
specifically providing dyes or pigments for the tattooing of human skin.
   (B)   In preparing dyes or pigments to be used in tattooing procedures, only
non-toxic sterile materials shall be used. Single use or individual portions of
dyes or pigments in clean, sterilized containers shall be used for each patron.
   (C)   After tattooing, the remaining unused dye or pigment in single use or
individual containers shall be discarded along with the container.
   (D)   (1)   Written instructions shall be given to each patron or customer
on the care of the skin on the tattooed site to prevent infection after each
tattooing.
      (2)   A copy of these instructions shall also be posted in a conspicuous
place in the tattoo establishment, and be clearly visible to the person being
tattooed.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.08 WORK ENVIRONMENT.
   (A)   No tattooing or body piercing shall be conducted in any room used as
living quarters or in any room that opens directly into living or sleeping
quarters.
   (B)   Live animals shall be excluded from areas where tattooing or body
piercing is being conducted. This exclusion does not apply to:
      (1)   Patrol dogs accompanying security or police officers; and
      (2)   Guide dogs accompanying:
         (a)   Blind persons;
         (b)   Partially blind persons;
         (c)   Physically disabled persons;
         (d)   Guide dog trainers; or
         (e)   Persons with impaired hearing.
   (C)   Eating, drinking, smoking, or applying cosmetics shall not be allowed
in work areas where there is a likelihood of exposure to blood or OPIM.
   (D)   Food and drink shall not be kept in area where there is a reasonably
anticipated risk of exposure to blood or OPIM.
   (E)   All equipment and environmental surfaces shall be cleaned and
decontaminated after contact with blood or OPIM.
   (F)   Environmental surfaces and equipment not requiring sterilization that
have been contaminated by blood shall be cleaned and decontaminated.
   (G)   All work surfaces shall be:
      (1)   Non-absorbent;
      (2)   Easily cleanable;
      (3)   Smooth; and
      (4)   Free of:
         (a)   Breaks;
         (b)   Open seams;
         (c)   Cracks;
         (d)   Chips;
         (e)   Pits; and
         (f)   Similar imperfections.
   (H)   Disinfectant solutions shall be:
      (1)   A hospital grade, tuberculocidal Environmental Protection Agency
(EPA) registered disinfectant; or
      (2)   Sodium hypochlorite, 0.5% concentration by volume (common household
bleach is 10% concentration in water); the solution shall be dated and shall
not be used if it is more than 24 hours old.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.09 REGULATIONS GOVERNING SANITATION OF MOBILE TATTOO, BODY PIERCING
BUSINESSES.
   In addition to complying with all of the requirements of this chapter,
mobile tattoo/body-piercing vehicles and operators working from the mobile
tattoo and body-piercing establishment shall also comply with all of the
following requirements.
   (A)   (1)   Mobile tattoo and body-piercing businesses are permitted for use
only at special events, lasting calendar days or less. Permits must be obtained
at least 14 days prior to the event, and no tattoo or body-piercing procedures
are to be performed prior to a permit being issued.
      (2)   Permit holders are responsible for ensuring that all other local
agency regulations are complied with, such as, but not limited to, zoning and
business license requirements.
   (B)   Tattoos and/or body piercings performed pursuant to this section shall
be done only from an enclosed vehicle such as a trailer or mobile home, having
an interior floor space of at least 100 square feet. No tattoos or body-
piercing procedures shall be performed outside of the enclosed vehicle.
   (C)   The mobile tattoo/body-piercing business shall be maintained in a
clean and sanitary condition at all times. Doors shall be self-closing and
tight fitting. Operable windows shall have tight-fitting screens.
   (D)   Mobile tattoo/body-piercing business, must have approved sterilization
equipment available, in accordance with all requirements of §
113.31.
   (E)   The mobile tattoo and body-piercing business shall be used only for
the purpose of performing tattoos or body-piercing procedures. No habitation or
cooking is permitted inside the vehicle.
   (F)   (1)   The mobile tattoo and body-piercing business shall be equipped
with an equipment washing sink and a separate hand sink for the exclusive use
of the operator for hand washing and preparing the client for the tattoo or
body-piercing procedures.
      (2)   An adequate supply of potable water shall be maintained for the
mobile tattoo and body-piercing business at all times during operation.
   (G)   (1)   All liquid wastes shall be stored in an adequate storage tank
with a capacity of at least 50% greater than the capacity of the on board
potable water supply.
      (2)   Liquid wastes shall be disposed of at a site approved by the
Department.
   (H)   A hand sink shall be supplied with potable hot and cold running water
under pressure to a mixing type faucet with antimicrobial liquid soap and paper
towels in dispensers.
   (I)   All tattoo/body-piercing operators working in a mobile tattoo/body-
piercing business must comply with the operator requirements of this chapter.
   (J)   No animals, except service animals of clients, shall be allowed in the
mobile tattoo/body-piercing business at any time.
   (K)   (1)   Mobile tattoo/body-piercing businesses must receive an initial
inspection at a location specified by the Department prior to use to ensure
compliance with structural requirements.
      (2)   Additional inspections will be performed at every event where the
mobile tattoo/body-piercing establishment is scheduled to operate.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.10 TEMPORARY TATTOO, BODY PIERCING ESTABLISHMENTS.
   Temporary tattoo/body-piercing operations are to comply with all aspects of
the provisions relating to temporary permits, health, cleanliness, and personal
hygiene of operators, practices, and procedures for tattoo/body-piercing
establishments and notifications as specified in §§
113.01 through
113.08,
113.32,
113.33, and
113.52.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.11 OPERATOR REQUIREMENTS; PROFESSIONAL STANDARDS.
   (A)   The following information shall be kept on file on the premises of a
tattoo/body-piercing establishment and available for inspection by the
Department;
      (1)   The names of all operators/technicians in the establishment and
their exact duties, including full names, date of birth, gender, home address,
home/work phone numbers, identification photos of all operators/technicians;
      (2)   Establishment name, hours of operation, and the owner’s name and
address;
      (3)   A complete description of all tattoo/body-piercing procedures
performed;
      (4)   An inventory of all instruments and body jewelry, all sharps, and
all inks used for and all tattoo/body-piercing procedures, including names of
manufacturers and serial or lot numbers, if available or applicable; and
      (5)   A copy of this chapter.
   (B)   Permits shall be prominently displayed in the tattoo/body-piercing
establishment and shall not be defaced or altered in any manner.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful for any person to perform tattoo or body-piercing
procedures unless such procedures are performed in a tattoo/body-piercing
establishment with a current permit.
   (D)   The operator must be a minimum of 18 years of age.
   (E)   The operator shall maintain a high degree of personal cleanliness,
conform to hygienic practices and wear clean clothes when performing tattoo or
body-piercing procedures. Before performing tattoos or body piercings, the
operator must thoroughly wash his or her hands in hot running water with liquid
anti-microbial soap, then rinse hands and dry with disposable paper towels.
This shall be done as often as necessary to remove contaminants.
   (F)   In performing tattoos and body-piercing procedures, the operator shall
wear disposable medical gloves. The gloves shall be discarded, at a minimum,
after the completion of each procedure on an individual client.
   (G)   (1)   If, while performing a tattoo or body-piercing procedure, the
operator’s glove is pierced, torn, or otherwise contaminated, the procedures
(in divisions (E) and (F) above) shall be repeated immediately. The
contaminated gloves shall be immediately discarded and the hands washed
thoroughly (see division (E) above) before a fresh pair of gloves are applied.
      (2)   Any item or other instrument used for a tattoo or body piercing,
which is contaminated during the procedure, shall be discarded and replaced
immediately with new ones before the procedure resumes.
   (H)   (1)   Contaminated waste, as defined in this chapter, which may
release liquid blood or body fluids when compressed or may release dried blood
or body fluids when handled must be placed in an approved “red” bag which is
marked with the international biohazard symbol. It must then be disposed of by
a waste hauler approved by the Department.
      (2)   Sharps ready for disposal shall be disposed of in approved sharps
containers.
      (3)   Contaminated waste which does not release liquid blood or body
fluids when compressed or does not release dried blood or body fluids when
handled may be placed in a covered receptacle and disposed of through normal,
approved disposal methods.
      (4)   Storage of contaminated waste on-site shall not exceed the period
specified by the Department or more than a maximum of 30 days.
   (I)   Each tattoo artist and operator should be able to provide the
following information, upon request of the Department, that each tattoo artist
and operator has either completed or been offered and declined, in writing, the
hepatitis B vaccination series; that antibody testing has revealed that the
employee is immune to hepatitis B; or that the vaccine is contraindicated for
medical reasons.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 113.12 EXEMPTIONS.
   (A)   Physicians licensed by the state who utilize tattooing or body-
piercing procedures as part of patient treatment are exempt from these
regulations.
   (B)   Earlobe piercings are exempt.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
**** SANITATION ****
§ 113.25 SANITARY OPERATIONS.
   All places, individuals, and businesses that offer body piercing or affix
any type of permanent tattoo to a person shall be regulated by this chapter and
shall maintain the premises in which tattoos and body piercings are performed,
and maintain equipment used in the tattoo and body-piercing process in a
sanitary manner.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.26 GENERAL SAFETY, SANITARY STANDARDS.
   A business offering tattoo and/or body-piercing services shall comply with
the following provisions.
   (A)   The premises in which tattooing or body piercing is conducted shall
meet state and local building codes and shall consist of an area of at least
100 square feet. The floor space for each individual performing tattoo or body-
piercing services shall be an area of at least 45 square feet. These areas
shall be separated from each other and from waiting patrons or observers by a
panel or by a door. Complete privacy should be available upon a patron’s
request.
   (B)   The entire procedure room and equipment shall be maintained in a
clean, sanitary condition and in good repair.
   (C)   The tattoo/body-piercing business shall be equipped with artificial
light sources equivalent to at least 20 foot-candles at a distance of 30 inches
above the floor throughout the establishment. A minimum of 40 foot-candles of
light shall be provided at a level where the tattooing/body piercing is being
performed. Spotlighting may be used to achieve this required degree of
illumination.
   (D)   All floors directly under equipment used for tattooing and body-
piercing activities shall be of an impervious, smooth, washable surface, have a
minimum dimension of six feet by six feet, and shall be maintained in a
sanitary manner at all times. All walls shall be maintained in a sanitary
manner.
   (E)   All tables and other equipment shall be constructed of easily
cleanable material, with a smooth, washable finish.
   (F)   (1)   Restroom facilities shall be made available to the employees and
customers of the business and must be located within the establishment.
      (2)   The restroom shall be accessible at all times the business is open
for operation.
      (3)   The restroom shall be equipped with a toilet, toilet paper, and
lavatory supplied with potable hot and cold running water, soap, and disposable
towels. Equipment and supplies used in the course of tattoo and body-piercing
services or disinfection and sterilization procedures shall not be stored or
utilized in the restroom.
   (G)   A lavatory or hand washing sink, with potable hot and cold running
water, soap, and disposable towels shall be located in close proximity of each
individual performing a tattoo or body piercing procedure.
   (H)   There shall be no overhead or otherwise exposed sewage lines so as to
create a potential hazard to the sanitary environment of the business.
   (I)   Gloves must be worn during all tattooing and body-piercing procedures.
   (J)   Clean dressing must be applied after completion of each tattoo.
   (K)   Prior to tattooing and body piercing, the operator who will be
performing the procedure shall inquire of a patron for conditions that could
affect the healing process. The operator shall not perform a tattoo or body
piercing on patrons indicating the presence of such a condition without
documentation from a licensed physician, indicating acceptance of the patient
for appropriate care following the procedure.
   (L)   With respect to tattooing services, written records must be
maintained, which include the professional tattooing ink for each tattoo
performed.
   (M)   Comply with the safety and sanitation requirements for preventing
transmission of infectious diseases, as established in Rule 5, 410 I.A.C. 1-5.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.27 ILLNESS.
   Anyone employed by a tattoo/body-piercing parlor or acting in behalf of the
establishment who is experiencing symptoms of acute disease that include, but
are not limited to, what is listed below shall refrain from providing tattoos
or body piercing:
   (A)   Diarrhea;
   (B)   Vomiting;
   (C)   Fever;
   (D)   Potentially infectious rash;
   (E)   Productive cough;
   (F)   Jaundice; or
   (G)   Draining (or open) skin infections, boils, impetigo, or scabies.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.28 HAND WASHING.
   (A)   Hand washing facilities shall be readily accessible in the same room
where tattooing and body piercing are provided.
   (B)   Hands shall be washed with soap and potable hot and cold running water
immediately before putting on sterile gloves and after removal of gloves or
other personal protective equipment.
   (C)   Only single-use towels shall be used.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.29 PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT; OPERATING EQUIPMENT.
   (A)   Personal protective equipment. Appropriate personal protective
equipment shall be worn as follows.
      (1)   A clean protective clothing layer shall be worn whenever there is a
reasonably anticipated risk of contamination of clothing by blood or OPIM.
      (2)   Masks in combination with eye protection devices, such as goggles
or glasses with solid side shield, or chin length face shield, shall be worn
whenever splashes, spray, splatter, or droplets of blood can be reasonably
anticipated.
      (3)   Disposable gloves shall be worn during the tattooing and body-
piercing process. Gloves shall be changed and properly disposed of each time
there is an interruption in the application of a tattoo or body piercing, when
the gloves become torn or punctured, or whenever the ability to function as a
barrier is compromised. Disposable gloves shall not be reused.
      (4)   Gloves shall be worn when decontaminating environmental surfaces
and equipment.
   (B)   Tattooing, body-piercing equipment.
      (1)   Only single-use razors shall be used to shave the are to be
tattooed, or body part to be pierced. Single-use razors shall be discarded in
sharps containers immediately after use.
      (2)   The stencil for transferring a design to the skin is to be single-
use only, and shall be properly disposed of after a single use.
      (3)   After shaving the area to be tattooed or pierced, or if the area
does not need to be shaved, the site of the tattoo or piercing shall be cleaned
with soap and warm water, rinsed with clean water and a germicidal solution
applied in a sanitary manner before piercing, or before a design is placed on
the skin for use during tattooing. Other sterile, individual towels or gauze
shall be used in preparing the site to be tattooed or pierced, and properly
disposed of after use on each patron.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.30 NEEDLES.
   (A)   Needles shall be packaged and sterilized prior to use, for each tattoo
or body piercing.
   (B)   Needles shall be single use only.
   (C)   Needles shall be discarded in sharps containers immediately after use.
   (D)   Contaminated needles shall not be bent or broken or otherwise
manipulated by hand.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.31 REUSABLE EQUIPMENT.
   (A)   Autoclave sterilization must be used when heat stable, non-disposable
equipment is sterilized.
   (B)   Records must be maintained to document:
      (1)   Duration of sterilization technique;
      (2)   Determination of effective sterility, such as use of a biological
indicator, including spores, is performed monthly; and
      (3)   Equipment is maintained as recommended by the owner’s manual and
proof is available that the owner’s manual recommendations are reviewed
monthly.
   (C)   Reusable contaminated equipment shall not be stored or processed in a
manner that requires any person to reach by hand into the containers where the
sharp items have been placed.
   (D)   Reusable contaminated equipment shall be:
      (1)   Placed in puncture-resistant containers;
      (2)   Labeled with the biohazard symbol;
      (3)   Leak-proof on both sides and bottom; and
      (4)   Stored in a manner that does not require reaching by hand into the
container where the equipment is stored until cleaning prior to sterilization.
   (E)   Contaminated reusable equipment shall be effectively cleaned prior to
sterilization.
   (F)   Reusable tubes shall be effectively cleaned and sterilized before
reuse.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.32 INFECTIOUS WASTE CONTAINMENT; TREATMENT, TRANSPORTATION.
   (A)   Infectious waste containment.
      (1)   Contaminated disposable needles or sharp instruments shall be
stored in leak-resistant, puncture-resistant containers, tightly sealed to
prevent expulsion, labeled with the biohazard symbol, and effectively treated
in accordance with this rule prior to being stored in an unsecured area and
sent for final disposal.
      (2)   Infectious wastes other than contaminated sharps or objects that
could potentially become contaminated sharps shall be placed in bags that meet
the following requirements:
         (a)   Impervious to moisture;
         (b)   Sufficient strength and thickness to prevent expulsion;
         (c)   Secured to prevent leakage expulsion;
         (d)   Labeled with the biohazard symbol; and
         (e)   Effectively treated in accordance with this rule prior to being
placed in an unsecured area and sent for final disposal.
      (3)   If infectious waste is stored prior to final disposal, all persons
subject to this rule shall store infectious waste in a secure area that:
         (a)   Is locked or otherwise secured to eliminate access by or
exposure to the general public;
         (b)   Afforded protection from adverse environmental conditions and
vermin; and
         (c)   Has a prominently displayed biohazard symbol.
      (4)   Infectious waste shall be stored in a manner that preserves the
integrity of the container, and is not conducive to rapid microbial growth and
putrefaction.
      (5)   Reusable containers must be disinfected against infectious waste
each time that they are emptied, unless the surfaces of the reusable containers
have been protected from contamination by disposable liners, bags, or other
devices that are removed with the infectious waste.
   (B)   Treatment, transportation of infectious waste.
      (1)   All tattoo/body-piercing operators shall ensure that infectious
waste is either treated on-site in accordance with this rule, or transported
off-site for treatment in accordance with this rule.
      (2)   A treatment is effective if it reduces the pathogenic qualities of
infectious waste for safe handling, is designed for the specific waste
involved, and is carried out in a manner consistent with this rule. Effective
treatment may include:
         (a)   Incineration in an incinerator designed to accommodate
infectious waste;
         (b)   Steam sterilization;
         (c)   Chemical disinfecting under circumstances where safe handling of
the waste is assured;
         (d)   Thermal inactive;
         (e)   Irradiation; or
         (f)   Discharge in a sanitary sewer or septic system that is properly
installed and operating in accordance with state and local laws.
      (3)   All persons subject to this rule shall:
         (a)   Transport infectious waste in a manner that reasonably protects
waste haulers and the public from contracting a dangerous communicable disease;
and
         (b)   Effectively treat infectious waste in accordance with this rule
before it is compacted.
      (4)   The tattoo/body-piercing operator shall ensure that infectious,
effectively treated or not, is transported off-site in compliance with 410
I.A.C. 1-3.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.33 POST TATTOO, BODY PIERCING INFECTION PREVENTION.
   (A)   Each tattoo/body-piercing business is to provide instruction to its
clients in methods to prevent infection, such as the use of bactericidal creams
and ointments and soap, and appropriate barrier dressings where indicated.
   (B)   Each client should be instructed to seek immediate medical attention
should there be any evidence of infection.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
**** LICENSING, PERMITS ****
§ 113.45 GENERAL.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any individual to perform tattooing and/or
body piercing upon any person within the county without possessing a current
and valid artist license issued by the County Health Department.
   (B)   The artist license shall be prominently displayed in the tattoo/body-
piercing establishment and/or mobile tattoo/body-piercing establishment and
shall not be defaced or altered in any manner.
(Ord. 2012-5, passed 7-31-2012) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 113.46 LICENSE PERIOD.
   An artist license shall be issued for a term beginning January 1 and/or
before commencement of operation, and expiring December 31 of the same year and
shall be applied for by the artist annually.
(Ord. 2012-5, passed 7-31-2012)
§ 113.47 LICENSE CONTENT.
   An artist license issued by the Health Officer shall contain:
   (A)   Name of the artist;
   (B)   Whether the artist has a “regular” or “temporary” status; and
   (C)   The issuance and expiration date(s).
(Ord. 2012-5, passed 7-31-2012)
§ 113.48 REQUIREMENTS FOR LICENSE.
   (A)   Regular artist license. An applicant applying for a regular artist
license shall submit to the Department proof that the artist:
      (1)   Is at a minimum of 18 years of age;
      (2)   Has successfully completed training described in the Indiana
Occupational Safety and Health Administration’s Blood-Borne Pathogens Standard
(20 C.F.R. § 1910.1030); and
      (3)   Has passed a written exam administered by the County Health
Department with a passing grade of 70% or better, and:
         (a)   Has successfully worked at a permitted tattoo/body-piercing
establishment in the county with a temporary license for a period of not less
than six months under the supervision of an artist that holds a valid regular
artist license; or
         (b)   Has worked in a licensed tattoo and/or body piercing
establishment for a minimum of two years. Proof must be submitted to the
Department and may include a copy of establishment license, tax records, or
other similar documents. The Health Officer shall have final approval on other
forms of documentation submitted as evidence or proof.
   (B)   Temporary license. An applicant applying for a temporary license shall
submit to the Department proof that the artist:
      (1)   Is at a minimum of 18 years of age;
      (2)   Has successfully completed training described in the Indiana
Occupational Safety and Health Administration’s Blood-Borne Pathogens Standard
(20 C.F.R. § 1910.1030); and
      (3)   Will work under the direct supervision of an artist with a valid
regular artist license.
(Ord. 2012-5, passed 7-31-2012)
§ 113.49 RENEWAL OF LICENSE.
   All licenses shall expire on December 31 of each year. Applications for
renewal of a license shall be submitted on the form provided by the Department
before December 31 of each year. Failure to renew an artist license can cause
the tattoo/body-piercing establishment to have its permit suspended or revoked.
(Ord. 2012-5, passed 7-31-2012)
§ 113.50 LATE FEES.
   (A)   A late fee shall be assessed as set by the County Health Department
for failure to acquire a permit or artist license prior to the operation of a
tattoo/body-piercing establishment and/or mobile tattoo/body-piercing
establishment.
   (B)   A late fee shall be assessed as set by the County Health Department
for failure to renew a permit or artist license within 30 days after its
expiration date.
(Ord. 2012-5, passed 7-31-2012)
§ 113.51 REVOCATION.
   The Health Officer may suspend or revoke the permit of any tattoo/body-
piercing business for any period of time for any violation of this chapter/
state or federal regulations concerning blood-borne pathogens, tattoo, body-
piercing, or work place regulations (IOSHA). The suspension and/or revocation
shall be effective upon issuance of the Health Officer. The establishment may
have the permit reinstated upon compliance and to the satisfaction of the
Health Officer. Appeals of orders of revocation shall be conducted pursuant to
I.C. 4-21.5-3-1 et seq. The Board of Health shall conduct administrative
hearings concerning the suspension or revocation of any permit issued herein as
set forth in I.C. 4-21.5-3 et seq.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002; Ord. 2012-5, passed 7-31-2012)
§ 113.52 PERMIT REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   Permanent establishment permit.
      (1)   No person, firm, partnership, joint venture, association, business
trust, corporation, or any organized group of persons may operate a tattoo/
body-piercing establishment unless it has received a tattoo/body-piercing
establishment permit from the Department.
      (2)   Any person operating a tattoo/body-piercing establishment shall
obtain a permit from the Department, annually, with beginning/ending permit
time frames and fees to be set by the Department.
      (3)   The applicant shall pay a fee as set by the Department’s fee
ordinance, for each tattoo/body-piercing establishment permit.
      (4)   A tattoo/body-piercing establishment permit shall not be
transferable from one place or person to another.
      (5)   A current tattoo/body-piercing establishment permit shall be posted
in a prominent and conspicuous area where clients may readily observe it.
   (B)   Temporary establishment permit. Temporary establishment permits may be
issued for tattoo/body-piercing services provided outside of the physical site
of a certified facility for the purposes of product demonstrations, industry
trade shows, or for educational reasons.
      (1)   Temporary establishment permits will not be issued unless:
         (a)   The applicant is currently affiliated with a fixed location of
permanent facility which, where applicable, is permitted by the appropriate
state and/or local jurisdiction; and
         (b)   The temporary site complies with temporary establishment
requirements of this regulation.
      (2)   Temporary establishment permits expire after 14 days, or the
conclusion of the special event, whichever is less.
      (3)   Temporary establishment permit will not be issued unless the
applicant has paid the annual permit fee as set by the Department’s fee
ordinance.
      (4)   (a)   Temporary establishment permits must be obtained prior to
each temporary event held in this county.
         (b)   Fees are not assessed for temporary establishment permits once
an annual permit fee is paid for the calendar year.
      (5)   The temporary establishment permit shall not be transferable from
one place or person to another.
      (6)   The temporary establishment permit shall be posted in a prominent
and conspicuous area where patrons may readily observe them.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
**** ADMINISTRATION, RECORDS ****
§ 113.65 IOSHA TRAINING.
   (A)   It is the responsibility of the tattoo/body-piercing parlor to assure
that anyone employed by the establishment, and anyone acting on behalf of the
tattoo/body-piercing parlor, who has a reasonably anticipated risk for skin,
eye, mucous membrane, or parenteral contact with blood or OPIM shall complete
the training program that is required under the requirements of the State
Occupational Safety and Health Administration’s Blood-Borne Pathogen Standard
(as found in 29 C.F.R. § 1910.1030). The programs under this section shall be
as follows:
      (1)   A blood-borne pathogen training session provided by the tattoo/
body-piercing operator meeting the requirements under the Indiana Occupational
Safety and Health Administration (IOSHA) (as found in 29 C.F.R. § 1910.1030).
      (2)   Any blood-borne pathogen continuing education program accredited by
a health care licensing entity.
   (B)   Anyone employed by the tattoo/body-piercing parlor or acting on behalf
of the tattoo/body-piercing parlor who has a reasonably anticipated risk for
skin, eye, mucous membrane, or parenteral contact with blood or OPIM must be
trained in the tattoo/body-piercing parlor’s policies on the handling of
infectious waste.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.66 PATRON RECORDS.
   Records of each patron shall be maintained for two years. The record shall
include:
   (A)   Patron’s name;
   (B)   Address;
   (C)   Age: must be verified by one photo identification item or two non-
photo identification items;
   (D)   Date tattooing or body-piercing was performed;
   (E)   Design of the tattoo;
   (F)   Location of the tattoo or piercing on the patron’s body;
   (G)   Name and description of decorative implement or other implement placed
into the pierced body area; and
   (H)   The name of the tattoo/body-piercing artist who performed on any minor
as permitted by law.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
§ 113.67 ACCESS TO RECORDS AND INSPECTIONS.
   (A)   The County Health Department, or a designated representative thereof,
may inspect the premises of any tattoo parlor or body-piercing facility a
minimum of two times per year at any time the facility is open for business
and, upon notice, at other times as may be arranged by agreement to determine
compliance with this chapter and any other applicable health codes or
standards.
   (B)   All records, manuals, certifications, and other documents required to
be kept in accordance to this chapter shall be subject to inspection by the
County Health Department or a designated representative thereof at any time the
tattoo parlor or body-piercing facility is open for business and, upon notice,
at such other times as may be arranged by agreement.
   (C)   The results of each inspection shall be provided to each operator.
Violations noted by the Health Department shall be corrected immediately. The
Department shall conduct follow-up inspections to determine compliance with
this chapter.
   (D)   The County Health Officer may bring an action in the Circuit or
Superior Court to enforce this chapter. The Health Officer shall be entitled to
recover all costs and expenses associated with any action for enforcement of
this chapter including reasonable attorney’s fees.
(Ord. 2002-03, passed - -2002)
CHAPTER 114: LANDFILL OPERATIONS
Section
   
114.01   Fees
   
114.02   Usage
   
114.03   Landfill operator
   
114.04   Collection of fees
   
114.05   Commercial trash haulers
 
   
114.99   Penalty
§ 114.01 FEES.
   (A)   (1)   Residents of the county may dump refuse in the landfill free of
charge as long as said refuse and trash (except wrecked buildings) was their
personal property and not that of another.
      (2)   Commercial trash haulers and other businesses must pay for the
dumping of refuse in the county landfill in accordance with the following
schedule:
         (a)   Pick-up trucks and two-wheel trailers: $3;
         (b)   One-ton trucks: $5;
         (c)   One- to two-ton trucks and pick-up trucks: $10;
         (d)   Tandem open trucks and larger: $15;
         (e)   Roll off dumpsters: $25;
         (f)   Semi-trailer trucks: $30; and
         (g)   Compactors (private, public, or school): $1.75 per cubic yard.
   (B)   All individuals must pay in accordance with the above rates for the
dumping of wrecked buildings, regardless if they are a private, individual, or
commercial trash hauler or business. Use of the term TIPPING FEES is used to
mean “use charge” as that same term is used in other state counties. This does
not mean a “tip” as the common term signifying a gratuity charge denotes.
(Prior Code, § 8-3-1) (Ord. 1985-, passed 1-22-1985)
§ 114.02 USAGE.
   No persons, businesses, or corporations may use the county landfill for the
dumping of solid waste, including, but not limited to, industrial or
manufactured bi-products, except as permissions might be granted therefor by
the Board of Commissioners of the county with prior permission therefor having
been obtained by the State Board of Health.
(Prior Code, § 8-3-1) (Ord. 1985-, passed 1-22-1985) Penalty, see §
114.99
§ 114.03 LANDFILL OPERATOR.
   The individual designated by the Board of Commissioners of the county as the
landfill operator can refuse to allow any load be dumped in the county landfill
which he or she feels would constitute a hazard by incompatibility with the
landfill techniques being used, or constitute an excessive burden upon the
landfill.
(Prior Code, § 8-3-1) (Ord. 1985-, passed 1-22-1985)
§ 114.04 COLLECTION OF FEES.
   The Auditor of the county is hereby empowered to make the necessary
arrangements to collect all fees for dumping at the county landfill and all
fees are to be paid to him or her directly at the Auditor’s office in the
Courthouse, in Bedford, Indiana, and not at the landfill site itself.
(Prior Code, § 8-3-1) (Ord. 1985-, passed 1-22-1985)
§ 114.05 COMMERCIAL TRASH HAULERS.
   Any commercial trash hauler or business who would have to pay a fee for
dumping at the county landfill under the terms of this chapter shall not dump
any trash at any of the portable dumpster sites maintained by the Board of
Commissioners of the county in and around the county.
(Prior Code, § 8-3-1) (Ord. 1985-, passed 1-22-1985) Penalty, see §
114.99
§ 114.99 PENALTY.
   Any person, firm, corporation, partnership, or entity found to have violated
any provision of this chapter shall be subject to a civil fine and penalty in
the amount of $50.
(Res. passed 6-1-1993)
TITLE XIII: GENERAL OFFENSES
   Chapter

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 130.01 USE OF ALCOHOL AT COUNTY COURTHOUSE.
   No person shall use, consume, or dispose of alcoholic beverages and/or the
containers or bottles in which the same are or were kept upon the premises of
the Courthouse, the Courthouse square, the Courthouse annex, the County
Detention center, or the environs thereof.
(Res. passed 6-1-1993) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.02 LOITERING.
   No personal shall loiter about the roads, streets, alleys, railroad
stations, or any other public places or places of public resort of the county,
nor shall congregate in such places in such a manner as to interfere with or
disturb other persons in their lawful activities upon said premises.
(Prior Code, § 8-24-1) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.03 CURFEW AT COUNTY COURTHOUSE.
   (A)   There shall be a curfew placed upon any persons being on the County
Courthouse grounds between the hours of 9:00 p.m. and 6:00 a.m. each day.
   (B)   The Courthouse grounds shall include all of the property in and about
the Courthouse extending to but not including the sidewalk bordering the
Courthouse square.
   (C)   The Board of Commissioners of the county may waive the restrictions of
this chapter upon request for special circumstances and it shall not apply to
any county officials or employees, or visitors to their offices, while
conducting county business.
(Prior Code, § 8-13-2) (Ord. 1982-2, passed 7-19-1982)
**** FIREARMS ****
§ 130.15 USE OF FIREARMS AT COUNTY COURTHOUSE.
   (A)   Definition. For the purpose of this section, the following definition
shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different
meaning.
      WEAPON.
         (a)   Includes, but is not limited to, any of the following:
            1.   Club;
            2.   Deadly weapon, as defined by I.C. 35-41-1-9;
            3.   Firearm, as defined by I.C. 35-47-1-5;
            4.   Electric stun weapon, as defined by I.C. 35-47-8-1;
            5.   EXPLOSIVE refers to any agent or substance tending or serving
to explode. EXPLOSIVE shall include, but not be limited to, gunpowder and
dynamite;
            6.   Knife;
            7.   Stun gun, as defined by I.C. 35-47-8-2; and
            8.   Taser, as defined by I.C. 35-47-8-3.
         (b)   WEAPON shall also include any other object that in the manner in
which it is used, or could ordinarily be used, or is intended to be used, is
readily capable of causing SERIOUS BODILY INJURY (as defined by I.C. 35-41-1-
25) as determined by the County Sheriff’s officers.
   (B)   Restrictions. Except as provided in division (C) below, a person shall
not possess on or about his or her person a weapon within the County Courthouse
and Courthouse Plaza.
   (C)   Exceptions. The following persons are exempt from this section: law
enforcement officers under I.C. 35-41-1-17, the State Department of Correction
officers, community correction officers, judicial officers, and probation
officers who are in the Courthouse for official business and duly authorized to
carry deadly weapons. Employees of the Courthouse who carry chemical spray
devices for personal protection are also exempt. The persons described as
exempt from this division (C) shall not be exempt if they or any member of
their family is a party to any proceeding taking place in court.
(Ord. 2013-01, passed 3-26-2013) Penalty, see §
130.99
**** SALE, USE, POSSESSION OF SYNTHETIC CANNABINOID ****
§ 130.30 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   SYNTHETIC CANNABINOID. Any of the following:
      (1)   All or parts of the plant presently classified botanically as
salvia divinorum, whether growing or not, any extract from any part of such
plant, and every compound, manufacture, salts derivative, mixture, or
preparation of such plant, its seeds, or its extracts;
      (2)   HU-210, a substance also known as (6aR, 10aR)-9-(Hydroxymethyl-6,
6-dimethyl-3-(2-methyloctan-2-yl)-6a, 7, 10, 10a-tetrahydrobenzo[c]chromen-1-
ol;
      (3)   JWH-018, a substance also known as Naphthalen-1-yl-(1-pentylindol-
3-yl) methanone;
      (4)   JWH-073, a substance also known as Naphthalen-1-yl-(butylindol-3-
yl) methanone;
      (5)   TFMPP, a substance also known as 1-[3-(triflouromethyl)phenyl]
piperazine;
      (6)   Cannabicyclohexanol, a substance also known as 2-[(1R,
3S)3hydroxycyclohexyl] 5-(2-methylnonan-2-yl)phenol;
      (7)   CP47, 497, a substance also known as 2-[(1R, 3S)3hydroxycyclohexyl]
5-(2-methyloctan-2-yl)phenol;
      (8)   JWH-250, a substance also known as 2-(2-methoxyphenyl)-1-(1-
pentylindol-3-yl)ethanone;
      (9)   Structural analogs of any of these substances; and
      (10)   Any herbal-based substance sold under the name of K2, Spice,
Acapulco Spices, Serenity Now, Spice Gold, Shokotsu, Afghan Incense, Baked,
Black Magic, Buzz, Cherry Charm, Fire Bird, Fire “N” Ice, Pulse, Solitude, or
Voodoo, or any other herbal-based substance containing any of the foregoing
described substances.
(Ord. 2010-07, passed 11-23-2010)
§ 130.31 SALE.
   A person my not sell, barter, give, publicly display for sale, or attempt to
sell, give, or barter, or possess any synthetic cannabinoid.
(Ord. 2010-07, passed 11-23-2010) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.32 ENFORCEMENT.
   (A)   The County Sheriff’s Department is responsible for the interpretation
and civil enforcement of this subchapter.
   (B)   Law enforcement personnel may seize and destroy synthetic cannabinoids
that are in violation of this subchapter.
(Ord. 2010-07, passed 11-23-2010)
§ 130.33 DEFENSES.
   (A)   It is a defense under this subchapter that a person otherwise in
violation is acting at the direction of an authorized agent of the County
Sheriff’s Department to enforce or ensure compliance with this subchapter.
   (B)   It is a defense under this subchapter that a person otherwise in
violation is acting, with respect to the violation, under the direction or
prescription of a person who holds an unlimited license to practice medicine
under I.C. 22-22.5 or a license to practice dentistry under I.C. 25-14.
   (C)   It is a defense under this subchapter that a person otherwise in
violation is acting, with respect to the violation, in connection with a bona
fide research or scientific endeavor funded by public entities or non-profit
organizations.
(Ord. 2010-07, passed 11-23-2010)
§ 130.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who shall violate §
130.01 shall pay a fine of up to $500, together with the costs of any actions.
   (C)   Any person who violates §
130.02 shall pay a fine of $500, together with costs of any actions.
(Prior Code, § 8-24-2)
   (D)   Any person violating the provisions of §
130.03 shall pay a fine of $500, together with the costs of any actions.
(Prior Code, § 8-13-2)
   (E)   Any person violating §
130.15(B) shall be subject to punishment for indirect contempt of court
pursuant to I.C. 34-47-3 and a fine not to exceed $2,500 pursuant to I.C. 34-
28-5-4 and 36-1-3-8. Any person violating §
130.15(B) shall also be subject to having the firearm or weapon confiscated by
the proper law enforcement officials.
   (F)   A person in violation of §§
130.30 through
130.33 shall be fined $250. Each day a violation occurs or continues
constitutes a separate offense.
(Ord. 1982-2, passed 7-19-1982; Res. passed 6-1-1993; Ord. 2010-07, passed 11-
23-2010; Ord. 2013-01, passed 3-26-2013)

**** COUNTY HIGHWAYS, STREETS ****
§ 150.01 MINIMUM REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   No roadway shall be accepted into the county highway system to be
maintained by the Board of Commissioners without the same first meeting the
following minimum specifications and requirements.
      (1)   All streets, roads, and highways in subdivisions (plats which have
been recorded and approved by and bonded to the Board of Commissioners, or unto
the county prior to the effective date of this subchapter), shall conform to
all specifications, standards, and requirements of such ordinances and bonds as
were in effect at such time.
      (2)   All streets, roads, and highways, the right-of-way of which is
dedicated by plat, survey, or other legal instrument recorded prior to the
effective date of this subchapter, but not bonded to the Board of Commissioners
or to the county as required by ordinances in effect at the time of such
recording, shall be required to be constructed in accordance with the
standards, specifications, and requirements of Section I and II of this
Ordinance.
      (3)   All streets, roads, and highways, the right-of-way of which is
designated on unrecorded plats, unrecorded surveys, or other unrecorded legal
instruments of dedications, shall be required to be constructed in accordance
with the specifications, requirements, and standards of Section I and II of
this Ordinance, and shall further be approved by the Board of Commissioners of
the county prior to recording. Provided, however, that all streets, roads, and
highways in subdivisions, the plats of which have not been recorded, but which
have been approved previously by the Board of Commissioners, or which have been
officially filed for plat approval with the Board of Commissioners of the
county prior to the effective date of this subchapter, shall conform to and be
bound in accordance with the specifications, standards, and requirements of the
ordinances which were in effect as of the date of such filing for approval
prior to recording.
(Prior Code, § 3-2-1)
   (B)   The minimum requirements for acceptance into the county highway system
are as follows:
      (1)   Right-of-way. Fifty feet minimum, 25 feet on either side of the
centerline;
      (2)   Pavement width. The width of the surface shall be a minimum of 24
feet;
      (3)   Shoulders.
         (a)   The width of the shoulders shall be four feet on each side of
the pavement. Provided, however, that a minimum width of three feet on each
side of the pavement may be permitted under the exceptional circumstances and
this deviation must be only upon the Board of Commissioners giving its written,
signed approval.
         (b)   The slope of the shoulders shall be a minimum of one-half inch
per foot and a maximum of one inch per foot.
         (c)   The materials for shoulders shall be #53 compacted aggregate
base conforming to State Highway Commission specifications.
      (4)   Subgrade.
         (a)   Unless otherwise provided, the first five inches below the
surface shall be compacted to at least 100% of the maximum dry density as
determined by the provisions of AASHOOT 99, as modified in 203.24.
         (b)   During the subgrade preparation and after its completion,
adequate drainage shall be provided at all times to prevent water from standing
on the subgrade.
         (c)   Unless otherwise provided, the roadbed below the subgrade shall
be so constructed that it will have, as nearly as possible, uniform density
throughout. In both cuts and fills, it shall be rolled with a three-wheeled
roller weighing at least ten tons or with other approved compacting equipment
capable of providing a smooth, even subgrade surface. In areas not accessible
to the roller or other equipment, the required compaction shall be obtained
with mechanical tamps or vibrators. In any case, the upper six inches shall
comply with embankment density requirements of the contract immediately prior
to placing the materials thereon.
         (d)   All soft, yielding, or other unsuitable material which will not
compact readily when approximately dry shall be removed. All rock encountered
shall either be removed or broken off to conform with the required cross-
section.
         (e)   Any holes or depressions resulting from the removal of
unsuitable material shall be filled with satisfactory material and compacted to
conform with the surrounding subgrade surface.
         (f)   Paving material shall not be placed before the subgrade is
checked and approved and at no time when the subgrade is frozen or muddy.
         (g)   No hauling shall be done nor equipment moved over the subgrade
when its condition is such that undue distortion results. If these conditions
are present, the subgrade shall be protected with adequate plank runways, mats,
or other satisfactory means if hauling is done thereon.
         (h)   If limits of the work make mechanical preparation of the
subgrade impractical, hand methods may be used.
         (i)   The condition of the subgrade as finally accepted and meeting
all of the above criteria must continue to exist and prevail from the time that
any paving material is placed thereon and continuing thereafter until all
paving material has been completed in order that the paving operation shall not
be delayed.
      (5)   Drainage.
         (a)   All backslopes shall be a minimum of one to four grade. They
shall be seeded or sodded.
         (b)   All side ditches with over a 3% grade shall be of concrete
construction for such side ditch. Where there is sodding or seed, it also must
be limed and fertilized before seeding. The same shall be maintained by the
petitioner so seeking the acceptance of the road into the highway system.
         (c)   The minimum shall be 15 inches square aluminum or corrugated
metal pipe. Squash is an arch type pipe. Length shall be the minimum under
property entrance shall be 24 feet or longer if necessary to maintain proper
fill slope. Drainage areas for all structures under the main roadway must be
calculated in order for correct size of structures to be placed and this must
be done by a qualified engineer or “professional engineer” (for example, some
land surveyors are also “professional engineers.” A land surveyor who is not
also a professional engineer shall not be so qualified under this division (B)
(5).) Furthermore, the plans so drawn up by such engineer shall include
calculations of drainage area and structure size, and such plans shall be
furnished to the Board of Commissioners of the county prior to any work being
commenced.
         (d)   The length of structures under the main roadway shall be as
required by fill base dimensions. The structure shall be aligned and provided
with end structures or rip rap as required to prevent erosion.
      (6)   Aggregate base. After the preparation of the subgrade has been
completed to the following requirements.
         (a)   Four inches of #53 stone conforming to the ISHC specifications
shall be placed. This shall be rolled with a ten-ton roller and compacted to
100% dry density. After this, four inches of stone shall be applied and rolled
to specification, then another four-inch layer shall be applied and rolled to
100% density. The above material will total an eight-inch depth. The same shall
be placed a total width of 30 feet.
         (b)   The crown shall conform to the finished surface, one-half inch
per foot. The total shall equal three inches in 12 feet.
      (7)   Bituminous surface.
         (a)   The aggregate base will be primed with back asphalt MC-70, AEP,
tar, RT-1, or RT-2 for prime coat. The same shall be applied from 0.50 to 0.8
gallons per square yard, depending on the condition of the surface. No course
shall be placed on a primed surface until bituminous material has penetrated
and the surface is dry and until the aggregate base has been check and
approved. Provided, however, that other types of surfacing and priming
materials may be used if they are approved in writing and signed by the Board
of Commissioners or its delegated representative prior to any obligation being
incurred for the payment of the same.
         (b)   Material for surface shall be hot asphalt concrete pavement type
B (using #11 stone).
         (c)   Thickness shall be bituminous pavement and shall be a minimum of
five inches total depth, three and one-half inch of #5 base, and one and one-
half inch of #11 surface, all after compaction has been completed.
         (d)   Compaction shall be by a ten-ton roller.
         (e)   Placement shall be by machine in one lay.
         (f)   Unless otherwise approved by the Board of Commissioners of the
county or its delegated representative, all materials and workmanship shall
conform to the current issue of “Standard Specifications of Indiana,” State
Highway Department.
      (8)   Ditches. Minimum of one foot below the outer edge of the shoulder
on a four to one slope, and of sufficient size to carry the surface water. A
paved side ditch will be required on slopes over 3%. Such a paved side ditch
must conform to ISHC specifications and shall be approved in writing by the
County Highway Engineer, the County Commissioners, or any personnel whom they
may hire to make such determination. Minimum side slope shall be 0.5%. (The
County Commissioners may not have a full-time County Engineer. In which case,
the County Commissioners may seek the advice of any other qualified engineer in
order to make such determination, whether or not he or she is actually being
paid by the county for that particular purpose.)
      (9)   Design for turn arounds for dead-end streets. Where any street or
road or highway so petitioned to be accepted by the Board of Commissioners of
the county shall have a part of the street, road, or highway that ends in a
dead-end, then the following shall be the minimum standards for right-of-way
and blacktopping according to the detail set forth as follows, and such roadway
shall be constructed according to such minimum standards: [DIAGRAM FOUND TITLE
THREE, CHAPTER 3, SECTION 2, SUBSECTION 3-2-2(9a), P. 79.];
      (10)   Interference with sight lines at intersections. No fences, wall
hedge, or shrub planting which obstructs sight lines at elevations between two
and six feet above the street shall be placed or permitted to retain any corner
lot within the triangular area formed by the street property lines (which
comprise two sides of the triangle) and a line drawn between two points which
are 25 feet from the intersection of said street line (which line comprises of
the remaining third side of the triangle), or in the case of a rounded property
corner, from the intersection of the street lines extended. The same sight line
limitation shall apply to any lot within ten feet from the intersection of a
street line with the edge of a driveway pavement or alley line. No tree, bush,
or other object, either animate or inanimate, shall be permitted to remain
within such distances of such intersection unless the foliage line is
maintained by the owner of such property (or one of the owners) at sufficient
height to prevent obstruction of such lines;
      (11)   Sight line distances at intersections. Further, no road or street
hereunder shall intersect an existing county road with a stopping sight
distance on the county road or proposed road of less than indicated in AASHOT
197a, as amended, geometric design guide for local roads and streets maximum
grade of highways, roadways, or streets;
      (12)   Maximum grade of roadway. No road, street, or highway shall be
accepted into the County Highway System that has more than 6% grade;
      (13)   Street signs. All street signs for roadways shall be as follows.
         (a)   Size of materials. The post which supports such street signs
must be a minimum of two-inch galvanized pipe, ten feet minimum in length to
the point where the street signs themselves attach to the pole.
         (b)   Street signs. Street signs must be six inches wide and 24 inches
long; be of aluminum material normally used for such purposes; and must bear
four-inch letting of “Scotchlite” or other equivalent letters. There shall be
two signs, one affixed immediately above the other, in a crossways fashion so
that the person driving on one of the two roads at such intersection shall be
able to see only the name of the road upon which they are driving.
         (c)   Method of installing. A hole must be dug at least 30 inches deep
and the post placed in the center of such hole in such a manner so that 30
inches of said post are underground. Concrete shall be placed in the hole and
allowed to dry. When the concrete has dried, that part of the post which
remains above ground shall be seven feet six inches long, and the signs be
placed and secured firmly atop that post. The bottom of the lowest sign shall
thus be six inches above ground level immediately under the street sign.
         (d)   Diagram of street sign. [DIAGRAM FOUND TITLE THREE, CHAPTER 3,
SECTION 2, SUBSECTION 3-2-2(9a), P. 81.]
      (14)   Fill areas, cuts, and fills.
         (a)   A fill area of five feet or less shall have a side slope of four
to one (four feet horizontal to one foot vertical). Cut slopes shall be no
steeper than two to one (two feet horizontal to one foot vertical).
         (b)   Cuts and fills which are greater than five feet vertical shall
be reviewed by the County Highway Superintendent or County Engineer. To ensure
that adequate easement is available for future maintenance by the county,
slopes should not be steeper than two to one (two feet horizontal to one foot
vertical) in any case except where rock is excavated.
(Prior Code, § 3-2-2)
(Ord. 73-1, passed 6-3-1973; Ord. passed 6-17-1974; Ord. 2021-03, passed 3-30-
2021)
§ 150.02 OWNER COMPLIANCE.
   (A)   On and after taking effect of this subchapter, the Board of
Commissioners of the county shall not approve or accept into the County Highway
System for maintenance any streets, roads, or highways covered by the
specification, standards, and requirements herein set out, until and unless the
applicant (petitioner) has certified that he or she have met these standards:
      (1)   Posted prior to field construction, a construction bond in the
amount of 100% of the total cost from construction plans filed with and
approved by the County Highway Department. Further, said applicant has posted a
one-year maintenance bond, in the amount of 20% of the improvement bond which
warrants the workmanship and all materials used in the construction,
installation, and completion, to be of good quality and in accordance with the
standards, specifications, and requirements of this subchapter; said
maintenance bond shall be in full force and effect for a period of one year
from the date said streets, roads, and highways are approved or accepted in the
County Highway System. Moreover, said maintenance bond shall warrant and
guarantee that the applicant will, at his or her own expense, make all repairs
to said improvements and installations which may become necessary by reason of
improper workmanship or materials;
      (2)   Has furnished an irrevocable letter of credit in form as follows
(see Appendix E for a copy of sample “Irrevocable Letter of Credit” which is
incorporated by reference herein);
      (3)   Same to be furnished with a bank located in the county.
(Prior Code, § 3-2-3)
   (B)   No plat submitted to the Board of Commissioners of the county shall be
approved for recording until such time as the construction bond or irrevocable
letter of credit referred to in division (A)(2) above is actually on file and
in force in the office of the Auditor of the county.
   (C)   No plat submitted to the Board of Commissioners of the county shall be
approved for recording until the owner or developer of the real estate subject
of the plat has fixed a completion date or a review date for completion or
progress towards completion of said project. No plat shall hereafter be
approved where the owner or developer has an open-ended timetable for
completion and approval by the county of the roads laid out in said plat.
   (D)   No plat submitted to the Board of Commissioners of the county for
approval for recording shall make any reference whatsoever to the county’s
acceptance of the roads contained therein until the roadways are completed and
the one year maintenance bond described in division (A)(1) above is actually on
file and in force in the office of Auditor of the county. Any plat submitted
for approval to the County Board of Commissioners which refers to approval or
acceptance of the roads contained therein shall be rejected unless the roads
are actually completed or a 100% construction bond and 20% maintenance bond as
called for in division (A)(1) above are on file.
   (E)   All letters of credit submitted by owners or developers to guarantee
the timely construction and completion of roadways contained in said plat shall
be irrevocable, in the form recommended by the County Board of Commissioners,
and shall have no expiration date fixed upon them.
   (F)   The County Board of Commissioners expressly reserve the right to elect
to take against any bond or irrevocable letter of credit posted by an owner or
developer for the construction, completion, or maintenance of any road set out
in any approved plat whether or not the owner or developer has reached a
completion date or review date for any approved project if, in the opinion of
the Board of Commissioners, such an election to take against said bond or
irrevocable letter of credit is necessary to assure timely completion of
roadways at the owner or developer’s expense and not at taxpayer’s expense.
   (G)   The County Board of Commissioners by and through the County Highway
Department will review each project submitted to it for approval no later than
60 days prior to a completion date or a project review date to determine
whether or not the County Board of Commissioners shall elect to take against
any bond or irrevocable letter of credit pending for said project.
(Ord. 73-1, passed 6-3-1973; Ord. passed 6-17-1974; Ord. 2006-07, passed 12-22-
2006)
§ 150.03 LEGAL DESCRIPTION OF EASEMENTS.
   The legal description for any and all easements for the right-of-way for any
and all roads, streets, and highways shall include all fill and/or cut slope. A
fill area of the five feet or less shall have a side slope of four to one (four
feet horizontal to one foot vertical). Cut slopes shall be no steeper than two
to one (two feet horizontal to one foot vertical).
(Prior Code, § 3-2-4)
§ 150.04 PETITIONER REQUIREMENTS.
   The person or persons so petitioning the county to accept such roadway into
the county highway system shall be solely responsible for ensuring that the
side slope and cut slope minimum requirements are met and shall so state under
oath in writing at the same time that the petition for acceptance of the
roadway is so presented to such Commissioners for consideration. The verified
statement shall further state that one right-of-way width shall be so that any
fill or cut slope shall be on the road easement.
(Prior Code, § 3-2-5)
**** CONSTRUCTION UNDER, ALONG, OVER HIGHWAYS ****
§ 150.15 REQUIREMENTS FOR CONSTRUCTION.
   (A)   Any corporation, utility, governmental agency, or any person or
persons planning to construct any pipeline, sewer line, cable, or any other
item of construction that will pass over, under, or along any county road or
highway, or right-of-way thereof, in the county must first obtain written
permission from said Board of Commissioners before said construction is
commenced. The said Board of Commissioners shall have the right to establish
conditions of said construction.
   (B)   Any corporation, utility, governmental agency, or any person or
persons planning to construct any pipeline, sewer line, cable, or any other
item of construction that will pass over, under, or along any county road or
highway, or right-of-way thereof, in the county must construct any of the above
items that are to pass under the surface of the road at least 24 inches below
the surface of said road.
   (C)   Any corporation, utility, governmental agency, or any person or
persons planning to construct any pipeline, sewer line, cable, or any other
item of construction that will pass over, under, or along any county road or
highway, or right-of-way thereof, must first file with the Board of
Commissioners a performance bond in an amount not less than 10% of the total
cost of the project or $500, which ever is the greater.
(Prior Code, § 8-22-1) (Ord. 1970 - ____, passed 3-20-1970)
§ 150.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Penalty for failure to so comply with §
150.04 shall be $1,000 upon conviction.
(Prior Code, § 3-2-5)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 151.01 STATUTORY AUTHORIZATION.
   The state legislature has in I.C. 36-1-4-11 granted the power to local
government units to control land use within their jurisdictions. Therefore, the
Board of County Commissioners does hereby adopt the following floodplain
management regulations set forth in this chapter.
(Ord. 2024-04, passed 7- -2024)
§ 151.02 FINDINGS OF FACT.
   The flood hazard areas of the county are subject to periodic inundation
which results in loss of life and property, health, and safety hazards,
disruption of commerce and governmental services, extraordinary public
expenditures for flood protection and relief, and impairment of the tax base,
all of which adversely affect the public health, safety, and general welfare.
Additionally, structures that are inadequately elevated, floodproofed, or
otherwise protected from flood damage also contribute to the flood loss. In
order to minimize the threat of such damages and to achieve the purposes
hereinafter set forth, these regulations are adopted.
(Ord. 2024-04, passed 7- -2024)
§ 151.03 STATEMENT OF PURPOSE.
   It is the purpose of this chapter to promote the public health, safety, and
general welfare and to minimize public and private losses due to flood
conditions in specific areas by provisions designed to:
   (A)   Protect human life and health;
   (B)   Minimize expenditure of public money for costly flood control
projects;
   (C)   Minimize the need for rescue and relief efforts associated with
flooding and generally undertaken at the expense of the general public;
   (D)   Minimize prolonged business interruptions;
   (E)   Minimize damage to public facilities and utilities such as water and
gas mains, electric, telephone, and sewer lines, streets, and bridges located
in floodplains;
   (F)   Help maintain a stable tax base by providing for the sound use and
development of flood-prone areas in such a manner as to minimize flood blight
area;
   (G)   Ensure that those who occupy the areas of special flood hazard assume
responsibility for their actions;
   (H)   Minimize the impact of development on adjacent properties within and
near flood-prone areas;
   (I)   Ensure that the flood storage and conveyance functions of the
floodplain are maintained;
   (J)   Minimize the impact of development on the natural, beneficial values
of the floodplain;
   (K)   Prevent floodplain uses that are either hazardous or environmentally
incompatible; and
   (L)   Meet community participation requirements of the National Flood
Insurance Program.
(Ord. 2024-04, passed 7- -2024)
§ 151.04 METHODS FOR REDUCING FLOOD LOSS.
   In order to accomplish its purposes, these regulations include methods and
provisions for:
   (A)   Restricting or prohibiting uses which are dangerous to health, safety,
and property due to water hazards, or which result in damaging increases in
flood heights or velocities;
   (B)   Requiring that uses vulnerable to floods, including facilities, which
serve such uses, be protected against flood damage at the time of initial
construction;
   (C)   Controlling the alteration of natural floodplains, stream channels,
and natural protective barriers, which help accommodate or channel flood
waters;
   (D)   Controlling filling, grading, dredging, excavating, and other
development which may increase flood damage; and
   (E)   Preventing or regulating the construction of flood barriers, which
will unnaturally divert floodwaters, or which may increase flood hazards in
other areas.
(Ord. 2024-04, passed 7- -2024)
§ 151.05 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ACCESSORY STRUCTURE. A structure with a floor area of 400 square feet or
less that is on the same parcel of property as a principal structure and the
use of which is incidental to the use of the principal structure; an accessory
structure specifically excludes structures used for human habitation.
      (1)   ACCESSORY STRUCTURES are considered walled and roofed where the
structure includes at least two outside rigid walls and a fully secured roof.
      (2)   Examples of ACCESSORY STRUCTURES include, but are not necessarily
limited to, two-car detached garages (or smaller), carports, storage and tool
sheds, and small boathouses.
      (3)   The following may have uses that are incidental or accessory to the
principal structure on a parcel, but are generally not considered to be
ACCESSORY STRUCTURES by the NFIP:
         (a)   Structures in which any portion is used for human habitation,
whether as a permanent residence or as temporary or seasonal living quarters,
such as a detached garage or carriage house that includes an apartment or guest
quarters, or a detached guest house on the same parcel as a principal
residence;
         (b)   Structures used by the public, such as a place of employment or
entertainment; and
         (c)   Development that does not meet the NFIP definition of a
structure for floodplain management purposes. Examples include, but are not
necessarily limited to, a gazebo, pavilion, picnic shelter, carport that is
open on all sides (roofed but not walled), or the like.
   ADDITION (TO AN EXISTING STRUCTURE). Any walled and roofed expansion to the
perimeter of a structure in which the addition is connected by a common load-
bearing wall other than a firewall. Any walled and roofed addition, which is
connected by a firewall or is separated by independent perimeter load-bearing
walls, is new construction.
   ALTERATION OF A WATERCOURSE. A dam, impoundment, channel relocation, change
in channel alignment, channelization, or change in cross-sectional area of the
channel or the channel capacity, or any other modification which may alter,
impede, retard, or change the direction and/or velocity of the flow of water
during conditions of the base flood.
   APPEAL. A request for a review of the Floodplain Administrator’s
interpretation of any provision of this chapter, a request for a variance, or a
challenge of a Board decision.
